>
> - Requirement source: raised by a real customer on 2026-08-26. Core requirements: ① one codebase executing in multiple regions; ② execution-environment management (create and initialize / test run / run / **deletion requires approval**); ③ tasks pushed down to multiple regions for execution, with
>   every execution resource (tasks / schedules / S3 Tables / DMS / Redshift / EMR Serverless / Glue) initialized and run per region; ④ naming-rule constraints for buckets and other resources; ⑤ metadata reported centrally to the control region (= the installation region, DynamoDB) — 🔴 **exception
>   since revision 3.0 (axis D / section 8.5.3)**: this requirement refers to **technical metadata** (definitions / status / structure / counts); **the personal-data dimension of audit** (operator identity, SQL text, plaintext queryId) **must stay in regional audit storage for cross-jurisdiction
>   targets**, with the center receiving only a non-PII summary; otherwise "central reporting" would itself constitute a cross-border transfer of personal data; ⑥ cost / metadata management per region at the first level; ⑦ tasks allowed to differ per region (default: one codebase, many regions); ⑧
>   **Ask-Your-Data executes against one region at a time, using that region's own large language model; parallel querying across regions is not allowed**; ⑨ evaluate whether parallel push-down to multiple regions is allowed. The customer's pain point = **management cost**: N regions require N
>   environments to be deployed (code / metadata / scheduling, one copy each), and the fear that an operation misses a region so that the data no longer matches.
>
> - Evidence base (all file:line references are pinned there and not repeated here): the research note on infrastructure / naming / metadata, the research note on the seven execution chains, and the research note on the consumption and AI side.
>
> - Related designs: [model configuration](./model-configuration.md) (the D8 region hook) and [project isolation](./project-isolation.md) (task-runs gains the dimension in the same batch); see section 15.
>
> - 🔴 Red line: this document is pure design; no code is written without an explicit instruction.

---

## 0. Requirement restatement and core terminology

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Control plane (control region)** | The hub in the installation region: the ECS platform process, CloudFront / WAF, Cognito, all `bgp-*` DynamoDB tables, the three OpenMetadata stacks. **Unique** platform-wide, never replicated |
| 🆕 **Execution target (ExecutionTarget / `targetId`)** | 🔴 **The authoritative identity introduced in revision 3.0**: the **immutable primary key** of one execution-environment instance (for example `tgt_usw2_a`); once created it can neither be changed nor reused. `partition` / `accountId` / `region` / `cellId` / `stage` / `jurisdiction` **are all demoted to its attributes** and no longer serve as the primary key — so the same region can hold two targets side by side (blue/green, primary/standby, old and new during a migration), and targets may span accounts and partitions (aws / aws-cn / aws-us-gov). **The run primary key, the run-lock identity, resource bindings, pushed artifacts and cost / monitoring attribution are all anchored to `targetId`** (replacing the earlier `envId=region`; section 20.1 item 1) |
| **Execution environment (Execution Env)** | One set of execution resources on one **target** (before revision 3.0 phrased as "one set in one region"). **`core` is mandatory = compute + storage + network + data lake** (VPC / subnets / security groups, data-lake bucket / scripts bucket / S3 Tables table bucket, Glue, Athena, EMR foundation); **optional components (all support supplemental deployment, section 4.6)**: `redshift` (data warehouse), `kafka` (MSK), `mwaa`, `dms`, `flink`, `agentcore` (the per-region Ask-Your-Data trio), `residency-api` (the cross-jurisdiction data plane, section 8.5; installed only when `jurisdiction≠home`). At most one execution environment per region (phase one) |
| **home environment** | The execution environment of the region where the control plane lives = all existing resources as they are today, registered automatically at installation, **cannot be deleted** |
| **Push-down** | Instantiating a resource definition (sync / job / workflow / schedule) for a target execution environment: generate that region's DAG / script artifacts, write them to that region's buckets, run them on that region's engines |

The current state in one sentence (infrastructure research note, section 1): the platform is built on the strong assumption "single account × single region × assembled once" — a single region point at `infra/bin/app.ts:28` (one env passed to all **21 stacks**, confirmed in the first review round), 112 `us-east-1` fallbacks in 97 files, 117 module-level singleton SDK clients, **64 env variables** in the ECS block at `platform-stack.ts:754-858`, of which about **41** are execution-environment facts (REDSHIFT_* / GLUE_* / MWAA_* / ATHENA_* / S3TABLES_* / DMS_* / FLINK_* / EMR_* and so on — the part this design folds into descriptors) and the remaining about 23 are control-plane / AI wiring (Cognito / GENBI_* / NETADVISOR_* / OPENMETADATA and so on, **not folded in**); region and resource names are **baked into the text of the DAG and Glue script artifacts**, platform-wide metadata has **no region dimension**, and asset FQNs do not contain the region.

---

## 1. Overall architecture: single control plane × multiple execution planes (D1)

### 1.1 Architecture decision

**Selected: single control plane × multiple execution planes.** The control plane stays a single copy (it was made explicit that metadata is reported centrally to the control region); each execution environment is a "pluggable compute + storage partition" of the control plane.

```
┌─ Control plane (home region, unique) ──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ CloudFront/WAF → ECS platform → Cognito                                     │
│ All bgp-* DynamoDB tables (including the new registry bgp-execution-envs)   │
│ OpenMetadata (governance plane) │ Copilot brain │ alerting/approvals/audit  │
└──────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┘
       │ push artifacts + invoke APIs   │ (executors write straight back to the control-plane DDB across regions)
┌──────▼──────────┐     ┌──────▼──────────┐     ┌──────▼──────────┐
│ Exec env use1    │     │ Exec env usw2    │     │ Exec env euw1    │
│ (=home, existing)│     │ buckets×3/S3Tables│    │   ……             │
│                  │     │ Redshift/Athena  │     │                  │
│                  │     │ MWAA/DMS/EMR/    │     │                  │
│                  │     │ Flink/(AgentCore)│     │                  │
└──────────────────┘     └──────────────────┘     └──────────────────┘
```

Key properties:
- **Zero migration of existing resources**: the home environment = the current resources registered under their original names (the descriptor stores the "actual resource name", not a rule-derived name; see section 2); all existing tasks are bound to home by default and behave byte-for-byte as before.
- **A single metadata point**: executors (MWAA DAGs / runtime-lib) keep writing directly to DynamoDB; only the client now explicitly names the control-plane region — a cross-region DynamoDB call needs IAM only, with no network prerequisite (argued in the infrastructure research note, section 5; latency is tens of milliseconds, acceptable for a run lock with a seconds-level lease).
- **Data never crosses regions**: the data of each execution environment (lake / warehouse / streams) stays in its own region; the platform performs no cross-region data replication (that is the business tasks' job).

### 1.2 Comparison of alternatives (one half of the necessity assessment; see section 13)

| Option | Description | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| A. Deploy a complete, independent platform per region (feasible today) | N runs of deploy-all, N entry points / Cognito pools / metadata databases | ❌ This is exactly the pain point the customer complains about: management cost × N, no unified view, code / definition drift impossible to detect |
| **B. Single control plane × multiple execution planes (selected)** | This design | ✅ Cures the pain point at the root; the price = a platform-level rework that must be phased (section 17) |
| C. Compromise: keep the architecture, build a "consistency inspection tool for multiple deployments" | A cross-environment reconciler + deployment automation scripts | ⚠️ Small engineering effort, but the entry point / metadata / scheduling remain N copies; it only relieves "afraid of missing a region" and does not solve the management cost. Can be offered as a transitional selling point before B lands; not a project of its own |

---

## 2. Execution-environment model and registry (D2)

### 2.1 New table `bgp-execution-envs` (control-plane DynamoDB)

The core step of going multi-region: fold the ~40 flat env variables at platform-stack.ts:754-858 **into per-region descriptors**, stored in DynamoDB and manageable from the web UI.

| Attribute | Description |
|---|---|
| `pk` | The constant `"env"` |
| `sk` | 🔴 **Changed in revision 3.0 to `targetId` (the immutable identity, for example `tgt_usw2_a`) — region is no longer the primary key** (confirmed by the independent architecture review: a region string contains neither account nor partition and cannot express blue/green, primary/standby or old-and-new-during-migration coexistence in one region, nor multiple accounts or partitions). Once created it can neither be changed nor reused |
| 🆕 `partition` / `accountId` / `region` / `cellId?` / `stage?` (all `S`) | **Location and deployment attributes (demoted to attributes)**: `partition` (aws / aws-cn / aws-us-gov), `accountId`, `region`, `cellId?` (multiple cells / blue-green within one region), `stage?` (**orthogonal** to the dev / staging / prod axis of the environment-promotion design). "Which targets does region X have" goes through a GSI query, not through the primary key |
| 🆕 `desiredVersion` / `observedVersion` (both `S`) | **Desired / observed separation** of the component template version (paired with the commit pinning + upgrade waves of section 12.6; the registry stores desired, DescribeStacks / probing yields observed, continuously reconciled; section 20.5) |
| `displayName` / `isHome` | The home environment has `isHome=true` and cannot be deleted |
| `jurisdiction` | Jurisdiction tag (default = the home jurisdiction); `≠home` triggers data-residency mode (section 8.5: install `residency-api`, the central data entry points fail closed on it [🔴 void since revision 3.20: no refusal, only regional audit + a central summary, decision 6quinquies], results / memory / SQL history stay in the region, the model accepts the AI-path gap with `global.*` in phase one). This is the decision basis for the unified front middleware of section 8.5.5 ① and the endpoint origin routing of section 8.5.7 |
| `status` | `registered → initializing → testing → active → decommissioning → deleted` (lifecycle, section 4) |
| `resources` | **The bag of actual resource names** (not derived): core always has `{datalakeBucket, scriptsBucket, tableBucket, athenaWorkgroup, emrSubnetIds[], emrSecurityGroupId, flinkSubnetIds[]?, glueRoleArn}`; **optional-component fields exist only when the component exists**: `redshiftWorkgroup?, redshiftDatabase?, redshiftSecretArn?` (only with redshift installed), `dagBucket?, mwaaEnvName?` (with mwaa), `dmsSubnetGroup?` (with dms), `flinkRoleArn?` (with flink) — ⚠️ the three redshift* fields are **marked optional** (consistent with mwaa? / dms?); consumers must not assume a value at the type level (empty in a no-redshift region, degraded per section 8.1). **Most** correspond one-to-one with ECS env variables. ⚠️ First-review check: 3 items are **not env variables but discovered at runtime by fixed name**; folding them into the descriptor is justified, but the "one-to-one" wording is inaccurate — `redshiftSecretArn` (`admin-secret.ts:11-25` calls DescribeSecret with the fixed name `redshift!bgp-namespace-admin`, and `_arn` is a **process-level single cached value**; multi-region requires a per-env cache), `emrSecurityGroupId` (`platform-env.ts:27-46` discovers it by the name `bgp-emr-sg`), `dmsSubnetGroup` (not present in the ECS env block) |
| `components` | The set of installed components (component boundaries calibrated on 2026-08-27): **`core` is mandatory = compute + storage + network + data lake** (VPC / subnets / security groups, S3 data-lake bucket / scripts bucket / table bucket, S3 Tables, Glue, Athena, EMR foundation) — any region that comes up can run batch and lake analytics; **optional and all supporting later supplemental deployment (section 4.6)**: `redshift` (data warehouse), `kafka` (MSK), `mwaa` (scheduling), `dms` (CDC real-time sync), `flink` (stream computing), `agentcore` (the per-region Ask-Your-Data trio), **`residency-api` (the cross-jurisdiction data plane, section 8.5.2; installed only in regions with `jurisdiction≠home`; hosts the regional data API + regional result / memory storage)**. ⚠️ Note: Redshift **moves from the original draft's "mandatory core" to optional** — build core first, add redshift / kafka later on demand, with no rebuild (section 4.6) |
| `agentcore?` | Wiring of the per-region Ask-Your-Data trio: `{genbiRuntimeArn, genbiMemoryId, gatewayUrl}` (section 8.2) |
| `probe` | The most recent health probe: `{at, ok, items:[{name, ok, error?}]}` |
| `createdBy/updatedAt/version` | Audit + optimistic locking |

- **Automatic registration of the home target (🔴 revision 3.0 removes an ambiguity: earlier text wrote `sk=<home-region>` and "sk=targetId", which collided)**: the existing env values are written as the item with **`sk = "tgt_home"`** (**a fixed constant, not the region literal**; `region` is stored as its attribute holding the actual home region); `isHome(target)` tests `targetId === "tgt_home"`. **The execution moment is fixed = the end of the deployment script** (not "at platform start-up" — to avoid concurrent writes from multiple instances and a first-request race), using **a conditional write `attribute_not_exists(sk)` for idempotency**; 🔴 **correction in revision 3.5 (the third external audit's fallback-to-env finding)**: the original text "at runtime the descriptor is read first and **falls back to env by default** — rollback = delete the item and the current state returns" **directly conflicts with the fail-closed rule of section 2.2 and of [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.1**; building it as written would re-introduce the admission-read and residency fail-open findings. **Settled**: ① the platform's read path is **read-only**; a missing or incomplete home descriptor ⇒ **fail closed and point to the seed entry point**, **no fallback to env, no self-healing write to the database**; ② "fall back to env" holds only on **not-yet-wired** existing code paths (those paths do not read the descriptor at all) and is **not** a semantic of the descriptor layer; ③ the rollback semantic becomes: **delete the item ⇒ the new paths that depend on the descriptor fail closed** (rather than "return to the current state"); real rollback relies on **not wiring** (the code-review round left zero callers).
- The descriptor is the **runtime SSOT (single source of truth)**: the client factory of section 6, artifact generation, and the 4 atomic functions of section 8 all consume it.
- 🔴 **Atomicity of descriptor writes + behavior on reading an incomplete descriptor (a review item: prevent hitting the wrong resources)**: the descriptor is written back **field by field** from CodeBuild outputs / the admin-secret ARN and so on (section 4.1), so a "partial write" window exists (for example redshiftWorkgroup written, redshiftSecretArn not yet). A **half-written item equally fails the whole-package validation of section 3.1a** (🔴 **correction in revision 3.7**: the original text "descriptor first, fall back to env only when it is missing as a whole" is the retired fallback semantic — the descriptor layer is now **read-only and fail-closed**, and a half-written item is rejected by the whole-package validation rather than "taken as a half copy"). Requirements: ① the registry write is **a whole-package write, or a `ready` bit is set after writing completes**, and the runtime accepts only descriptors with `ready=true`; ② when the runtime reads an **incomplete / non-ready** descriptor, it **falls back to env as a whole or refuses the push-down (with an explicit error), never field by field** (field-by-field fallback = partly hitting the descriptor and partly hitting env, the easiest way to hit the wrong resources).

### 2.2 ExecutionEnv runtime type (platform side)

```
ExecutionTarget = { targetId, partition, accountId, region, cellId?, stage?, jurisdiction,
                    resources{…}, components[], capabilities[], agentcore?, ready,
                    desiredVersion, observedVersion }
lib/exec-env/registry.ts (new): getTarget(targetId) 🔴 **correction in revision 3.5: admission verdicts are not cached + every read uses ConsistentRead:true** (the original text said "60 s TTL cache, following the caching convention of the model-configuration design" — building it as written would **re-introduce the admission-read finding as is**: the mutable admission bits status/ready/lockEpoch/credentialEpoch would be cached ⇒ after the management plane switches a target to draining, new runs would still be admitted for 60 s. The authority is the specification, section 3.1)
                              listActiveTargets() / getHomeTarget() / listTargetsByRegion(region)
// 🔴 revision 3.0: the input is always targetId (no longer envId=region); region is only a filter / display attribute (section 20.1 item 1)
```

Every question of "which **target** should this hit" goes through this single entry point; adding scattered `process.env.X` reads is forbidden.

🔴 **A non-home target whose descriptor is missing / not `ready` / stale ⇒ fail closed and refuse (with an explicit error), never fall back to home** (an item of the independent architecture review; falling back to home would silently send region A's tasks to the home data plane = a cross-region mis-write and possibly a cross-jurisdiction transfer). The "fall back to env as a whole or refuse" at the end of section 2.1 holds only for the **home target** (🔴 **clarification in revision 3.7**: it refers to **not-yet-wired existing code paths**, not to the descriptor layer; at the descriptor layer a missing home descriptor fails closed just the same).

---

## 3. Naming SSOT (D3; "constraint design of the naming rules" was explicitly requested)

### 3.1 Current pain points (infrastructure research note, section 3)

There is no naming SSOT: CDK literals and platform env fallbacks are scattered across two layers; the `bgp-workgroup` fallback is duplicated 🔴 **re-measured and corrected in revision 3.8: `bgp-workgroup` hits 67 lines / 39 files in total, of which the `||` / `??` fallback form accounts for 30 lines / 23 files; counting every `bgp-*` fallback gives 73 lines / 51 files** (measured on 2026-08-31 excluding `cdk.out`; the "46 lines / 28 files" written in revision 3.1 matches neither count and is retired); the S3 bucket name `bgp-datalake-<acct>` contains the account **but not the region** (a second region creating the same bucket name is guaranteed to collide, because S3 bucket names are globally unique); IAM roles have fixed names (global resources; a second-region stack creating them again is guaranteed to hit AlreadyExists).

### 3.2 Rule design

Copy the platform's only SSOT precedent, the two-sided re-export pattern of `ai-model.json` (platform/ai-model.json → infra/lib/shared/model.ts + platform/src/lib/ai/model.ts):

- A new **`platform/naming.json`** (the rule table: prefixes and the name template of each resource) + `infra/lib/shared/naming.ts` + `platform/src/lib/aws/naming.ts` re-exported on both sides, with the same function on both sides, **`resourceName(kind, {targetId, account?, component?})`**.
- 🔴 **Settled in revision 3.8 (three colliding contract passages converged while landing the naming SSOT)**:
  1. **The derived key is `targetSlug`, not `region` and not `region + cellId`**. Basis: **[the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.5, Q-2** (the original revision 3.0 decision text: "FQN segments / OpenMetadata service names / audit shard keys / model-configuration model keys / **the naming SSOT** are all derived from `targetId` (not region)") + the acceptance criterion asserting that `bgp-glue-scripts-<account>-<slug>` is a legal bucket name.
     - Deriving by region **cannot work**: [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.5, Q-2, explicitly allows **multiple targets in one region** (blue/green, primary/standby, old and new during a migration); the bucket names / workgroup names / MWAA names of two targets in the same region would all collide.
     - "`region`, appending `-<cellId>` only for **targets that are not the first in that region**" **cannot work either**: "first" is **registration-order state** that a pure naming function cannot see; it would have to query the registry to know whether it is the first ⇒ the name could no longer be reproduced independently from the descriptor, conflicting with the auditability of the "bag of actual names (not derived)" in section 2.1.
     - "`region` + `slug` as two segments" **overflows the length limit**: `bgp-glue-scripts-` (17) + account (12) + `-` + the longest region (14) + `-` + the longest slug (20) = **65 > the S3 limit of 63** (the arithmetic is pinned in `naming.test.ts`). Taking the slug segment only = 50, safe.
  2. **The signature does not accept `isHome`**: `isHome` is always derived from `targetId` by the single authoritative function of section 2.1. Accepting `isHome` would be **a forgeable bypass** (a caller passing `isHome:true` could let a non-home target obtain the home's existing resource names = cross-target resource mix-up), consistent with how the registry pins the home invariant on the descriptor in both directions.
  3. **`region` remains in the input but no kind consumes it today** (no kind lists it in its `vars` declaration); the function accepts only the variables that the kind **declares**, and throws if one is missing — so that no "passed but unused" dead parameter is left behind.
- **Fail-closed**: an unregistered kind / a missing variable / a `{` still left after interpolation / an over-long name all throw; **a guessed name is never returned**.
- **Naming rules**:

| Resource class | Rule | Example |
|---|---|---|
| S3 buckets (global namespace) | 🔴 **Revision 3.8**: `bgp-<purpose>-<acct>-<targetSlug>`; **home is exempt** and keeps its existing legacy name (without the target segment) | `bgp-datalake-<acct>-tgt-usw2-a`; home stays `bgp-datalake-<acct>` |
| Region-level resources (workgroup / Athena WG / table bucket / MWAA / DMS / SG) | 🔴 **Corrected in revision 3.8**: **home keeps its existing fixed names; non-home targets carry `-<targetSlug>`**. Both reasons of the original "same name in every region" no longer hold: ① a region-level namespace isolates only **across regions**, while [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.5, Q-2, allows **multiple targets in one region** ⇒ guaranteed collision within a region; ② the convenience "the same name across regions helps one codebase" has been superseded by section 2.1 — the runtime always reads the **actual name** from the descriptor and never assembles names from the region, so identical names bring no benefit any more | home `bgp-workgroup`; non-home `bgp-workgroup-tgt-usw2-a` |
| IAM roles (global) | **The second-region construction batch still follows this**: no target segment, one set shared account-wide, the IAM stack deployed once in home only, execution-environment stacks referencing the existing roles (`Role.fromRoleName`), and the roles' trust / permissions extended per multi-region (section 12). 🔴 **Added in revision 3.8**: the naming SSOT also registers the per-target form `BgpExecEnv-<slug>-<role-short-name>` (for example `-glue-role` / `-emr-role` / `-redshift-copy-role`, listed one by one in `naming.json`) for use by [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.1, "switch to per-target execution roles before a cross-jurisdiction target goes live" — **callers arrive only with the data-plane construction batch; the second-region batch does not use it** | home `bgp-glue-role`; per-target (data-plane batch) `BgpExecEnv-tgt-eu1-glue-role` |
| DynamoDB tables | Control plane only (a single copy in home), names unchanged; other regions have their executors **read and write the control-plane tables across regions** (section 14bis.2). 🔴 **Boundary made explicit in revision 3.8**: DynamoDB table names **do not go into `naming.json`** — the true source is already `TABLES` in `platform/src/lib/aws/dynamodb.ts` (56 tables); adding them here would create a second definition. `naming.json` lists this class explicitly under `notCovered` | `bgp-task-runs` (one table platform-wide) |

- **How the home exemption is implemented**: the naming function is used only to generate names when **creating** an execution environment; at runtime the **actual names** in the registry descriptor are always read (section 2.1) — so existing bucket names are not migrated, and old and new rules coexist unnoticed.
- While landing, fold the 🔴 **30 lines / 23 files** (the revision 3.8 measured count, see section 3.1) of `bgp-workgroup` fallback literals into the naming SSOT (**lossless refactoring content of the naming batch, not of the naming-SSOT-body batch: that batch delivers only the SSOT itself and its gate, with zero call-site changes**); fix the pitfalls already surveyed in the infrastructure research note, section 3: the DAG-bucket fallbacks at `mwaa-offline.ts:22-23` / `publish-dag.ts:59` lack the account suffix.

---

## 4. Execution-environment lifecycle (D4; the four operations were explicitly requested)

### 4.1 Create and initialize (🔴 fully automatic deployment by the platform, with deployment privileges isolated in CodeBuild)

> - **Design tone (corrected on 2026-08-27, core principle)**: "CDK must have deployment permission. The whole point of this platform is to save manual operations, so environment deployment must be the platform's responsibility; a human only controls which region to deploy to and which services to
>   deploy. If people have to run the deployment by hand, the platform is pointless."
> - **The original draft's "semi-automatic: generate commands for operators to type by hand" was the wrong direction** (it sacrificed the platform's automation value to guard "least privilege") — it is abandoned in favor of the **one-click platform deployment** below.

**Key point: one-click deployment and a minimal standing attack surface do not contradict each other; a CodeBuild-isolated deployment role satisfies both (note: this is "minimal standing attack surface", not "least privilege on the deployment path" — the honest positioning is in section 12.0):**

| Role | Permissions | Who can use it |
|---|---|---|
| Platform ECS TaskRole | 🔴 **Changed in revision 3.0**: **only `lambda:InvokeFunction` (to the exec-env deployment broker)** + region-side `cloudformation:Describe*` (progress read-back) + reading build logs. **No longer holds `codebuild:StartBuild`** — StartBuild carries overrides such as `buildspecOverride` (which may be inline), so holding it is enough to borrow CodeBuild's powerful role for an arbitrary deployment (an item of the independent architecture review; end of section 12.0); StartBuild is instead **held exclusively by the broker**, which forwards no overrides | The platform process |
| **CodeBuild service role** (the deployment role) | CloudFormation / IAM PassRole / creating buckets · Redshift · MWAA · MSK · VPC … and other powerful permissions | **Only CodeBuild running our predefined buildspec**; the platform cannot use it for anything else |

Hence: the platform clicks once → StartBuild → CodeBuild runs `cdk deploy` with the isolated, powerful role → the platform polls the build status / logs and shows progress. **The platform is responsible throughout; nobody types a command; even if the platform ECS were compromised, all the attacker would gain is StartBuild (which can only trigger the predefined buildspec and cannot create arbitrary resources) — that is the "minimal standing attack surface".** ⚠️ Honest boundary: this **does not** mean "no powerful privilege exists in the account" — the deployment power still resides in the CodeBuild service role / the bootstrap cfn-exec role, indirectly held by whoever can StartBuild or change the buildspec (fully argued in section 12.0).

**Decision table for the deployment mechanism (added after an architecture-review remark: the original draft only asserted "the standard paradigm" without comparing alternatives):**

| Mechanism | Trigger | Changeset approval / multi-account | Fits this platform's needs | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CodeBuild (selected)** | The platform process **actively calls `StartBuild`**; progress flows back to the platform page | Must be added by hand (section 12.2 deploy lock / section 12.6 changeset gate) | ✅ Fits the interaction need "one click on the platform + progress back on the web page" (the core requirement: the platform is responsible and the page shows it) | ✅ Selected |
| CDK Pipelines | **Triggered by git push**, self-mutating | Native changeset gate, multi-region / multi-account | ⚠️ A git-push trigger **does not fit** "click once on the platform page and it deploys" (the platform cannot actively trigger a pipeline and feed its progress back into its own administration page) | No (trigger model mismatch) |
| Terraform Cloud / Service Catalog | External workspace / product catalog | Each has its own mechanism | ❌ Introduces a second IaC toolchain (everything is CDK today) or an extra product layer, violating "a big ship does not turn — introduce no new stack"; Service Catalog's productization granularity is coarse | No |

> Honest note: the changeset gate / multi-account support of CDK Pipelines are native capabilities; this design approximates them by hand in sections 12.2 / 12.6. **The decisive reason for choosing CodeBuild is the trigger model** — what this platform needs is "the administrator clicks 'Start deployment' on the page → the platform actively calls StartBuild → progress returns in real time to the same page", which CodeBuild (active API trigger + BatchGetBuilds read-back) fits and CDK Pipelines (git-push driven) does not. ⚠️ **If the changeset-gate / multi-account requirements grow in phase two, re-evaluate Pipelines** (migrating the buildspec's self-heal logic will then have a cost, section 12.5). This is one of the standard paradigms for "self-service infrastructure provisioning" on AWS.

**Creation flow (fully automatic):**
1. The administration page's "New execution environment" wizard: choose the region + **set the `jurisdiction` tag (default = the home jurisdiction; choosing a foreign jurisdiction triggers data-residency mode, section 8.5)** + tick components (core is mandatory; redshift / kafka / mwaa / dms / flink / agentcore / **`residency-api` (mandatory for a cross-jurisdiction region)** are optional, and **all support later supplemental deployment**, see section 4.6) → write the registry item (`status=registered`).
2. The platform **automatically** calls `StartBuild` (passing `-c region=<region> -c components=core,redshift,…`) and sets `status=initializing`; inside CodeBuild: `cdk bootstrap` the region → deploy the execution-environment stack group `BgpExecEnvStackGroup` (assembled conditionally from the ticked components) → **write the actual resource list from the CDK outputs back into the registry item** (`aws dynamodb update-item` at the end of the buildspec, or the platform reads the exported outputs from `BatchGetBuilds` and fills them in).
3. During deployment the page shows **real-time progress** (polling the phase from `BatchGetBuilds` + the CloudWatch build log stream, reusing the probing / progress conventions of section 6.1): bootstrap → CREATE of each stack → write-back. On failure `status=initializing` is kept + the failed stack and a log summary are shown; the operation can be **retried** (idempotent, see section 4.6).
4. Deployment succeeded → the platform probes each resource according to the descriptor (HeadBucket / GetWorkgroup / …) → all green sets `testing`.
   - ⚠️ **Per-region Redshift admin secret**: the admin secret `redshift!bgp-namespace-admin` (`admin-secret.ts:15`) is the managed secret (region-level) generated automatically when Redshift Serverless creates the namespace. Only when the `redshift` component is ticked does the region have this secret; after CodeBuild deploys the redshift stack, a CDK output writes the actual ARN back into the registry as `resources.redshiftSecretArn`. Code side: the process-level single cached value `_arn` in `admin-secret.ts` must become a per-env Map (otherwise the home ARN pollutes region B; for an env without redshift the value is empty and consumers must tolerate that).

**IAM impact (section 12 already updated)**: the platform gains `codebuild:StartBuild/BatchGetBuilds`, restricted by parameters to the exec-env-specific CodeBuild project ARN; updating the `aws:RequestedRegion` condition still requires one redeployment of the control-plane stacks (which CodeBuild may do as well), see section 12.1.

#### 4.1a 🔴 The exec-env stack group must be a "self-contained stack group" (the self-contained-stack-group finding; first review round: the first gate on whether deployment is possible at all, severely underestimated in the original draft)

The original draft's "deploy the execution-environment stack group `BgpExecEnvStackGroup`" glossed over it, but the first review round revealed that **the existing stacks cannot be reused across regions at all** — they consume **live CDK construct references** from the control plane, not names / ARNs:

- `mwaa-stack.ts:9-12` consumes `vpc: ec2.Vpc` + `albSecurityGroup: ec2.ISecurityGroup` + `dataKey: kms.IKey`, and `:31` adds an ingress rule to **the platform ALB security group**; `redshift-stack.ts:9-10` / `emr-stack.ts:17-18` / `realtime-stack.ts:20-21` / `flink-stack.ts:16` all consume `vpc` / `dataKey` (🔵 measured by grep: `dataKey` is consumed by 14 stacks).
- **Region B has no control-plane stacks** → no VPC, no data-domain CMK (**a KMS key is region-level and cannot be shared across regions**), none of the platform-built dagBucket / ALB security group.

**Therefore the exec-env stack group must be refactored to be self-contained (supplementary design for the self-contained-stack-group finding)**:
1. **Create an independent stack group `infra/lib/exec-env/*`**, stack names 🔴 **corrected in revision 3.9: `BgpExecEnv-<targetSlug>-<component>`** (the original "region prefix `BgpExecEnv-usw2-*`" is the old formulation from before revision 3.0 introduced `targetId` — naming by region prefix means **two targets in one region (blue/green, primary/standby) collide on stack names**; the authoritative form is in [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.1a ①), with a namespace **disjoint** from the 21 control-plane stacks → `cdk deploy 'BgpExecEnv-<targetSlug>-*'` can be targeted precisely and never touches the control plane by mistake. **Landed** (second-region batch, slice a): two stacks, `base` (foundation) + `core`; `base` is **a foundation, not a component** (it does not enter the descriptor's `components`, but every target has one and every component stack depends on it).
2. **The stack group builds its own** VPC / its own KMS key / its own DAG bucket and security groups, **injecting no control-plane construct** (the existing stacks' `props.vpc/dataKey/albSecurityGroup` dependencies are changed, in the exec-env version, to creation inside the stack group).
3. **Cross-region network for status write-back from MWAA (region B) → the control plane (home)**: today `mwaa-stack.ts:30-37` relies on security groups referencing each other within the same VPC, which does not work across regions → a cross-region reachability solution is required (writing directly to the control-plane DynamoDB goes through IAM with no network prerequisite, settled in section 6.3; but if an ALB callback is needed it requires public network + authentication or a cross-region private network) — **designed as its own item, not left blank**.
4. **Parameterize the CDK app**: app.ts is a flat single-env layout today (no Stage / StackGroup) → 🔴 **input parameters finalized in revision 3.9**: `-c exec-env-target=<targetId>` (**identity, mandatory**) + `-c exec-env-region=` (mandatory; **"use the default region" is not accepted**) + `-c exec-env-account=` (default `CDK_DEFAULT_ACCOUNT`) + `-c exec-env-components=` (default `core`) + `-c exec-env-nat-gateways=` (default 2) + `-c exec-env-azs=` (default `<region>a,<region>b`). When `exec-env-target` is passed ⇒ **only** that target's stack group is instantiated, none of the 21 control-plane stacks is built (and the SSM wiring self-heal does not run either); when it is not passed ⇒ the original path, **behavior unchanged by a single byte** (`readExecEnvContext` returns `null`). **Landed**: the control-plane body is wrapped into `synthControlPlane()`, the function body unchanged except for indentation, and the 17 templates are byte-for-byte identical (confirmed by a diff against the pre-refactoring baseline).
5. **Compile-level acceptance first**: first a `cdk synth` acceptance that "the stack group synthesizes with the correct region and zero references to control-plane constructs", only then real deployment. **Landed** as `infra/scripts/exec-env-synth-gate.mjs` (`npm run synth-gate`): stack-name prefix / zero intersection with the control plane / environment is the target region / zero control-plane construct references (including `Fn::ImportValue` allowed only within the group) / **all data resources `Retain` with no `AutoDeleteObjects`** / **every physical resource name is a product of the naming SSOT** (🔴 asserting only the CloudFormation Output is not enough — mutation test: with a hard-coded `bucketName` while the Output still wrote the SSOT variable, the gate stayed all green, and that is exactly "the descriptor points to a bucket that does not exist") / unimplemented components must raise an error. **Turns red for each of 19 mutations, verified one by one**.
6. 🔴 **New hard constraint in revision 3.9: the stack group's `cdk synth` must run with [zero credentials] and `manifest.missing` must be empty**. Measurement showed that `ec2.Vpc({ maxAzs })` on a stack with a concrete account + region triggers CDK's `availability-zones` context provider, **which assumes a role in the target account to look up the AZs** — both consequences are unacceptable: ① the CI-gate-level synth required by item 5 cannot run at all; ② the measured error output revealed **the local machine's AWS account id** (synthesizing another account's stack touched local credentials). Passing `availabilityZones` **does not help either** (CDK validates it as a subset of `stack.availabilityZones`, and reading that getter triggers the provider). ⇒ Settled: **override `Stack.availabilityZones`** (`lib/exec-env/exec-env-stack.ts`). ⚠️ Companion measure (must be done together, otherwise the risk only moves from synth to CloudFormation): AZ availability is **account-level**, so **a preflight `ec2 describe-availability-zones` is required before deployment**; if an AZ is unavailable, raise an error and suggest `-c exec-env-azs=` to switch — 🔴 **landed in revision 3.10** (`infra/buildspec/exec-env-deploy.yml`, step 1; the synth gate asserts that it may not be removed).
7. 🔴 **Data-protection rule landed in revision 3.9**: the stack group's data-lake bucket / scripts bucket / S3 Tables table bucket / CMK are **always `RETAIN`** and **must not** carry `autoDeleteObjects` (the home side is exactly the opposite, `DESTROY + autoDelete`; see the data-protection finding in section 4.4). The synth gate asserts per resource that both `DeletionPolicy` and `UpdateReplacePolicy` are `Retain`, and scans the whole template for the `AutoDeleteObjects` keyword.
8. **NAT defaults to 2** (one per AZ), matching the availability trade-off of the control-plane `VpcStack` — **a new region does not quietly get one notch less availability**; cost-sensitive verification may pass `-c exec-env-nat-gateways=1` explicitly. ⚠️ The control plane's "import an existing VPC" escape hatch (the default regional VPC quota is 5) **is deliberately not built in this stack** (`Vpc.fromLookup` needs synth-time credentials, conflicting with item 6); if a real deployment hits `VpcLimitExceeded`, it is added then, assigned to **slice g of the second-region batch**.

> Honest effort estimate: this is **refactoring 5+ stacks from "control-plane construct injection" into a "self-contained stack group"**, not adding a flag. It is the largest single piece of work in the second-region minimal-loop construction batch.

### 4.2 Test run

With `status=testing`, the page offers two levels of testing:
- **Probe suite** (seconds): probe each resource + a Bedrock converse probe (model availability in that region, reusing the model-availability probe of [the model-configuration design](./model-configuration.md)) + **a preflight probe of service availability / quotas in the target region** (a review item: whether the region offers the chosen services such as MWAA, and whether the account's service quotas have headroom such as Redshift RPU / VPC / EIP; if not, the wizard greys out the component and offers a "request a quota increase / choose another region" exit);
- **Canary task** (minutes, optional): a built-in minimal task chain really runs, **conditioned on the env's installed `components`** (Redshift is not hard-coded):
  - **By default the data-lake path (included in core, runs in any region)**: create temporary S3 data → Glue sync into that region's **S3 Tables** → **verify by querying with Athena** → clean up.
  - **Only when the env has the `redshift` component** is a Redshift branch appended (Glue sync into that region's Redshift → verify by querying Redshift).
  - The result lands in the probe field. **A core-only region verifies the closed loop with the data-lake path alone, without depending on Redshift.**

All green → the administrator clicks "Enable" → `active`, and the environment starts accepting push-downs.

### 4.3 Run (resource monitoring)

- The environment detail page aggregates that region: health of each component (probing), number of running tasks (task-runs filtered by region, section 7), key CloudWatch metrics (a per-region CloudWatch client; consumption-side research note, section 6), monthly cost (the FinOps region dimension, section 8.4).

### 4.4 Delete (🔴 requires approval; explicitly requested)

1. Deletion is initiated (admin/manager) → **preflight validation**: the number of resource definitions bound to this env = 0, enabled schedules = 0, running runs = 0; otherwise 400 with the details;
2. An approval ticket is created: `bgp-approvals` gains `type="exec-env-delete"` (reusing the approval workflow); **the approver = the platform admin** (default; configurable).
3. Approved → `decommissioning` + the platform **triggers destruction via `StartBuild`** (CodeBuild runs `cdk destroy` on that region's stack group, with the same isolated role as deployment); progress is visible as in section 4.1.
   - 🔴 **Data protection (the data-protection finding: a factual error confirmed by the first review round; the code must be fixed before work starts)**: today the data-lake bucket / scripts bucket / DAG bucket at `platform-stack.ts:51-53` are all `RemovalPolicy.DESTROY + autoDeleteObjects:true` (**not** RETAIN as the original draft claimed) — `cdk destroy` would **empty and delete the data-lake bucket = delete customer data**. The data buckets of the exec-env stack group **must explicitly switch to `RemovalPolicy.RETAIN` and drop `autoDeleteObjects`** (following the existing RETAIN convention of the kms / database / audit stacks); destruction removes only rebuildable resources such as compute / network; the page states explicitly "data buckets are retained and must be cleaned up separately after manual confirmation".
   - 🔴 **The facts about the S3 Tables table bucket have changed (corrected in revision 3.0; the original "a runtime orphan outside CDK management" is outdated)**: `platform-stack.ts:58-61` **already creates the table bucket with `s3tables.CfnTableBucket` in CDK, with `applyRemovalPolicy(DESTROY)`** ⇒ **`cdk destroy` deletes the table bucket = deletes customer lake data** (**more dangerous** than the original description: it was assumed "CDK does not manage it, so it will not delete it"; in fact "CDK manages it and is set to delete"). Remediation: the exec-env stack group's table bucket **must be explicitly RETAIN** (in the same batch as the data-lake / scripts / DAG buckets); in addition, a table bucket created **at runtime** by `start/route.ts:41 CreateTableBucketCommand` is an unmanaged resource, and the destruction flow **lists it separately with a prompt for manual confirmation**. The premise of the acceptance criterion on destroying an environment is corrected accordingly.
   - 🔴 **The regional result storage of residency-api must be protected equally (a review item mirroring the privileged-credentials finding; missed in the original)**: the `residency-api` component of a cross-jurisdiction region carries **regional result storage** (SQL history / Ask-Your-Data memory · turns / exports in regional DynamoDB + S3, the regional `MEMORY_BUCKET`, sections 8.5.2 / 8.5.7 ④) — this is **the only copy of compliance-sensitive cross-jurisdiction data** (the center keeps only a metadata index). `cdk destroy` of the regional stack group would destroy it along the way; keeping the `DESTROY+autoDelete` convention of `platform-stack.ts:51-53` = **permanent loss of compliance data**. Therefore: ① these regional DynamoDB tables / S3 buckets **must be RETAIN** (in the same batch as the data buckets); ② the **deletion preflight** (bindings=0 in section 4.4 step 1) **naturally does not cover** them (an environment can be "drained and deletable" yet still hold history / memory) → before deletion, the disposition of the regional result storage must be **explicitly exported or confirmed a second time**; ③ the deletion acceptance of the data-plane construction batch gains the item "regional result storage RETAIN / exported".
4. Destruction complete → the platform probes that the resources no longer exist → `deleted` (the item is kept as a tombstone for audit).
- The home environment can never be deleted (hidden in the UI + hard-refused by the API).

### 4.5 Honest cost accounting (the motive for componentization)

The **fixed cost** of each execution environment: MWAA mw1.small ≈ $350/month + NAT ≈ $65/month (if a new VPC is created) + OpenMetadata ingestion scan fees; Redshift Serverless / EMR / Glue / Flink are billed by usage. **Hence components are optional**: a region that needs no scheduling can skip mwaa (that env then supports only manual / API triggers, and pushing down a schedule validates component presence first); the wizard shows the estimated fixed cost. Build the minimal core first, add the expensive components as the business grows (section 4.6), and avoid paying up front for capabilities that are never used.

### 4.6 Supplemental deployment (🔴 explicitly requested: add components any time after core, without rebuilding)

> Stated on 2026-08-27: "If the data warehouse is not deployed, it must be possible to add it later; supplemental deployment must be supported — and kafka must support supplemental deployment too."

**This is a necessary consequence of componentization and a natural capability of CDK incremental deployment**:

- **Entry point**: the "Components" area of the execution-environment detail page; installed components show ✓, uninstalled components show a "+ Supplemental deployment" button (admin/manager only).
- **Action**: click to add → impact preview (which resources will be created + the estimated cost increment; mechanism B) → confirm → the platform calls `StartBuild` passing `-c region=<r> -c components=<existing>+<new>` (**pass the full set; CDK is idempotent**: existing stacks are a no-op, only the new components' stacks are created incrementally) → progress as in section 4.1 → on success the registry's `components` array is appended and the new resource names are written back.
- **Idempotency and safety**: ⚠️ **"add only, change nothing" holds only while the source template is unchanged (the template-drift finding, section 12.6)** — CDK / CloudFormation no-ops only stacks whose template bytes are unchanged; if add-components builds from an evolved source, it may produce a changeset for the core stack, or even replace stateful resources. Therefore add-components **pins the commit / template with which the env was created** (sections 12.6 / 12.5, source acquisition), stacks only the new component stacks on top and replays the rest with the original template; stateful core stacks (Redshift / MWAA) get a changeset approval gate, and unattended replacement is forbidden.
- **Dependency validation**: adding a component with prerequisites is validated (for example the CDC → warehouse chain of `dms` / `flink`: if the target is redshift, prompt "add the redshift component first"); only after `mwaa` is added can that region's tasks enable scheduling (otherwise the push-down preflight of the schedule blocks it, section 4.5).
- **Typical path**: first `core` (batch + lake up at minimal cost) → add `redshift` when the business needs a warehouse → add `kafka` + `flink` / `dms` when streaming is needed → add `agentcore` when region-local Ask-Your-Data is needed → **a cross-jurisdiction region (`jurisdiction≠home`) adds `residency-api` (regional data plane + regional result / memory storage, section 8.5.2)**. Each step is independent, traceable, with visible progress.
- **The reverse (uninstalling a single component)**: phase one **does not** uninstall single components (deleting a CloudFormation stack must handle data / dependencies; high risk); to remove a component, "delete the whole execution environment" (section 4.4) or manual operations. ⚠️ **This is a "capability boundary", not a deferred feature** (no conflict with the "everything in phase one" decision: that decision covers the four axes of section 19 and the consumption / operations side; uninstalling a single component is **a new capability** that was never in the requirements).

---

## 5. Task model: region binding and push-down (D5)

### 5.1 Definition model: one definition bound to many regions (primary) + region-specialized copies (secondary)

| Model | Semantics | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **(a) One definition, many bindings (selected as primary)** | Resource definitions gain 🔴 **`targets?: string[]`(revision 3.0: a list of targetIds, replacing `regions?`; default = [home target])**; at publish / run time the definition is instantiated per bound **target** | ✅ Directly cures the customer pain point "one codebase, N copies, afraid of drift" — there is only one definition; change it once and it takes effect in every region |
| (b) Copy-based (per-region copies) | Copy the definition into region-specific copies and edit each separately | As a **secondary** mechanism: satisfies "tasks allowed to differ per region" — a region is "specialized" out of the shared definition (reusing the copy mechanism of [the project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), with the target dimension changed from project to region, recording `specializedFrom`); after specialization that region uses the copy while the other regions keep using the shared definition |

> ⚠️ **First architecture review round: (a) and (b) carry an inherent tension that must be stated clearly to users / the customer, not glossed over**. The entire value of (a) is "one definition, change once, effective in every region" (curing drift at the root); as soon as (b) specializes a region, that region **leaves** the automatic synchronization of the shared definition — in essence "re-introducing drift in a controlled way". This is not a bug but a trade-off: the customer wants both "one codebase, many regions" and "regions may differ", and the two cannot both hold for the same resource. Design trade-off: **specialization is explicit, leaves a trace, and is visible** — ① specialization must be an explicit administrator action (never the default); ② the version dashboard (section 5.3) gives specialized (region × definition) cells a **special marker**, "detached from the shared definition; does not follow updates of the main definition", so that "which regions have drifted" is visible at a glance (turning hidden drift into an explicit ledger); ③ a reverse action "cancel specialization and follow the shared definition again" is provided. **(a) is strongly recommended by default**; (b) is only for the few tasks with genuine regional differences (for example different table partitioning strategies per region).

- Scope = the same five configuration resource types as the environment-promotion design (sync / etl-job / engine-job / workflow / redshift-task) + flink-job; data sources **are not bound to multiple regions** (a data source has a physical location, and its connection bag already contains its own region — execution-chain research note, section 6; when a task is bound to a region for execution, a preflight verifies that the data sources it references are reachable from that region, section 12.3).
  - 🎨 **The interaction handling of regional heterogeneity (decided) is in [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 2bis**: it was stressed that "not all regional tasks are absolutely identical; some regions have exclusive data". By design, ① **task heterogeneity** = selective binding through the region multi-select (already supported; an exclusive task = bound to a single region, not an anomaly); ② **data heterogeneity** = a data source exclusive to one region (the region fact in the connection bag); tasks referencing it can only be bound to that region by the preflight, and other regions are greyed out with an explanation; ③ the dashboard visually distinguishes "region-exclusive / data unavailable" cells from "missed push-down" to avoid false alarms. This is the interaction surfacing of this section's "a data source has a physical location" conclusion; the backend conclusion is unchanged.
- **Ask-Your-Data / BI is "which region to query", not "binding"** (the data-service feature was retired on 2026-09-05; [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), revision 3.23, section 3.9c); it goes through the request-level env parameter of section 8.

### 5.2 Push-down action (publish)

Publishing (publish / schedule-enable / start) changes from "generate one artifact" to "**generate + push per bound region**":
- **🔴 Push-down preflight: component presence (aligned with moving Redshift out of core; prevents blowing up only at run time)** — before a task is bound / pushed to a region, verify that the region has the required components installed; if one is missing, **grey out / refuse with the message "X is not deployed in this region; add it first via supplemental deployment (section 4.6)"**: a `redshift-task` or an `etl-job` sinking to Redshift → needs `redshift`; enabling a schedule → needs `mwaa` (already in section 4.5); a `flink-job` → needs `flink`; real-time CDC → needs `dms`. This shares its source with the validations of section 4.5 (mwaa) and section 4.6 (component dependencies) and is done once at the push-down entry point.
- workflow: generate the DAG per region (the dagId carries a region suffix, for example `bgp_wf_<id>__usw2`; home carries no suffix for compatibility) → write it to **that region's** DAG bucket → unpause in that region's MWAA;
- sync / etl / emr: bake the Glue script for that region's ExecutionEnv → upload it to that region's scripts bucket → ensure + run on that region's engine;
- all baked values of the artifact (region / buckets / workgroup / roles / table names) come from the ExecutionEnv descriptor (the change points = the "artifact baking" list in the infrastructure research note, section 4, plus the table in the execution-chain research note, section 8).
- **Push-down is serial per region and fail-fast** (section 9); each region's push-down result lands in the `deployments` sub-structure: `{region: {version, publishedAt, status}}`.

### 5.3 Version-consistency dashboard (the direct answer to "afraid of missing a region")

The customer's own words: "afraid that an operation misses a region so that the data no longer matches" — one definition with many bindings already cures "changing the code and missing a region" at the root (there is only one definition); the remaining risk = **a partially successful push-down** (a region's push-down fails and nobody follows up). Designed as a first-class citizen:
- The "Multi-Region Dashboard" page: a matrix view = resource definition × bound region, each cell showing the pushed version; **version mismatches / failed push-downs are highlighted** + one-click re-push;
- Data verification (optional column): the output row counts of the same task's most recent run in each region side by side (taken from task-runs metrics), with abnormal deviations highlighted — the early signal of "the data no longer matches".

---

## 6. Reworking the execution chains (the full `file:line` evidence is in the preparatory code survey of the execution chains; this section only fixes the changes)

### 6.1 SDK client factory (the cross-cutting foundation)

New `lib/aws/clients.ts`: `getClient(Service, region)` (Map cache, region defaults to home). The 117 module-level singletons are **not all changed at once** — only the chains touched by multi-region execution change (the "client construction sites" column of the execution-chain survey, section 8); the rest stay singletons (they only call the control plane, behavior unchanged). datasource-spi and the Kafka branch of flink-service already have this shape (the connection bag's region takes precedence); copy it.

> 🔴 **The convergence point must be upgraded from a "convention" to a "constraint" (a P1 finding of the architecture review)**: this design itself caught **5 direct-warehouse-query bypasses** that skirt the 4 atomic functions and each build their own region singleton with a `bgp-workgroup` fallback (table in section 8.1: `quality/service.ts:29`, `metrics/service.ts:21`, `modeling/redshift.ts:18`, `sensitive/scan.ts:37`, `catalog/route.ts:79`) — **this proves that "convergence by convention" cannot hold back entropy; the bypasses already exist**. Once the three designs (the region, project and model dimensions) land, if no mechanical guard rail is added, a 6th and a 7th bypass will inevitably reappear, the convergence point exists in name only, and a platform that is "uniform on the surface and divergent underneath" becomes even harder to troubleshoot. **Guard rail (recommended to land with the foundation construction batch)**: add lint / change-safety rules (reusing the existing change-safety PreToolUse hook mechanism) that **forbid new bare `new RedshiftDataClient` / `new GlueClient` etc. (must go through the `getClient` factory), bare task-runs `PutCommand` (must go through the stamping wrapper), and bare Bedrock `InvokeModel` (must go through `resolveModelIdRuntime`)**; the 5 existing bypasses are folded in when the consumption-side construction batch converges. This is the key gate that makes "the blast radius of the next new requirement shrink (more flexible) instead of grow (more rigid)" (see section 13, evolvability).

### 6.2 Parameterizing artifact generation

The signatures of `generateGlueScript` / `generateAirflowDag(V2)` / the ETL and EMR DAG generation functions uniformly gain an `env: ExecutionEnv` parameter; every baked-in value is taken from env (4 region sites in glue-script-generator.ts; the region / workgroup / db / warehouse ARN templates of the dag-generator family; the applicationId / role baked in by the schedule route). The `spec.region` at dag-generator-v2.ts:128 is a ready-made hook; its meaning is upgraded to "execution region".

### 6.3 Splitting runtime-lib into two regions (🔴 the most critical single point)

Current state: `_mk(spec)` feeds one `spec.region` to all three clients — glue / redshift-data / dynamodb (runtime-lib.ts:104-114). The change:
- The DAG spec splits into two fields: `region` (execution region, feeds glue / redshift-data) + **`controlRegion`** (new, feeds dynamodb); `_mk` splits into `_mk_exec()` / `_mk_ctrl()`;
- The legacy DAG / ETL DAG / EMR DAG change the same way (the 7 hard-coded sites listed in the execution-chain survey, section 4);
- **The byte-level alignment of the lock across its 4 sites is not broken**: the Node `run-lock.ts` and backfill `execute.ts:90` already run in the control-plane process; after the runtime-lib side switches to `_mk_ctrl`, all three parties still write the same attribute of the same table in the control plane — add one lock-parity unit test that pins the `controlRegion` semantics (following the earlier lock-parity precedent);
- 🔴 **Revision 3.0 correction of the lock scope (an item of the independent architecture review, CONFIRMED; measured to be worse than the review judged)**: today the workflow lock **key is only workflowId** and the attribute is only `runLock_<nodeId>` (`runtime-lib.ts:195,248`), and the ETL lock **key is only jobId** (`etl/dag-generator.ts:74-79`) — **when the same shared definition is bound to two targets, two regions running the same node contend for the same lock**: the workflow side waits the full 2700 s and throws (`:251`), the ETL side throws `AirflowSkipException` (`:117`) = **the whole execution is silently skipped** (not "false serialization" but **a lost execution with no error**). This **directly contradicts** section 9, "each target runs in parallel by nature".
  - **The correct invariant (fixed)**: **same target + same task (+ same node) = mutually exclusive**; **different target + same task = parallel allowed**.
  - **Landing (🔴 corrected again in revision 3.0: the former "key includes the target" is [not implementable] under the existing storage structure — the lock hangs on an attribute of the business row, and the key is the business row's primary key; the implementation level follows [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.6)**: lock identity = **the attribute name carries the target** — `runLock_<targetId>` (ETL / EMR) / `runLock_<targetId>_<nodeId>` (workflow); **byte-aligned across the 4 sites** (Node `etl/run-lock.ts`, the Node workflow lock, Python `runtime-lib._acquire_wf_lock`, 🔴 Python `etl/dag-generator.ts:67-90`) + `backfill/execute.ts:91`; the `targetId` character set `[a-z0-9_]` makes sanitization on both sides an invariant (the specification, section 1.1a); the `lock-parity` unit test grows by three cases — ① **different targetIds on the same business row may hold the lock at the same time**; ② the same targetId and the same nodeId are strictly mutually exclusive; ③ **all four sites produce the same string** (following the headless-browser evidence of the earlier lock incident).
  - ⚠️ **When the home DynamoDB is unreachable**: the lock cannot be obtained ⇒ **fail-closed, refuse to start**; never "let it through if the lock cannot be obtained" (otherwise a double run on the same target collides on the OID — the lesson of the earlier double-run incident); see section 14bis.2(b), SLA.
- The Glue sync script does not write DynamoDB (stdout markers are back-filled), but the CloudWatch Logs client the platform uses to fetch logs (start/route.ts:254) and the whole pollGlueJob / reconcile family build their clients by **the task's execution region**.

### 6.4 Mandatory changes per chain

The summary table of the execution-chain survey, section 8, is the construction list (sync Glue / DMS / Zero-ETL, the three ETL engines, Flink, workflow + MWAA, backfill, interactive, advisors). Special items:
- **🔴 The Glue Connection of a JDBC source is a region-level resource (added by the architecture review; the original draft missed it)**: `glueConnectionName` is a single field on the data-source item (`datasource-spi/normalize.ts:23`); at registration `createGlueConnection` creates it once in the home region's Glue (`datasources/route.ts:137`), and sync start references it directly (`start/route.ts:140`). When a JDBC source (MySQL / PG / …) is pushed down to execute in region B, the connection that B's Glue job `--connection` points to **does not exist in B at all** → the job must fail. Section 5.1, "data sources are not bound to multiple regions + pre-validate reachability", only solved "the source database is reachable over the network"; **it did not solve "the Connection artifact must be rebuilt in B"**. Two options, to be decided before construction starts: (a) in phase one, **JDBC-source sync can execute only in the home region** (pre-validation: a task bound to a non-home region that references a JDBC source → rejected with a hint); DynamoDB / S3 / Kinesis / MSK sources have no such limit (their connection bag carries its own region and is already half region-aware); (b) in phase two, rebuild a same-named Glue Connection on demand in the target region (requires a network path to the source database in that region + cross-region secret reads or a per-region secret copy). **Phase one is recommended to take (a)**, leaving cross-region JDBC to the consumption-side construction batch;
- **EMR subnets / security groups are VPC resources** (platform-env.ts:26-31) → taken from the env descriptor's `emrSubnetIds` / `emrSecurityGroupId`; Flink likewise (`FLINK_SUBNET_IDS` → descriptor);
  - 🔴 **A third process-level single-value cache, `_sgIdCache` (added by the later deep review; same class and same disease as `admin-secret._arn` (section 15) / `zeroetl-service._secretArnCache`)**: `platform-env.ts:27-46` discovers the security group by the name `bgp-emr-sg` and then **caches it as a process-level single value** — under multi-region it would pollute region B with home's security group id (B's EMR referencing home's security group = cross-region illegal / not found). It must be changed **in the same batch as the other two caches to a per-target cache (keyed by `targetId`)** and folded into the cache rework surface of section 15; **if any one of the three caches is missed, that path hits the wrong region**;
- **Zero-ETL requires both ends in the same region**: the source RDS and the target namespace must be in the same region (the execution-chain survey, section 1e); validate it when the region is chosen in the front end;
- The DMS `REDSHIFT_HOST` hostname embeds the region → derived from the descriptor;
- Backfill / dependency triggering (dependency.ts) runs in the control plane and chooses the execution client by the run's region field;
- sync's `scheduleEnabled` is only persisted, never executed (already surveyed in the execution-chain survey, section 4) — multi-region scheduling uniformly goes through a workflow-attached node; no separate scheduling path is built for sync.

---

## 7. Unified metadata reporting (D6, explicitly requested)

### 7.1 Adding dimensions to bgp-task-runs (same batch as the project-isolation design; 🔴 the largest single point)

`bgp-task-runs` today has zero dimensions (no projectId / env / region) and is consumed by four modules: monitoring, cost attribution, baseline and alerting. **Add the attribute bundle `{projectId, targetId}` in one pass** (already agreed in [the project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 8; in revision 3.0 `region` → `targetId`; the region can be looked up from the target): the write sites = ⚠️ **a repository-wide grep during the architecture review measured 13 item-creation sites (9 inside the platform process + 4 on the MWAA bypass); the 4 in-process sites missing from the original 5+4 list have been added in the project-isolation design, section D2** (`sync/[id]/start:464` zero-etl, `sync/[id]/start-realtime:121` DMS, `workflow/[id]/trigger:72` manual, `scheduler/dependency:64` dependency-triggered workflow); legacy records default to `{proj-default, home}`. The consumption side (monitor / finops / baseline / alerting) gains region filtering / grouping in the same batch; **the full-table Scan of Copilot `list_recent_runs` (read-tools.ts:103-119) must also be included** (added in the project-isolation design, section D2).

#### 7.1a 🔴 Revision 3.0 correction of the run primary key (an item of the independent architecture review, CONFIRMED; the former "just add attributes, zero table migration" does not hold)

**The collision is confirmed**: `bgp-task-runs` today has PK=`taskId` / SK=`runId` (`database-stack.ts:31`), while a workflow run's `runId` **is taken directly from Airflow's `scheduled__<logical_date>`** (`runtime-lib.ts:140`) = **deterministically identical for the same cron**, and `taskId` is **the definition id shared across targets** ⇒ the same definition bound to two targets, at the same cron instant → **`(taskId, runId)` completely identical → the later `put_item` overwrites the earlier one**: one run record vanishes into thin air, and monitoring / cost attribution / baseline / alerting are all wrong. **Putting region / targetId in as an ordinary attribute (not in the key) = the collision is not resolved.**

**Decision (value-level composite runId, no table rebuild; 🔴 corrected again in revision 3.0: the target segment goes in as a [suffix], not a prefix)**: on the write side `runId` becomes **`<original runId>#<targetId>`**; the key **schema is unchanged** (still PK=taskId / SK=runId string) ⇒ **no table rebuild, no dual-write migration**; existing legacy runIds (without `#`) are interpreted as the home target; split on the **last** `#`. 🔴 **Why it must be a suffix**: a prefix would break three classes of existing reads — the 6 sites using `ScanIndexForward:false+Limit:1` to fetch "the most recent run" would fetch **the one with the largest targetId**, the 4 sites using `startsWith("scheduled__")` would all miss (**every scheduled run would be judged manual**), and 2 sites that take the latest by lexical order would stop working. `Query PK=taskId` still fetches the runs of all targets; **"the most recent run of a given target" goes through the GSI `byTaskTarget` (pk=`taskTargetKey`=`<taskId>#<targetId>`, sk=`startedAt`), no longer through `begins_with`** (🔴 **revision 3.5 correction**: the former `byTarget(pk=targetId, sk=startedAt)` **is withdrawn** — it has no taskId dimension, and `Limit` is applied before the filter ⇒ `Limit:1` almost always returns empty; building it as originally written would **create the wrong index** and make the acceptance criterion for the most-recent-run lookup FAIL). Implementation level: [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.2.
- ⚠️ **Cost and convergence point (honestly, not to be underestimated)**: `runId` is a **shared downstream identifier** — log paths (`monitor/tasks/[id]/logs`), diagnostic evidence, backfill, cost attribution, Copilot `list_recent_runs`, and the front-end run detail all read it. Therefore **every runId read site must be enumerated one by one** and uniformly passed through one `parseRunId()` (returns `{targetId, rawRunId}`, legacy → home); **any read site missed = that place cannot find the run or mismatches it**. Listed as a deliverable of the foundation construction batch (**same batch as the 13 write sites; reads and writes go live together**).
- **Alternative (only if the read side proves excessively entangled)**: change the PK to `<projectId>#<targetId>#<taskId>` (the independent review's original proposal) — **requires an online migration + dual write / dual read + a rollback window**; risk and effort are significantly larger; **not taken by default**.
- 🔴 **Indexes for scale (an item of the independent architecture review, CONFIRMED)**: add GSIs covering the real access patterns — 🔴 **corrected in revision 3.5 to the final choice of the specification, section 1.2**: **`byTaskTarget` (pk=`taskTargetKey`=`<taskId>#<targetId>`, sk=`startedAt`)**, `byProject` (pk=`projectId`, sk=`startedAt`), **`byStatus` (pk=`statusDay`=`<status>#<yyyy-mm-dd>`, sk=`startedAt`)** (the former `byTarget(pk=targetId)` and `byStatus(pk=status)` **are withdrawn**: the former has no taskId dimension and `Limit` is applied before the filter ⇒ `Limit:1` almost always returns empty; the latter is a single-partition hot spot); the **bounded `Scan`s** of monitor / alerting / baseline / finops (`alerting/evaluate.ts:43` Limit=500, `baseline/learn.ts:120` Limit=2000; their comments describe themselves as "demo scale") **change to Query** — once multiplied by many targets, a bounded Scan returns only the first page **without an error = silently missed data** (more dangerous than an error).
- **Audit is sharded for the same reason**: `audit.ts:12` `pk="audit"` is a single logical partition (its comment describes itself as workshop / demo low volume) → **shard by `region|tenant|day` + archive** (same batch as the audit residency of section 19.4).

### 7.2 The region dimension of FQN / OM (curing collisions in the governance layer at the root)

One codebase running in many regions → workgroup / db / schema / table are all identically named → today's FQNs (`redshift.<db>.<schema>.<table>`, OM `bgp-redshift.<db>…`) **must collide** across regions (the consumption-side survey, section 5). The change:
- **OM side: the service name carries a target segment** (🔴 revision 3.0: the former wording said a region segment — two targets in the same region would **collide on the same service name** and metadata / lineage / classification would all be mixed up; the specification, section 6.5, question 2) — a non-home target is registered as **`bgp-redshift-<targetId>` / `bgp-s3-<targetId>`**; home keeps the old names (zero migration of existing lineage / catalog). OM is organized by service by nature, so the catalog / lineage UI needs no parsing change; **the OM ingestion loop iterates by target** (not by region);
- **Platform-side FQNs are isomorphic**: the fqn-generation functions of lineage-service / quality / classification / grants converge on one authoritative function (following the single-source-of-truth lesson of the sanitize family and the earlier lesson on identifier / FQN metadata traps); a non-home prefix carries the region;
- OM ingestion (Fargate, scheduled): loops over the active envs in the registry and collects region by region (per-region Glue / Redshift connections, service names by the rule above).

### 7.3 Configuration metadata (resource definitions / glossary / projects = zero change; 🔴 cookbooks = non-zero change, the earlier misjudgment is corrected)

Configuration-class tables such as resource definitions / business glossary / projects live in the control plane, **zero change**; only resource definitions gain the `regions` binding field (section 5.1).

🔴 **Exception: cookbooks are not "zero change" (the coverage audit of 2026-08-28, section 19.5, corrects the earlier misjudgment here)** — the seed cookbooks **weld in** home resource names (`bgp-workgroup` / `dev`) and flow into 6 codegen outlets; under multi-region, code generated for region B hard-codes the home warehouse → cannot connect or violates residency (reading and writing us-east-1 from region B). Cookbooks must switch to **platform-context injection of schema / resource names** (region + project) and the core-dist copies must be redistributed (P0, details in section 19.5; same source as the public cookbook of the project-isolation design).

---

## 8. The consumption side (D7; evidence in the preparatory survey of the consumption side)

### 8.1 Making the 4 atomic functions ExecutionEnv-aware (the main convergence point; ⚠️ not full coverage)

> **🔴 The consumption-side boundary of a no-redshift region (aligned with "Redshift moved out of core"; the design layer must declare it first — the original draft handled no-redshift only at the admin secret)**: a region may install core only (no warehouse). Therefore every **consumption path that hits a Redshift workgroup** (querying `runSelect`, the 5 direct warehouse queries quality / metrics / modeling / sensitive / catalog, and OM ingestion's Redshift collection) degrades uniformly **when the target region has no `redshift` component installed**: ① in the UI, that region's "SQL querying / warehouse-class analysis" entry points are greyed out with the hint "this region has no data warehouse deployed; only data-lake (Athena / S3 Tables) queries are supported"; ② the direct warehouse-query functions return "capability unavailable" for a no-redshift env instead of throwing; ③ OM ingestion **skips** the Redshift service according to `components` and collects only Glue / S3 Tables. **Paths that go through Athena / S3 Tables (`runSelectAthena` / `introspectS3Tables`) are unaffected** (core ships them) — that is, a core-only region still has full data-lake querying / BI / governance capability; it just has no warehouse path. This boundary belongs to the consumption-side construction batch, but the design layer fixes it here.

The signatures of `runSelect` / `runSelectAthena` / `introspectSchema` / `introspectS3Tables` gain `env?: ExecutionEnv` (defaults to home, behavior unchanged) — GenBI / BI / Copilot discovery / governance health score / OM back-fill thereby gain region capability (Data Service was retired on 2026-09-05) (the consumption-side survey, section 0). Done in the foundation construction batch (pure parameterization, zero behavior change).

**⚠️ Fact-check correction from the architecture review: the 4 atomic functions ≠ the whole consumption-side convergence.** The repository has 22 `new RedshiftDataClient` sites; besides the interactive chain (covered in section 6.4), **another 5 direct warehouse-query paths skirt the 4 atomic functions and each build their own `region` singleton with a `bgp-workgroup` fallback, which sections 6–8 of the original draft never addressed** — they must be made ExecutionEnv-aware together, otherwise modeling / metrics / quality / sensitive sampling in region B cannot run:

| Path | Evidence | Current state | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| Data-quality rule execution | `lib/quality/service.ts:29,151` | `rsData` singleton + WORKGROUP fallback | Section 7.2 converged only its **FQN**; the **query execution region** must also converge (build the client by the env that owns the inspected table) |
| Metric materialization / preview | `lib/metrics/service.ts:21,183` | Same as above | Same as above |
| Data-modeling direct query | `lib/modeling/redshift.ts:18,40,201` | Same as above; **the original draft never mentioned modeling anywhere** | Included in the convergence surface of section 8.1 |
| Sensitive-data sampling | `lib/governance/sensitive/scan.ts:37,75` | The catalog side uses introspectSchema (gains region with section 8.1), **but the sampling SELECT uses its own rsData singleton** (half done) | The sampling client also consumes env |
