| Governance catalog fallback | `app/api/governance/catalog/route.ts:79` | Builds Glue + RS clients directly, **hard-coded `us-east-1` fallback** | Both fallback clients are built by env |

Therefore the premise of section 6.1, "the remaining singletons only call the control plane, behavior unchanged", **does not hold for these 5** (they hit the execution environment's warehouse) — these 5 files must be listed in the consumption-side construction batch's rework list (same batch as the 4 atomic functions). Also: the `workgroup/database` of consumers such as `genbi/agent.ts` go through the `GENBI` constants (`config.ts:12,19`, pinned at module load time); beyond adding `env?` to the 4 functions, **the callers' parameter source (the GENBI constants) must also become ExecutionEnv-aware**.

> 🔴 **Forward caveat on cross-jurisdiction residency (added by the later deep review; in step with section 8.5.7 ① ③)**: this section lets the 4 atomic functions + 5 direct warehouse queries "hit the execution environment's warehouse" — valid for **same-jurisdiction** regions; but for **cross-jurisdiction** regions, if these paths (especially `sensitive/scan` pulling 200 detail rows, and the quality / metrics / modeling direct queries) are still initiated by the central in-process code, they pull business data back to us-east-1. Across jurisdictions they must **execute in the regional data plane, with the center receiving only non-data-value verdicts / aggregates** (convergence mechanism in section 8.5.7 ① ③), and the central entry is fail-closed (section 8.5.5 ①).

### 8.2 Single-region querying (decided: one region per question, that region's model, no parallelism)

- **UI**: a region selector at the top of the Ask-Your-Data page (list of active envs, defaults to home); one conversation is anchored to one region (switching region prompts to start a new conversation, to avoid mixing regions in the context).
- **Request chain**: the `ExecuteGenBiParams` / genbi-tasks row carries **`targetId`** end to end (the consumption-side survey, section 1f; revision 3.1: the field name is unified to `targetId`, `envId` is withdrawn); the in-process path = the 4 atomic functions with env + the Bedrock client by env.region + the modelId resolved through [the model-configuration design](./model-configuration.md)'s `resolveModelIdRuntime(capability, targetId)` (activating that design's runtime-resolution hook);
  - ⚠️ **Model-configuration cache invalidation across regions (an item of the deep review)**: the model-configuration design's "the write side bumps the version to invalidate immediately" is a **process-local** check; the per-region skill Lambdas / each region's processes **each have their own cache**, and a bump of `ai-model#<targetId>#global` in the control plane **is not actively pushed to cross-region processes** → convergence can only rely on that design's **60-second TTL as the backstop** (not immediate). Acceptable (the same trade-off as that design's dual-instance case), but it must be stated: after changing a per-region model, **it takes up to 60 s for that region to fully pick it up**; acceptance does not demand immediacy; if immediacy is required, a cross-region invalidation broadcast must be added (listed for phase two; phase one uses the TTL).
- **The agentcore path = a per-region triple**: when an execution environment installs the `agentcore` component, that region's GenBI Gateway + Runtime + Memory + skill Lambda are deployed (wiring stored in the registry's `agentcore` bag); the platform fetches the ARN by **`targetId`** and invokes it. A target without that component automatically falls back to the in-process backend (reusing the existing fallback mechanism);
- **The glossary / skill table (genbi-context) stays in the control plane; the execution region's skill Lambda reads it across regions** (✅ **closed and decided in revision 3.1; the former "awaiting a decision" is withdrawn**): a cross-region DynamoDB read needs only IAM + a few tens of milliseconds of latency and avoids replication-consistency problems; the alternative = replicate a copy per target (rejected because: changing a glossary entry in one place would have to be broadcast to N regions, reintroducing drift). **Basis of the decision = the specification, section 3.11, already implements it this way** ("GenBI retrieval: glossary / skills / OM descriptions **are filtered by the target**" ⇒ a single central table + target-dimension filtering, not a copy per region). 🔴 **Residency boundary (constraint attached to the decision)**: staying in the control plane presupposes that **genbi-context stores only metadata such as glossary / skills / OM descriptions and must not store business-data rows**; if a glossary entry's body embeds sample data values, handle it under section 8.5 track 2 (stored on the regional side, the center stores only a pointer).
  - ⚠️ **Added by the architecture review: the skill Lambda's DynamoDB client must explicitly name the control-plane region — the same class of problem as the runtime-lib two-region split (section 6.3)**. A per-region skill Lambda's default `AWS_REGION` = the execution region it lives in; when reading the control plane's `genbi-context` / writing `genbi-history` and similar tables with a default-region client, it would hit the execution region (where the table does not exist). The per-region skill Lambda must explicitly pin the client for control-plane tables to `controlRegion` (injected through the Lambda env), and IAM must grant it cross-region reads of the control-plane table ARNs (section 12.2 already includes the skill Lambda reading genbi-context; write permissions for the conversation tables such as history / turns must be added, or the writes routed back through the platform). This is the counterpart, on the AgentCore path, of the section 6.3 principle "DynamoDB always points at the control plane"; the original draft covered only the platform process and missed the agentcore path.
  - ⚠️ **Cross-jurisdiction caveat (the later deep review; in step with section 8.5.7 ④)**: the above "skill Lambda reads the control plane's genbi-context / writes turns · history back to the control plane" holds only for **genbi-context (pure metadata)**; **history / turns / sql-history contain questions, answers and data values, so a cross-jurisdiction region must write the regional tables and not write back to the control plane** (only same-jurisdiction regions write back). Hence a cross-jurisdiction skill Lambda's conversation-table client points at the **region**, not controlRegion.
- **Memory**: self-managed (DynamoDB + S3 summaries) stays in the control plane (read and written across regions); in AgentCore Memory mode it follows the component per region (a conversation is anchored to a region anyway, memory is not shared across regions). 🔴 **Residency correction (section 8.5, decided as tier one)**: the above "memory stays in the control plane, read and written across regions" holds only for **same-jurisdiction** regions; **for a cross-jurisdiction region, the querying memory / turns / history contain questions, answers and data values and must stay in that region, never written back to the control plane** (the glossary / skill table genbi-context is pure metadata and may stay in the control plane).
- History / task rows carry **`targetId`**; the history page can filter by target (and by its region attribute).

### 8.3 BI / Copilot (Data Service was retired on 2026-09-05; the DataApi row is withdrawn; [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), revision 3.23, section 3.9c)

- BI `WidgetSource` + `region?` (defaults to home); executeBiQuery routes the env per widget — **a dashboard may mix widgets from different regions** (each widget fetches its own data; small rework surface; the preference of the consumption-side survey, section 3);
- ~~DataApi definition + `region?`, executeDataApi converges the same way~~ **withdrawn** (Data Service retired on 2026-09-05);
- Copilot: brain / memory stay in the control plane; `discover_catalog` / `introspect_source_tables` gain a region parameter; the planner's draft output is annotated with the target region (PlanSpec params gain region).

> 🔴 **Forward caveat on cross-jurisdiction residency (the later deep review; in step with section 8.5.7 ① ②)**: BI data fetching (`bi/query` / `bi/export`), DataApi execute, and Copilot's `discover_catalog` / `introspect_source_tables` all touch cross-jurisdiction result rows → across jurisdictions they go through the regional data plane and the endpoint-classified data client (section 8.5.7 ②), and the center is fail-closed; on a mixed dashboard each widget fetches from its own regional data plane and the browser composes them (section 8.5.4). Copilot's "brain / memory stay in the control plane" holds only for metadata / orchestration; sub-calls that touch data values converge as above.

### 8.4 Cost / monitoring: the first level is by region (explicitly requested)

- FinOps: the Cost Explorer collection adds a `REGION` GroupBy (collector.ts); the `bgp-cost-daily` sk gains a region segment; the price list is split per region (`PRICE_VERSION` → per-region); run-level attribution gets region for free (task-runs, section 7.1); **the first level of the cost page = region cards / drill-down**, and only then the existing service / task views;
- Monitoring / alerting: the ledger is in the control plane (the evaluation logic is region-agnostic); the monitor page gains a region filter column; the three CloudWatch read paths (observability / flink / diagnostics) build their clients by the task's execution region;
- Governance catalog / lineage: the first level gains a region filter (naturally filterable once the OM service name carries the region).

---

### 8.5 🔴 Data-residency mode (tier one + Option 1; decided on 2026-08-27, and on 2026-08-28 decision B, two tracks: interactive query results = the user is responsible / background automatic data movement = the platform controls it)

> - **Origin (the global-grade deep review + the design conversation)**: section 8 was originally designed "convenience first" — the query is dispatched to the execution region, but **the result set comes back to the control plane (us-east-1) through the in-process 4 atomic functions to be rendered
>   / staged** (sections 8.1 / 8.2); for a **cross-jurisdiction** deployment this constitutes **read-side cross-border data transfer** (confirmed with AWS: when us-east-1 initiates a cross-region Athena / Redshift Serverless query, the data is pulled into us-east-1 and the result lands in us-east-1)
>   . The promise "business data stays in its region" was pierced on the **read / display side**. This section completes the read-side residency design.
> - **Decided on 2026-08-27**: ① tier = **tier one (mainstream data residency / GDPR: processing and storage of business / personal data staying in the region is compliant; serving UI code and accessing metadata from us-east-1 is acceptable)**; ② approach = **Option 1 (the lightest)**; ③
>   enforcement = **single account + architectural boundaries as the main line**; ④ cross-jurisdiction SQL history / querying memory **stays in the region** (accepting that there is no global unified view).

#### 8.5.0 🔴 Setting the residency model (decision B of 2026-08-28; governs this whole section — it overrides every "hard block / strictly forbidden to touch results" wording below)

> 🔴🔴 **Revision 3.20 correction (2026-09-05, section 18, decision 6quinquies; overrides all earlier wording in this section)**: the verbatim decision: "We do not make legal decisions; that part is left to the customer to plan, we do not interfere, we only provide the product. If their legal team believes there is a data-compliance problem, they avoid it from the start instead of relying on a tool to solve it. We only need to be auditable." ⇒ in the rest of this section, "track 1 informed + consent + responsibility" reads as "**audit + retention of the disclaimer acknowledgement**"; "track 2 fail-closed refusal / the center does not handle rows" reads as "**audit**; regional computation is a proximity optimization (a later construction item), not a refusal gate"; the paragraph "legal prerequisite = forbidden by default" in section 8.5.6 **is withdrawn**; the "hard residency" option **is not built**; the read-only guard / UNLOAD ban of section 8.5.7 ⑤ **is not built** (the customer manages it; the platform audits). The original text below **is kept as a historical record**, annotated inline, and is not rewritten sentence by sentence.

Decided on 2026-08-28: **"Cross-border movement of query results has to be the user's own doing; such a query will have this cross-border situation, and that is normal."** Accordingly, business-data egress **is split into two tracks and treated differently** (the mechanisms of sections 8.5.1–8.5.7 are reread by track):

- **Track 1 · cross-border movement of query results initiated deliberately by a user (interactive: SQL editor / Athena / Ask-Your-Data / BI / export) = normal, the user is responsible, the platform does not hard-block it**. The platform's duty = **explicit disclosure + consent + audit** ("you are querying cross-jurisdiction data and the result will come back to you = data export = you are responsible", consistent with the practice of other data-platform products, where choosing an overseas region = export = the customer's responsibility). → The **fail-closed hard gate of section 8.5.5 below is downgraded, for interactive queries, to "a disclosure banner + an audit marker", with no reject**; `x-bgp-region` is downgraded from "the criterion of a mandatory gate" to "**routing + an audit label**". **This also resolves the P0 finding of the adversarial review about hard-blocking results**: since "hard-blocking interactive results" is no longer pursued, no server-side DescribeStatement hard criterion and no multi-account SCP is needed to block it. AI querying's `global.*` egress is a user-initiated query and naturally belongs to track 1 (the phase-one gap is accepted unchanged, section 8.5.6).
- **Track 2 · the platform's background / automatic movement of business data back to the center (with no user query action) = still strictly controlled; the platform does not move data on its own initiative**. Includes: OM Profiler's scheduled column-value profiling, background sensitive-data sampling, telemetry / logs carrying results, S3 CRR automatic replication. Across jurisdictions: **compute in the region / the center receives only non-data-value verdicts · counts / CRR off** (mechanisms in section 8.5.7 ③ ④ ⑥ ⑦). This is the platform's own compliance responsibility and **cannot be pushed onto "the user is responsible"**.

> **In one sentence**: a query the user clicks that crosses borders = the user's business (disclosed + audited, allowed through); the platform moving business data behind the user's back = the platform's business (must be controlled). Wherever the text below says "the center strictly must not touch result rows / fail-closed refusal / results do not leave the region", that hard wording **holds only for track 2 (automatic platform movement)**; for track 1 (interactive user queries) it is read throughout as "**disclosed + audited, not blocked**". The hard-boundary table of section 8.5.3 is therefore split into the two readings "track 1 may cross (responsibility) / track 2 strictly controlled"; see the correction to that table.

**🔴 Convergence after the adversarial review (4 patches that make decision B actually hold; the adversarial review established that "relaxing one control without adding a boundary = opening a new hole"):**
1. **The track criterion is the caller type at runtime, not the endpoint (the most critical P0 of the adversarial review)**: track 1 / track 2 **cannot be distinguished by intent words such as "interactive vs. background" or by endpoint names** — `redshift/execute`, `bi/query`, `data-api execute` are **shared** by interactive and scheduled / headless callers. The criterion = **caller type**: a request carrying an **interactive session token** = track 1 (disclosed + audited); **scheduled / service / api-key / headless (MWAA DAG, anonymous data-API, automatic dashboard refresh) = track 2** (controlled, computed in the region), **regardless of who the owner is** — reusing the existing `triggeredBy` (schedule / manual / rerun / backfill, `page.tsx:157`). ⚠️ **A scheduled `INSERT … SELECT FROM eu_table` / a headless data-API call is always track 2** and does not escape track-2 control because "it has a user author / it hits a track-1 endpoint" (otherwise track 2 collapses into "anything that can be wrapped as track 1 is allowed through").
2. **Audit is track 1's only control → it must be hardened (a P0 of the adversarial review)**: B removed track 1's hard gate, so audit went from "a supplement outside the hard gate" to **the only control**, yet it is still an ordinary, modifiable and deletable `bgp-audit` DynamoDB table + the MWAA-path audit blind spot (noted in the project-isolation design) + only the not-yet-existing lint guard rail. Therefore: ① make audit **tamper-proof** (an independent write-only role + deny Delete / Update, or S3 Object Lock / QLDB-style); ② **add an independent audit channel for the MWAA / runtime-lib off-app-layer path** (CloudTrail Data Events / instrumentation inside the DAG writing back), not application-layer only; ③ "the wrapper was called" must be **verifiable at runtime**, not only at lint time (otherwise a missed wrapper call = neither blocked nor audited = silent egress without a trace).
3. **The central server handling rows one by one = track 2, regardless of who clicked (a P1 of the adversarial review)**: `bi/export:2` assembles the CSV server-side, and `genbi/tasks` has the central ECS driver writing rows (section 8.5.7 ⑨, deliverable 2) — that is **the platform's server handling rows one by one**, not "the user taking them back to the browser". Track 1's "the user is responsible" **covers only client-side delivery**; central server-side assembly / driving of cross-jurisdiction rows is always track 2 and must execute in the region.
4. **"The user is responsible" ≠ "the platform is exempt" (a P1 of the adversarial review; the honest compliance boundary, which needs legal endorsement)**: in the analogy with other data-platform products, "the customer = the data controller in person"; here "the user" is the customer's **employee**, the platform is a **processor**, and the data subjects in the result set are usually not the person clicking the button. Therefore under GDPR, **an employee clicking "I am informed and responsible" does not wash away the data subjects' cross-border protection, nor does it waive the processor's obligations under Article 28 / Chapter V**. The design **does not treat "user click = platform exempt" as settled**: disclosure + audit are **accountability / UX** measures, and **the platform-side track-2 egress control is not removed because of them**; before going live across jurisdictions, **legal endorsement of a lawful mechanism** (adequacy / SCC / BCR) is required; the disclosure copy **must not imply that the platform is exempt**. (The research treated GDPR only as a tier label and did no controller / processor analysis; the honest boundary is added here.)

> ⚠️ **Track 2's "strict control" in a single account is still architecture + soft policy** (consistent with the honest boundary of section 8.5.5; only the bucket-level CRR deny of section 8.5.7 ⑥ is hard); the disclosure banner is **permanently visible** per session + jurisdiction (not a one-time pop-up on first visit), so that per-query responsibility is not diluted.

#### 8.5.1 Baseline of judgment: serving app code ≠ cross-border transfer of business data
Compliance looks at **where business / personal data is processed and stored**, not where the app code is hosted. Hence serving the app shell (HTML / JS) from the central CloudFront **does not constitute** cross-border transfer of business data; **what really counts as cross-border is "a server of the control plane (us-east-1) touched cross-jurisdiction result rows"**. Design goal = **every server-side hop that touches result rows is in the data's region; the center degrades to pure metadata / orchestration / authentication and handles not a single result byte**.

#### 8.5.2 Option 1 topology (the front end is not regionalized)
- **Central us-east-1**: CloudFront serves a **single** SPA; the control-plane API (authentication / task definitions / catalog structure / monitoring status / cost / orchestration); Cognito; metadata DynamoDB; the central data API (serving only home and **same-jurisdiction** regions).
- **Each cross-jurisdiction region**: a **"thin" data API** (regional API Gateway + Lambda / Fargate, JWT verified locally against JWKS) + **regional result storage** (DynamoDB / S3 for SQL history / querying memory / exports) + the existing execution environment (Redshift Serverless workgroup / Athena workgroup / Glue catalog) + a querying runtime (reusing the per-region agentcore triple of section 8.2, or a regional in-process runtime). **That is, the consumption functions of section 8.1 such as runSelect / introspect execute in the regional data plane for a cross-jurisdiction region, not in the central in-process code.**
- **Gating**: the data-plane API is **one optional component of section 4.6 (`residency-api`), installed only for regions marked "cross-jurisdiction"**; home and same-jurisdiction regions get **nothing extra** (served directly by the center; behavior = today). The execution-environment descriptor (section 2.1) gains a `jurisdiction` field (default = the home jurisdiction).
- **Interaction when switching execution region**: the same SPA, according to the current execution region, points the base URL of **data-class calls** at that region's data API (cross-jurisdiction) or at the center (same jurisdiction); control-plane-class calls always go to the center. A cross-jurisdiction region shows a **"disclosure" banner** (query results come back to you = data export = you are responsible + fully audited; section 8.5.0 track 1). **No site hop, no regional front end installed** (interaction in [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 4bis).

#### 8.5.3 The hard boundary between central and regional

| Central only (metadata; allowed to leave the region) | Results / data values (two tracks per section 8.5.0) |
|---|---|
| query id, region, row count, scanned bytes, duration, status, model used, **masked or omitted SQL** | **Result rows, answer text of querying, SQL source text (may contain literal PII), querying memory / turns, exported files, query logs** |

🔴 **Added in revision 3.0 (axis D, sections 19.4 / 20.1-8): audit itself also splits into two classes; it is not "audit = metadata that may stay central unconditionally"** — ① **may stay central (non-PII summary)**: the queryId **hash**, target / region, row count, scanned bytes, duration, status, verdict; ② **must stay in the regional audit store across jurisdictions**: **operator identity (email / sub), SQL source text, the queryId in clear, answer / result fragments** — they are **regulated personal data + the key that can trace back that cross-border data**; sinking them to the center = cross-border transfer of personal data, which **contradicts** the premise of this table's left column, "metadata allowed to leave the region" (the left column means "technical metadata containing no personal data"). Tamper-proofing (write-only + deny delete / update) **is applied on the regional side as well**.

→ The right column (results / data values) follows the two tracks of section 8.5.0: **track 1 (results retrieved by a deliberate user query) = may cross, the user is responsible, the platform discloses + audits and does not block**; **track 2 (automatic platform background movement: OM Profiler / background sampling / telemetry / CRR) = automatic egress strictly forbidden; across jurisdictions compute in the region and the center receives only non-data values**. The unified monitoring / audit / cost dashboards (section 8.4) consume only metadata and work as before; the glossary / skill table (genbi-context) is pure metadata and may stay in the control plane (corrected in section 8.2).

#### 8.5.4 The data flow of the three query paths (all "browser → regional API → the region's engine → result back to the browser")
- **Redshift interactive SQL**: schema / execute / history hit the regional data API → the region's Redshift Serverless workgroup (ExecuteStatement / GetStatementResult in the region) → the result streams back to the browser; SQL history is written to the **region's** table; export = a presigned URL on the region's result bucket.
- **Athena / data lake**: hits the regional data API → the region's Athena workgroup (**OutputLocation pinned to the region's result bucket + the workgroup's "Override client-side settings" turned on**, preventing results from being redirected elsewhere) + the region's Glue catalog → the result returns to the browser; object download = a presigned URL on the region's S3.
- **Ask-Your-Data (GenBI)**: the regional querying runtime runs the agent loop — runSelect / introspect hit the region's warehouse, the prompt (the region's schema + results) is assembled in the region, the answer (data values) and memory / turns stay **in the region throughout**; the center gets only {category / model / status}. A conversation is anchored to a single region (section 8.2).
  - 🔴 **The residency boundary of the model-invocation hop (in step with the model-configuration design's global-inference-profile decision; the phase-one gap is accepted; this item previously said "avoid the us. profile / in-region throughout", which contradicted that design's "phase one uses `global.*`" — corrected by the later deep review)**: in phase one Bedrock goes through a `global.*` inference profile (default `global.anthropic.claude-opus-4-8`) → **the model invocation routes the prompt (including the region's schema + query results) globally = residency for this AI sub-path is not achieved in phase one**; decided on 2026-08-28: **accept this gap in phase one, do not gate it off** (the pure SQL / Athena / BI data paths do not include this hop, so residency is achieved there). **Phase two region-pinning** (region-pinned models invocable in that region; forbidding the `us.` / `global.` cross-region profiles; avoiding "a geographic `us.` profile necessarily fails in non-US regions") closes this hop (a later phase of the model-configuration design / section 15).
- **BI dashboards (correction to section 8.3)**: when cross-jurisdiction widgets are mixed, **each widget's data is fetched from its own region's data API and composed / rendered in the browser**; the center does not merge — Option 1 supports this natively (the browser is the composition point).

#### 8.5.5 Three binding constraints + enforcement (the main line of defense in a single account)
The **constraints** below guarantee that **the central server does not handle business data row by row (not cross-border in the track-2 sense)**; ⚠️ **per the two tracks of section 8.5.0**: track 1 results reaching the **browser** through CSR is still achieved (the user is responsible); these three **constrain "the central server does not touch rows" (track 2 + hard residency)**, not "results may not reach the user's browser" — that is, "only then truly not cross-border" refers to the track-2 meaning:
1. **Views that carry business data must be CSR**: the central Next.js server **never server-side-fetches cross-jurisdiction business data** (the app shell may be SSR but without data) — track-1 results are fetched back by the browser through CSR; track 2 / server-side assembly (such as `bi/export` building the CSV) must execute in the region (section 8.5.0, item 3);
2. **Cross-jurisdiction data calls go browser-direct to the regional API by preference; the central server does not proxy result bytes** (a proximity optimization + a smaller egress surface, not a fail-closed hard gate; section 8.5.7 ②);
3. **Telemetry / logs / error reports contain no result data, or land in a regional sink** (track 2).
- **Enforcement (single account + architectural boundaries as the main line; decided not to go multi-account; per the two tracks of section 8.5.0)**: ① **central data routing = one unified front wrapper that handles both tracks by `env.jurisdiction` (not the credential layer)** — 🔴 the later deep review established that the former "reuse the query-identity convergence L3 of the project-isolation design, section 7bis" **picked the wrong layer** (L3 is "which credential to use", knows nothing of jurisdiction, and `redshift/result/[id]` goes only by statementId and never passes L3); so a front wrapper keyed on `env.jurisdiction` is added at **every central data entry that touches results** (section 8.5.7 ①). **Per B: track 1 (interactive user queries) = the wrapper shows the disclosure banner + sets the audit marker (recording who / which cross-jurisdiction region / query id), no reject**; **track 2 (automatic platform background movement) = that class should never be initiated by the central in-process code in the first place; per section 8.5.7 ③ it computes in the region**. "Immediate fail-closed refusal" is reserved for **track 2** and for the "deployment explicitly requires hard residency" option (section 8.5.6); ② the regional API serves only its region's data; ③ **the lint guard rail** forbids bare clients that bypass the convergence point (track 2) and forces interactive data entries to call the wrapper for audit (track 1); ④ the regional data-plane VPC has no **automatic** data route back to us-east-1 (track 2); ⑤ data-class responses do not pass through the CloudFront cache (`Cache-Control: no-store`, section 8.5.7 ⑧). **The complete convergence mechanisms for read-side egress are in section 8.5.7 (classified by track).**
- **Honest boundary**: in a single account this is **an architectural boundary + soft policy** (`aws:RequestedRegion` / permission boundaries), not an organization-level hard guarantee of "refuse on overreach"; AWS's **regional deny SCPs / Control Tower data-residency controls require Organizations with multiple accounts** (and the management account is not bound by SCPs) — **listed as optional phase-two hardening, not chosen for phase one** (section 18, decision 5).

#### 8.5.6 Phase-one trade-offs (accepted)
- Cross-jurisdiction SQL history / querying memory **stays in the region** → **no "global unified SQL history / cross-region querying memory"** (the center has only a metadata-level index). Accepted (section 18, decision 6).
- 🔴 **The phase-one egress gap of AI-querying model invocation = accepted, not gated off** (decided on 2026-08-28, item b; in step with the model-configuration design's global-inference-profile decision): in phase one the model goes through a `global.*` profile, so for querying / Copilot in a cross-jurisdiction region **the model-invocation hop** routes the prompt globally; **the pure data paths (SQL / Athena / BI results through the regional data plane) still achieve residency**; only the model hop is not closed. Querying in cross-jurisdiction regions **stays open as usual**; region-pinning (a later phase of the model-configuration design) closes it as a follow-up optimization (section 8.5.4). This is a **known and accepted** phase-one boundary, not an overlooked defect.
- 🔴 **Residency strength = soft (tier one; decision B of 2026-08-28)**: cross-border movement of interactive query results = **the user is responsible (disclosed + audited); the platform does not hard-block** (section 8.5.0, track 1); automatic platform background data movement is still controlled (track 2). **The finding of the adversarial review that "the residency gate is soft against a malicious client" is therefore no longer a defect but a design choice** — phase one does not pursue a hard "refuse on overreach" guarantee. If a deployment really needs a hard guarantee, a **"hard residency" option** is offered (off by default) [🔴 withdrawn in revision 3.20: the "hard residency" option **is not built**, decision 6quinquies]: ① multi-account SCP regional deny (section 18, decision 5; requires Organizations; listed for phase two); ② or make the interactive gate fail-closed as well and switch to **the server-side authoritative criterion of DescribeStatement** (not trusting the client header; section 8.5.7 ①).
- 🔴 **Withdrawn (2026-09-05, decision 6quinquies: the platform has no legal gate and makes no legal judgment)** — original text: **legal prerequisite = cross-jurisdiction production go-live forbidden by default (decision B of 2026-08-28; a P1 of the later review)**: the platform, as a **data processor**, bears cross-border responsibility as **a fact, not something "the user clicking the disclosure can waive"** (section 8.5.0, item ④). Hence an env with `jurisdiction≠home` **does not open interactive querying to production by default**; it may be set active / interactive opened only when both ① `residency-api` is installed and initialized and ② **legal endorsement of a lawful mechanism is in place (adequacy decision / SCC / BCR)** (a pre-deployment gate; the specification, section 2.1; a deployment switch is kept). **The technical architecture is deliverable, but cross-jurisdiction production go-live is blocked by this legal prerequisite** — do not claim unconditional "deliverable" (sections 18 / 13 wording amended).
- For a deployment facing a **single jurisdiction** only, nothing in section 8.5 is enabled and the central set of services serves everything (zero regional backends) — the residency mode is **an optional capability gated by the env's `jurisdiction`**.
- If a **second tier, "digital sovereignty"** (even metadata access / app operation / operations staff must be inside the region), is ever needed, upgrade to "the front end is regionalized too" (one webapp host per region); Option 1 is a clean stepping stone to it (the data plane is already regionalized).

#### 8.5.7 Landing mechanisms for converging read-side egress (completed by the later deep review: the original section 8.5 named only one convergence point and missed several classes of central data pulls and the endpoint classification)

The deep review's grep established: the fail-closed of the original section 8.5 named only one entry, but **there are several central entries that touch results**, and **there is a whole class of paths where the center actively pulls business data into us-east-1** (not only "query dispatch"). Each is converged below. **All apply only across jurisdictions (`jurisdiction≠home`); home / same-jurisdiction behavior = today.**

**① The allow-list of central API entries that touch results (the scope of the fail-closed middleware)**: the unified front middleware (section 8.5.5 ①) intercepts, by `env.jurisdiction≠home`, **all** of the following entries (not only execute):
`redshift/execute` (SQL submission), **`redshift/result/[id]` (row fetch; goes only by statementId and is the easiest to miss, `route.ts:9-51`)**, `athena/*` (data-lake query / result fetch), `bi/query` and `bi/export` (dashboard data fetch / export), `data-api` execute, the data sub-calls of `genbi/ask`, **the 5 direct warehouse queries of section 8.1** (quality / metrics / modeling / sensitive-scan / catalog), and the paths in `observability` that read back run outputs / details. **Classified by the two tracks of section 8.5.0**: among the above, **those triggered by user interaction (execute / result / athena / bi / data-api / genbi interactive queries) = track 1**; across jurisdictions the central entry **sets the disclosure + audit marker and lets them through** (executing near the data through the regional data plane is recommended to move less data, section 8.5.7 ②, but it is not a fail-closed dependency); **those triggered by the platform in the background (scheduled / background calls of the 5 direct warehouse queries, automatic read-back in observability) = track 2**; across jurisdictions they **compute in the region and the center does not handle rows** (section 8.5.7 ③).

> 🔴 **The layer where the front wrapper lands + the region / jurisdiction decision + the missing-region branch (corrected by later reviews; per B downgraded to audit / routing, the hard gate only for the "hard residency" option)**:
> - ⚠️ **Downgraded per B (section 8.5.0)**: for **track-1 interactive queries this wrapper = disclosure banner + audit marker (no reject)**; `x-bgp-region` is **routing + an audit label**, not a hard-gate criterion. The hard decisions below are fail-closed only when **the deployment explicitly chooses "hard residency" (the option of section 8.5.6) or for track 2**.
> - **The landing layer = a shared wrapper function in the Node runtime, not the Next.js edge `middleware.ts`** — the edge middleware verifies JWTs with jose, **has no aws-sdk and cannot query `bgp-execution-envs`**; so the wrapper is built as **a shared function called on the first line of every allow-listed data route handler** (the logic is centralized; it is not physically "one edge middleware"), with **the lint guard rail pinning "a data route must call this wrapper (for audit; for the decision under hard residency)"**.
> - 🔴 **The decision reads `x-bgp-region` (not `x-bgp-env`! established by the later review)**: `x-bgp-env` is **the dev / staging / prod axis of the environment-management module** (`constants.ts:35`), whereas `bgp-execution-envs.sk = region` — looking up the region-keyed registry with a dev / prod value **always misses**. Hence an **independent, orthogonal `x-bgp-region` header** (the execution-region / jurisdiction axis, coexisting with `x-bgp-env`) must be introduced, attached by the front end when the execution region is switched; the wrapper uses it to look up `jurisdiction`. `x-bgp-region` currently has zero hits in the repository = to be added (consistent with [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 6.1).
> - **`redshift/result/[id]` row fetches also carry `x-bgp-region`** (the front end carries the session's execution region when fetching results), used for **routing to the right region + audit**; ⚠️ for something harder, switch to **the server-side authoritative criterion** (the `WorkgroupName` / `ClusterIdentifier` returned by `DescribeStatement` traced back to the region; statementIds are region-local; the later review) instead of trusting the client header — listed as the recommended practice for the hard-residency option.
> - **Track 1 without `x-bgp-region` = handled as the session's default region + an audit marker (no false kills)**; **under the hard-residency option, a missing region = fail-closed reject**; in a single-jurisdiction deployment section 8.5 is entirely off and requests pass as usual (avoiding the home-regression P0 finding).

**② The data / control endpoint classification table + the client-side "switch origin by region" router (correcting the landing mechanism of section 8.5.2's "browser-direct to the regional API")**: ⚠️ **Positioning adjusted per B (section 8.5.0)**: interactive query results are allowed to return to the center anyway (the user is responsible), so "browser-direct to the region" is downgraded from **a compliance hard requirement** to **a proximity-execution optimization + a smaller egress surface + keeping track 2 out of the center** — going to the regional origin is still recommended for cross-jurisdiction (less data moved, more accurate audit), but **it is not a fail-closed dependency**. 🔴 The deep review established that the fetch interceptor at `AuthProvider.tsx:56-76` **only adds headers and cannot change the origin** — "browser-direct to the regional API" must rely on the data client described below (not the interceptor). Landing = one **endpoint classification table** in the front end + one **data client that rewrites the base URL (origin) according to the current execution region**:

| Endpoint class | Routing | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Control class** (metadata / authentication / definitions / monitoring status / cost / orchestration) | Always the central `/api` | Task definitions, catalog structure, approvals, monitoring aggregates, cost dashboard |
| **Data class** (touches result rows / answers / details) | Cross-jurisdiction → browser-direct to the `data.<region>` regional API; same jurisdiction → the center | ① All allow-listed entries |
| **Mixed endpoints must be split** | Metadata to the center, data to the region | `genbi/ask` (orchestration metadata vs. data sub-calls), `monitor/runs` (status vs. output details), `catalog` (structure vs. fallback sampling) |

The data client (not the AuthProvider interceptor) decides the origin by `execEnv.jurisdiction`; the control client is unchanged. Mixed endpoints **are split into two branches, and the data branch does not pass through the center**: the metadata branch goes to the central `/api`; **the data branch is sent by the browser directly to the regional API (or delegated as a whole to the regional querying runtime to be completed inside the region)** — the center **does not fan out, does not relay, and does not handle result bytes** (sections 8.5.1 / 8.5.5 ②). ⚠️ **Corrected by the later review**: the earlier wording "split on the server into a control hop and a data hop" would have been implemented as "the central `/api` initiates the regional data sub-request and then assembles" = **the center handling result bytes = exactly the egress to be eliminated**; it has been deleted and rewritten.

> ⚠️ **The two landing deliverables of the origin router (a P1 of the later review; previously mentioned only in passing)**: ① **an inventory of front-end data-class call sites** — today the whole front end does `fetch("/api/…")` and relies on the interceptor; switching data-class calls to the regional origin requires **enumerating / migrating every data-class React call site one by one** to the new data client (in one-to-one correspondence with the allow-list of section 8.5.7 ①), listed as a deliverable of the consumption-side construction batch (having the server-side allow-list does not complete the front-end migration automatically); ② **the CORS / preflight policy of the regional API** — browser cross-origin direct calls to `data.<region>` need the regional side to allow the central front-end origin in CORS + pass preflight (regional JWKS-local JWT verification was already mentioned; CORS was not, and must be added).

**③ Residency handling of the classes where the center actively pulls data (completely missed by the original section 8.5, and section 8.1 gave the opposite requirement; the deep review's heaviest finding)**: the following are cases where **the central process actively pulls cross-jurisdiction business data back to us-east-1**, not "query dispatch", and must be converged separately:
- **Sensitive-data sampling** `governance/sensitive/scan.ts:72-101` **pulls 200 rows of real detail** into us-east-1 for PII detection → across jurisdictions, **run the sampling + detection in the regional data plane**; the center receives only {column name, matched type, count}, **never rows**.
- **Data-quality rule execution / metric materialization / data-modeling direct query** (quality / metrics / modeling in the table of section 8.1) → across jurisdictions the query **executes in the region**; the center receives only verdicts / aggregates (pass rate / row count / conclusion), no sample rows.
- **OM Profiler** `om-ingestion-stack.ts:462 computeMetrics:True` would compute min / max / distinct and other **column-value profiles of cross-jurisdiction tables and write them into the central OM** → ingestion for a cross-jurisdiction service **turns `computeMetrics` off** (collecting only schema / lineage, no column-value profiles), or the regional side computes them and returns only non-data-value statistics.
- 🔴 **Reconciling section 8.5 ↔ section 8.1**: section 8.1 lets these "hit the execution environment's warehouse" (valid for **same jurisdiction**); section 8.5.7 adds for **cross-jurisdiction** "execute in the regional data plane, the center receives no rows" — the two are routed by `jurisdiction` and no longer contradict (the forward caveat has been added at the end of section 8.1).

**④ SQL / answer / error source text is masked or kept in the region (preventing source text from entering the center through logs / responses)**:
- **SQL source text** may contain literal PII (WHERE values) → the center keeps only **masked SQL** (hooking into the existing `maskSqlLiterals` of `cookbook/mask.ts` — ⚠️ verified by the later review: it **masks string literals + comments with equal-length blanks and does not mask numeric literals**; the numeric PII in `WHERE ssn=123456` is not covered; hence numeric PII relies primarily on **staying in the region** rather than on masking) or omits it entirely; the source text stays in the regional `sql-history`.
- **Answer text / result rows** stay in the region (`genbi-history` / `turns` / `sql-history` → regional tables across jurisdictions; section 8.2 already corrected memory to stay in the region; SQL history and answers are added here).
- **error / observability**: error reports and the `question` / output details in observability contain no result data, or land in a regional sink (making the telemetry constraint of section 8.5.5 ③ concrete).
- ⚠️ **`MEMORY_BUCKET` defaults to the home data-lake bucket** (S3 summaries of self-managed memory) → a cross-jurisdiction region **must override it per region with a regional bucket**, otherwise memory summaries are written back to us-east-1.

**⑤ [🔴 withdrawn in revision 3.20 (decision 6quinquies): no read-only guard, no ban on UNLOAD / datashare — the customer manages it, the platform audits]** original text: a read-only guard on `redshift/execute` + a ban on cross-jurisdiction data-movement statements: execute on a cross-jurisdiction data plane gains a **read-only guard** (only SELECT / EXPLAIN-class statements allowed) and **bans `UNLOAD` / `CREATE EXTERNAL SCHEMA` / `datashare`** and other statements that move data out of the region or build cross-region channels — otherwise a service-level action bypasses the front-end convergence and exports data across regions.

**⑥ Cross-region replication (CRR) turned off on regional result / data buckets**: the regional result bucket / export bucket / memory bucket **disable S3 Cross-Region Replication**, and the bucket policy explicitly denies cross-region replication destinations, preventing "data landed in the right region and then a replication rule moved it back to us-east-1".

**⑦ The agentcore → in-process fallback must stay inside the region**: section 8.2's "fall back to in-process when the agentcore component is not installed" **may only fall back to the regional data plane's in-process runtime** (regional Lambda / Fargate) in a cross-jurisdiction region, **never to the central us-east-1 in-process runtime** (otherwise the fallback path itself pulls querying back to us-east-1). If a cross-jurisdiction region has neither agentcore nor a regional in-process runtime → the querying entry is handled as capability unavailable; it does not silently fall back to the center.

**⑧ Data-class responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store`**: all data-class responses forbid CloudFront / browser caching (landing section 8.5.5 ⑤), preventing results from being cached at edge nodes outside the region.

**⑨ Two landing points not yet made concrete (the later review; listed as design deliverables of the consumption-side construction batch, so that "scheduled after this design, but the joint design is empty" does not happen)**:
- **Deliverable 1: supply and lifecycle of per-region × per-project data-plane credentials**: section 15 of this design gives only the cache **key** `(targetId, project, purpose)`, and the project-isolation design's section 7bis L3 is home-shaped, but project P's `proj_P` schema + DB role + regional Secrets Manager secret **must exist in every region** — **when / by whom they are created and how the regional runtime obtains them** appear in none of the three drafts. To be added: the **creation moment** of the proj role / secret in non-home regions (recommended: with the deployment of that region's `redshift` component + an ensure step when the project first lands data in that region), their **storage** (regional Secrets Manager), and the regional runtime's **resolution path**; the public→proj_ migration of the project-isolation design, section 7bis.4, **runs once per region**.
- **Deliverable 2: re-homing the asynchronous querying machinery to the region**: today `genbi/tasks/[taskId]` GET is the only driving entry, the central `driveExecution` runs the agent loop, `markSucceeded` writes answer / sql / rows into the **central** genbi-tasks, and the front end polls the center; `ExecuteGenBiParams` / the agentcore payload / the genbi-tasks row **have no region / target dimension** (description of the current state: today neither has one). Section 8.5.4's "querying stays in the region throughout" (track-2 side: answers / memory land in the region) requires the **task driver + result table + front-end polling point** to be re-homed as a whole into residency-api, with the center keeping only `{status, category, model}` — a separate sequence design is needed (regional task table + regional driver + front end polling the region), and **`targetId`** must be threaded end to end into ExecuteGenBiParams / payload / the genbi-tasks row. (Note: the user taking back the answer under track 1 is within the user's responsibility; this item is the track-2 landing of "platform persistence / driving does not land in the center".)

> Interaction (switching region / the neutral 🌍 label / the three-path UX / the disclaimer dialog; the former "disclosure banner / informed confirmation" was withdrawn in revision 3.20) is in [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 4bis; implementation lands with the consumption-side construction batch (regionalizing querying / BI / Data Service); the `residency-api` component sits in the same frame as the optional components of section 4.6.

## 9. Parallel dispatch strategy (D8; the question asked was "is parallel dispatch to multiple regions allowed?")

| Layer | Design |
|---|---|
| **The dispatch action** (publishing DAGs / scripts) | **Serial region by region + fail-fast** (dispatch itself takes seconds, so serial costs no performance; stopping at the first failure prevents a "half-dispatched" state from spreading); a failure shows as a red cell on the version dashboard and can be redispatched for a single region |
| **Task runs** | Naturally **parallel per target** (each target's MWAA / engines schedule and run independently), with no waiting on each other and no cross-target orchestration / distributed transactions. 🔴 **Revision 3.0 premise**: parallelism **requires the lock identity to include `targetId` as a necessary condition** (section 6.3; otherwise cross-target contention for the same lock → ETL silently skips the whole execution); invariant = **same target + same task mutually exclusive / different target + same task parallel**. **Cross-target dependency edges** (a node of A depending on a node of B) are still **not built in phase one**; the "fan-out strategy" of section 19.6 refers to **which targets a downstream multi-target twin should trigger** — a different matter from "cross-target dependency edges", **no conflict** (removing the wording contradiction noted by the independent architecture review) |
| **Querying** | Decided: single-region, serial; one question hits one region at a time; no multi-region parallel querying / result merging |
| **Backfill** | A backfill plan is anchored to a single region (pick definition + region); cross-region bulk backfill = create a plan per region |

---

Legacy records are interpreted as `tgt_home` / `proj-default`.

## 11. API and front-end changes (skeleton)

> 🎨 **The front-end interaction design has been deepened separately into its own document, [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md)** (emphasized on 2026-08-26: "interaction soundness / convenience / safety must be considered, otherwise the feature is meaningless") — it covers 4 scenarios (the full lifecycle of execution environments / multi-region binding and push-down of tasks / a unified multi-region monitoring dashboard / switching region in Ask-Your-Data) × 4 safety mechanisms (second confirmation / impact preview / tiered approval by permission level / visual isolation of the danger zone), all anchored on existing UI conventions (the header environment switcher / PromoteButton approval / Steps wizard / monitor filters / settings probes). This section lists only the API / front-end skeleton; interaction details are governed by that document.

- **New**: `/api/admin/exec-envs` (list / get / register / probe / enable / delete-request) + `/api/admin/exec-envs/[id]/{deploy,add-components,canary,build-status}` (deploy / add-components = the platform's `StartBuild`; build-status = polls `BatchGetBuilds` and returns progress); approval goes through the existing `/api/approvals`;
- **Changed**: the publish / schedule routes of the 5 resource types accept **`targets?: string[]`** (default = the definition's `targets`), start accepts a single-valued **`targetId`** (🔴 a missing parameter must not default to `tgt_home`, [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 2.3); genbi ask / tasks, bi query and data-api execute accept **`targetId`** (the region is looked up from the target; `envId` is no longer accepted); the monitor / finops / governance list endpoints gain a region filter parameter;
- **Front end**: a new "Execution Environments" administration page (in the platform-administration menu group; list / wizard / detail probes / delete approval) + the "Multi-Region Dashboard" (version matrix, section 5.3); resource editors gain a region multi-select (default home; only active environments shown); a region selector on the Ask-Your-Data page; region as the first-level filter on the cost / monitoring / governance pages; i18n entries.

## 12. IAM / security / network

### 12.0 🔴 **Deployment-privilege isolation = minimizing the "resident attack surface" (the security cornerstone of fully automated deployment by the platform; wording corrected by the first review round)**: the platform ECS TaskRole gains **only** `codebuild:StartBuild` + `BatchGetBuilds` + reading that CodeBuild project's logs + region-B `cloudformation:Describe*` (progress read-back, section 12.6), with the `StartBuild` resource restricted to **the ARN of the dedicated exec-env CodeBuild project**. Strong deployment power (CloudFormation / `iam:PassRole` / creating buckets, Redshift, MSK, MWAA, VPCs) is welded onto the **CodeBuild service role** and can only be used by CodeBuild running the predefined buildspec.
   - ⚠️ **Honest security positioning (not overstated; wording decided on 2026-08-27)**: what CodeBuild isolation **really buys is "minimizing the resident attack surface" — the resident platform ECS role no longer holds strong deployment power (only StartBuild)**; even if the platform web process is compromised, it can only trigger the predefined pipeline. But **it does not mean "no strong power exists in the account"**: the `cfn-exec-role` of the default CDK bootstrap carries AdministratorAccess by default, the actual deployment identity is admin-equivalent, the strong power remains and is held indirectly by "whoever can StartBuild or can modify the buildspec / CodeBuild project". **We do not claim "least privilege on the deployment path"**. The three fallback measures: StartBuild-only + **the right to modify the buildspec / project is protected at the same level as deployment rights** (modifying the buildspec = privilege escalation in disguise) + full audit. Whether to use `--cloudformation-execution-policies` to narrow cfn-exec-role from Admin to an exact policy = a decision to take before construction starts (narrowing is safer but requires enumerating exactly the CloudFormation permissions of every exec-env resource — a large effort, easy to miss something; not narrowing means accepting admin-equivalent and relying on the fallbacks above).
     - 🔴 **"Same-level protection" must be made concrete and cannot stay a slogan (a threat identified in the threat-modeling review)**: ① **the buildspec storage location is locked down** — the buildspec travels with the control-plane IaC in git (not in S3 where it could be changed at runtime); changing it = changing code = PR review + deployment process, not a runtime overwrite; the CodeBuild project definition (including source / env) is managed only by the control-plane stack, and the `codebuild:UpdateProject` permission is confined to the same level as deployment (only the operations deployment identity; the platform ECS has no such permission); ② **any change to the CodeBuild project / buildspec enters the audit** (CloudTrail management events UpdateProject / StartBuild + the deployment commit leaves a trace); ③ the platform ECS role has **only StartBuild + BatchGetBuilds**, never UpdateProject / UpdateProjectVisibility. Only the three together bring the path "modify buildspec = escalate privilege" under control.
   - **P0-D: fixed-name IAM roles across regions**: the fixed-name roles that exec-env must create — `bgp-glue-role` (platform-stack.ts:88) / `bgp-emr-serverless-role` (emr-stack.ts:39) / `bgp-flink-role` (flink-stack.ts:26) / `bgp-redshift-copy-role` (redshift-stack.ts:35) / `bgp-dms-kinesis-role` (realtime-stack.ts:73) — are **global resources**; a second-region stack creating the same name again necessarily hits `AlreadyExists`. The fix: IAM roles are created once, in home only; the exec-env stack group references the existing roles with `Role.fromRoleName`, and role trust / permissions are extended for multiple regions (same for the region list of section 12.1).
   - The CodeBuild project itself is deployed once by the control-plane stack (at home installation time); the `buildspec` is fixed and the inbound parameters are only `targetId` / `components`, validated against an allow-list.
   - 🔴 **Revision 3.10 landed shape (implemented; the synth gate asserts it item by item)**: a new control-plane stack **`BgpExecEnvDeployStack`** (single copy in home) = CodeBuild project `bgp-exec-env-deploy` + narrow-privilege broker `bgp-exec-env-broker` + source-package asset.
     · **The platform ECS TaskRole has only three statements**: `lambda:InvokeFunction` (the broker; ARN assembled by name, no cross-stack reference), `codebuild:BatchGetBuilds` (restricted to that project), `cloudformation:DescribeStacks/DescribeStackEvents` (⚠️ currently `Resource:*` — the target region is only known at run time and cannot be enumerated in IaC; narrowing to `stack/BgpExecEnv-*` belongs to **slice f**). **No** `codebuild:StartBuild`, **no** `codebuild:UpdateProject`.
     · **The broker's `StartBuild` resource is restricted to that one project**; the broker accepts only `{targetId, components[], operationId}` and **rejects any extra field** (rather than ignoring it — ignoring would let the caller believe an override took effect); **`region` / `accountId` are always read from the registry**, never taken from the input (otherwise the caller could redirect the deployment to another region / account); **never forwards** `buildspecOverride` / `imageOverride` / `serviceRoleOverride` / `sourceLocationOverride` and so on.
     · 🔴 **The deploy lock is taken inside the broker, not in the platform API** (section 12.2): if the lock lived on the caller's side, anyone able to invoke the broker directly could bypass it; the lock and `lockEpoch++` are completed in **the same conditional write** (a gap between two steps could be raced).
     · **Fix ① of section 12.0 (explicit IAM Deny on override condition keys) is not done**: the exact key names must be verified against the official AWS CodeBuild IAM documentation (belongs to **slice g**, before real deployment); but fix ② (the platform does not hold StartBuild) already **closes this vector completely** for the platform role; ① is additional depth.
   - 🔴 **Revision 3.0 override blocking (confirmed by the independent architecture review — the original "StartBuild only = can only run the fixed buildspec" was falsified by the facts of the AWS API)**: the `StartBuild` API **natively carries** `buildspecOverride` (**inline plaintext is possible**), `sourceLocationOverride` / `sourceTypeOverride` / `sourceVersion`, `environmentVariablesOverride`, `imageOverride`, `privilegedModeOverride`, `serviceRoleOverride`, `computeType/environmentTypeOverride`; the official AWS documentation **carries its own security warning** (a principal able to call this API and set overrides can override the project's default settings). ⇒ **An application-layer parameter allow-list cannot stop an attacker who calls the AWS API directly**: with the platform ECS credentials one can inline a malicious buildspec and use CodeBuild's **admin-equivalent service role** to run any deployment. Section 12.0 previously blocked only `UpdateProject` (changing the project definition) and **missed the override vector** — a real gap in the security cornerstone of this design.
     - **Two fixes (both recommended)**: ① **explicit IAM deny of all overrides** — on the platform role's `codebuild:StartBuild` statement, `Deny` using CodeBuild's override-related condition keys (⚠️ **the exact condition key names follow the official AWS CodeBuild IAM documentation and must be verified before construction starts**; the design does not hard-code key names from memory); ② 🔴 **more robust: the platform does not hold `StartBuild` directly** — it calls a **narrow-privilege broker** instead (Lambda / Step Functions, which alone holds StartBuild): the broker **accepts only the allow-listed input `{targetId, components[], operationId}`**, **takes the commit from a signed release manifest**, and **forwards no override**; the platform ECS has only `lambda:InvokeFunction` on that broker.
     - **Audit**: buildspec hash + commit + inputs + initiator enter a **tamper-proof** audit (same framework as the audit residency of section 19.4).
     - **Negative acceptance**: with the platform identity, attempt an inline `buildspecOverride` / `sourceLocationOverride` / `imageOverride` / `serviceRoleOverride` → **AWS must reject directly**; P0-6 in section 16 is **upgraded from "static permission check only" to an executable negative probe**.

### 12.1 `aws:RequestedRegion` condition 🔴 **Revision 3.13 measured correction: 18 occurrences** (platform-stack **15** + emr-stack 1 + netadvisor-iam 1 + paramadvisor-iam 1; the earlier "19 (platform 16)" differed from the measurement by 1 — as with the naming-SSOT sub-batch precedent, counts follow the measurement): changed from a single region to a **region list**, injected through CDK context 🔴 **revision 3.13 correction: `-c execution-regions=us-east-1,us-west-2` (full region names; abbreviations are not accepted)** — the reverse mapping abbreviation (usw2) → full name would require maintaining an enumeration table = the next source of drift, and `regionAbbrev` is one-way, lossy compression. ✅ **Slice f has landed**: the single exit `infra/lib/shared/execution-regions.ts#requestedRegionCondition()`; all 18 occurrences go through it; **by default (no context passed) it returns a bare string ⇒ existing templates are byte-for-byte zero-diff** (confirmed with a baseline diff taken before the change); only when passed and >1 does it become an array (multi-valued IAM StringEquals = OR). 🔴 **The way "update the control-plane region condition" is executed has converged** (the original "done by CodeBuild as part of the same build" **collides** with the deployment-privilege isolation of section 12.0: putting it into the exec-env buildspec = giving the deployment role write access to control-plane stacks, and the gate of slice a already asserts that the buildspec never touches the control plane) ⇒ **operations run one control-plane redeploy in a maintenance window** (with `-c execution-regions=…`; side effect = one rolling restart of the control-plane ECS, stated below in this section); not relaxed to `*` (least privilege is kept).
   - ⚠️ **The order "change IAM first vs. create the environment first" (still self-consistent under the CodeBuild approach)**: the two kinds of principal have different permissions — ① the one creating region-B resources is the **CodeBuild deployment role** (an isolated strong-power role, not bound by the platform's `aws:RequestedRegion`); ② the one bound by that condition is the **platform runtime role** (ECS TaskRole / MWAA execution role), and all platform access to region B (probe / canary / push-down) happens after the IAM update. The correct order = **CodeBuild creates resources + updates the platform region condition (within the same build) → platform probe / canary / push-down**. To be pinned at construction start: in the buildspec, "update the platform IAM region condition" completes before "write back the registry to allow platform probing".
   - ⚠️ **Side effect (missed in the original draft; must be stated honestly)**: updating the `aws:RequestedRegion` condition = **redeploying the control-plane stack → the control-plane ECS of all existing regions rolls once**. That is, "enabling a new region" makes **the running control plane** go through a minute-scale rolling deployment (blue/green or rolling; the front end may flicker briefly). Mitigation: enabling / deleting an execution environment is a low-frequency operations task scheduled in a maintenance window; the documentation states this impact. Scheduling already running on the execution side is not affected (MWAA runs independently).
   - 🆕 **Revision 3.24 (candidate-region pre-authorization, released 2026-10-27; driven by the record of adding a Frankfurt region in a customer production environment on 2026-10-19) — authorization goes from "once per added region" to "zero times"**. The two points above are unchanged (authorization can still only be completed by a control-plane redeploy; the isolation of section 12.0 does not change by one word); what changes is that **the step "a human decides which regions the platform may touch" moves forward to installation / upgrade**: the deployment scripts accept `EXEC_REGION_CANDIDATES` (a list of full region names | `all-enabled` | `none`), validated by the single author `scripts/lib/execution-regions.sh` (must be regions enabled in the account; no abbreviations; `*` not accepted) and persisted to SSM `/bgp/wiring/execution-region-candidates`; **the allow-list = home ∪ regions in use in the registry ∪ candidate regions**, entering `requestedRegionCondition()` **through one and the same parser** by two channels: `-c execution-regions` (explicit for the platform stack) and the process variable `BGP_EXECUTION_REGIONS` (for **every** cdk deploy in the same deployment run — the three agent stacks / the EMR stack had never received the list before, so the skill roles of the network / parameter advisors had always authorized only the home region; a defect fixed with this change) (synth-gate section 9c-env pins both channels to byte-identical templates with `-c` taking precedence). Creating an execution region from the page inside a candidate region: once the build completes, directly "Check initialization" (the region's S3 Tables federated catalog is enabled idempotently by the probe item `lake:s3tablescatalog`, no longer depending on step 3c of the redeploy) → "Enable", **zero CLI**; when choosing the region in the wizard, each region is marked "authorized, usable right after the build" / "operations must authorize once after the build". **Not relaxed**: candidate regions are concrete region names named by operations, still not `*`; environments that declare no candidates behave byte-for-byte identically. **The cost is stated honestly**: candidate regions = power granted but not yet used (the platform TaskRole can call data-plane APIs in those regions while no execution environment exists there yet) — application-layer place validation remains the first gate, and the IAM region condition goes from "only installed regions" to "installed regions + candidate regions named by operations". Recorded in section 20.29.
2. **MWAA execution role (execution region)**: authorized to write the control-plane region's bgp-workflows / bgp-task-runs / bgp-etl-jobs / bgp-engine-jobs / bgp-sync-tasks table ARNs (explicit cross-region ARNs); the execution region's skill Lambda is authorized to read the control plane's genbi-context.
3. **Data-source reachability**: pre-validation when a task is bound to a region (the network advisor's probing is extended per region, following the PARAM_ADVISOR_REGION precedent); the platform **does not** build cross-region VPC connectivity (peering / TGW is the customer's network engineering; the documentation states the boundary).
4. WAF / CloudFront / Cognito stay anchored in home; the CloudFront prefix-list mapping (only use1 / use2) relates to the control plane, not to execution environments, and is not extended.
5. Audit: all lifecycle operations on environments + push-down actions enter bgp-audit (with region).

### 12.2 Deployment-level concurrency lock (P0-C; first review round: the original proposal had zero control over build concurrency)

The hard-won lesson "cdk cannot run concurrently" refers to **multiple processes stepping on the same stack**. In this design, add-components passes the full set and re-touches the core stack; colliding on the same stack with the initialization build reproduces exactly that (`already has operation in progress`); concurrent `cdk bootstrap` in the same region races on the asset bucket / SSM parameters. Section 9 only discussed data-plane / push-down parallelism and had **zero control over deployment build concurrency**.
- **Added design**: reuse the platform's existing `run-lock.ts` (DynamoDB conditional write + lease; an established precedent in the code base) as an **environment-level deploy lock**: take `deploy-lock#<targetId>` before `StartBuild`; while the lock is held, **reject / queue** any deploy / add-components / destroy for that region; builds in different regions do not block each other (different stacks, no CloudFormation conflict). This is P0.

### 12.3 CodeBuild cross-region deployment mechanism (companion to P0-B)

- Same account, cross region needs no cross-account trust (the CodeBuild service role's IAM is global and can naturally operate on region B).
- `cdk deploy` actually creates resources by assuming region B's bootstrap execution role `cdk-hnb659fds-cfn-exec-role-<acct>-<region>` → the CodeBuild service role must be able to `sts:AssumeRole` region B's bootstrap roles, and region B must be **bootstrapped first**. The buildspec order is pinned: `cdk bootstrap aws://<acct>/<region-B>` → `cdk deploy`.

### 12.4 Progress read-back mechanism (P0-F; first review round: BatchGetBuilds cannot give per-stack progress)

- AWS fact: `cdk deploy` runs entirely inside a **single BUILD phase** (the MWAA stack alone ~30 min); `BatchGetBuilds.phases[]` only reports "BUILD in progress" — **it cannot give the per-stack progress `VPC✓ / Redshift⟳ / MWAA⏳` drawn in the wireframe of section 1.2**.
- **Added design (final)**: the data source for per-stack progress = the platform **separately polls region B's CloudFormation `DescribeStacks/DescribeStackEvents`** (more stable than scraping build logs) and renders per-stack state from it; the CodeBuild phase serves only as the coarse "build as a whole running / succeeded / failed". **IAM cost**: the platform role must gain region-B `cloudformation:DescribeStacks/DescribeStackEvents` (already merged into section 12.0).

### 12.5 Reuse of the buildspec self-heal + source acquisition (P0-G + P0-H)

- **P0-G**: the buildspec must build in the preflight self-heal of `deploy-all.sh`, otherwise two pitfalls that every fresh environment hits reappear: `npm ci` in both directories (a fresh clone needs `npm ci`) and the self-heal for the CDK empty-zip asset bug (it reproduces every time), plus bootstrap. **Approach = extract `ensure_deps` / `heal_cdk_empty_assets` into a shared sourced script** that both deploy-all.sh and the buildspec source (following the established precedent of extracting shared functions to eliminate copies; copying is forbidden to prevent drift). ⚠️ deploy-all.sh is the full 21-stack / home-only deployment and **cannot be reused as a whole** to run "deploy only the region-X stack group"; the stack-group target must be written anew, but the preflight helpers are shared.
- **P0-H source acquisition** 🔴 **Revision 3.10 final (decided on 2026-09-01: CDK asset source package)**: at control-plane deployment the repository is packed into one **content-hashed, immutable CDK asset** (S3); the CodeBuild source points at it and the buildspec goes through `fromSourceFilename`. Why this rather than a GitHub source: **zero external credentials** (a temporary account need not first create a CodeStar Connection and authorize on GitHub), **verifiable offline** (the synth gate can assert the source-package content in a credential-less environment), and "the same commit" is **naturally locked at deployment time** (a different commit = a different hash = a different object; nobody has to remember the commit). ⚠️ Cost: every control-plane deployment re-uploads the source package once (measured ~9 MB; **the exclude list must name bare directory names** — writing only `dir/**` does not prune; when this was missed the staged asset reached **509 MB**, and `cdk.out*` must be excluded with a wildcard, otherwise the source package **packs its own output directory into itself level after level** and paths deepen without bound). The buildspec builds from **the same commit / tag the control plane is running**, eliminating infra / product drift; **`npm ci` is mandatory (respecting the exact package-lock) and `npm install` is never used** (`infra/package.json` has a `^2.170.0` caret; `npm install` would pull a newer CDK → an unexpected changeset in the synth diff of existing stacks).

### 12.6 The truth about add-components idempotency (P0-E; first review round: the claim "add only, never modify" was too strong)

CDK / CloudFormation no-ops only for stacks whose **template bytes are unchanged**. If add-components builds from the latest source while the control-plane infra has evolved, redeploying produces a changeset for the existing core stack and may `UPDATE/REPLACE` stateful resources (replacing the Redshift namespace / MWAA environment = downtime / data risk). Hence section 4.6's "pass the full set, CDK is idempotent, add only never modify" **does not hold under source drift**.
- **Added design**: add-components **anchors to the source commit / templates used when the environment was created** (same source as P0-H; the commit is stored in the registry), only layers on the new component stacks and replays the other stacks with the original templates; for the stateful core stacks (Redshift / MWAA) add a **changeset approval gate**, forbidding unattended replace.

### 12.7 Registry write-back tolerates partial success (the seventh item of the P0 review)

`BatchGetBuilds` does not return CloudFormation outputs; the `dynamodb update-item` at the end of the buildspec runs only if the build succeeds → after a **partial success followed by failure** (VPC / buckets created, MWAA failed) the registry is stale while the resources exist.
- **Added design**: the **probe / DescribeStacks of step 4 in section 4.1, which inspects the real resources, is the authoritative single source of truth**; write-back is downgraded to a hint; combined with reconcile-on-read (the platform's existing fallback pattern) to tolerate partial writes.

## 13. Necessity assessment (requested; honest conclusions)

| Dimension | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Reality of the customer pain point | **Real and typical** (data sovereignty / compute near the data for multinational enterprises is a common need; the pain = the management cost and drift risk of N environments; a mainstream commercial data platform likewise sells "one console, multi-region workspaces" as an enterprise-edition feature) |
| Existing alternatives | Option A (N independent deployments) is feasible today but is exactly the pain point; option C (consistency inspection) only treats the symptom (section 1.2) |
| Engineering effort | **Platform-wide refactoring**: 112 region fallbacks / 117 singletons / artifact baking / FQN / AgentCore topology / IAM … the largest single module of this platform so far. But the refactoring is highly **converged**: one registry (section 2) + one naming SSOT (section 3) + 4 atomic functions (section 8.1) + the runtime-lib split (section 6.3) cover most of the surface |
| Risk / benefit ratio | **The foundation batch (lossless refactor) is zero-risk, high-benefit** (a pure lossless refactor; even if nothing follows, it eliminates the **30 lines / 23 files** of fallback literals (revision 3.8 re-measured figure, see section 3.1) / absorbs naming / completes the task-runs dimensions); the real resource-investment decision point comes after the second-region batch (actually enabling a second region), so investment can be staged and cut |
| **Conclusion** | **Recommended, but strictly staged**: first the lossless-refactor batch (serving the model-configuration and project-isolation designs along the way), then the second-region batch to close the minimal loop and demonstrate it to the customer for feedback, then decide on the full investment of the lifecycle / push-down and consumption-side batches |

## 14. Capability boundaries not supported in phase one (honest statement)

> The risk register has been consolidated into **section 14bis.3** (the production-grade P0 / P1 / P2 table); this section keeps only the **capability-boundary statements** of "explicitly not done in phase one" (not risks but scoping, to be made explicit to the customer to avoid over-promising):

- **Cross-region task dependencies**: a node in region A depending on a node in region B = not supported in phase one (section 9).
- ~~**Multiple execution environments in one region**: at most one environment per region in phase one~~ → 🔴 **Revision 3.0 withdraws this restriction (final per [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.5, Q-2)**: **multiple targets in the same region are allowed** (blue/green / active-standby / old and new coexisting during a migration), because that is exactly why the immutable `targetId` exists; a hard rule "one target per region" would make a future blue/green **a second metadata migration** (an order of magnitude more expensive). Companions: the FQN segment / OM service name / audit shard key / model-configuration key / naming SSOT are **all derived from `targetId` (not region)**; the wizard defaults to "one per region"; multiple targets in one region is advanced usage.
- **Cross-region data replication**: the platform does not do it; that is the business tasks' job (section 1.1).
- **JDBC sources synchronized across regions**: Glue Connections are region-level and are not recreated in the target region; in phase one JDBC sources can execute only in home; DynamoDB / S3 / Kinesis / MSK sources are not restricted (section 6.4).
