- **The model call of cross-jurisdiction Ask-Your-Data does not stay resident (an accepted gap in phase one)**: in phase one models use `global.*` profiles; **the model-call hop** of cross-jurisdiction Ask-Your-Data / Copilot is routed globally (the pure SQL / Athena / BI data path achieves residency; this hop is excluded); phase one **accepts it and does not gate it off**; phase two closes it with region pinning (sections 8.5.4 / 8.5.6; D9 of the [model-configuration design](./model-configuration.md) → its region-pinned-model phase).
- **No global unified SQL history / cross-region Ask-Your-Data memory**: cross-jurisdiction SQL history and Ask-Your-Data memory stay in their region (section 8.5.6); the center keeps only a metadata-level index — **no unified history view aggregated across regions** (accepted; section 18, item 6).
- **Multi-region disaster recovery of Cognito / the entry point**: the control plane remains single-region — if the control plane is down, platform administration is unavailable, but scheduling already running on the execution side is unaffected (MWAA runs independently). This single-point boundary is in section 14bis.2.
  - 🟠 **DR hook (a P1 item of the architecture review round; 🔴 revision 3.0 corrected per the independent architecture review — the original "state is almost entirely in DDB + IaC" was inaccurate)**: control-plane state is **not only DDB**; there are also the Cognito user pool, OpenMetadata RDS MySQL, OpenSearch, Secrets, AgentCore Memory, and audit / deployment state (section 14bis.2). DDB can use Global Tables, but **every other state domain needs its own replication / recovery solution**, and Global Tables **do not provide** the single-writer semantics of the current distributed lock (without epoch / fencing there would be **two primaries**). Hence **the statement "phase-two cold standby of the control plane is affordable" is withdrawn**; this design positions itself as **a federation of regional execution and does not promise global HA / DR** (section 20.5); a customer who wants DR must **run a separate project** (replication point / RPO·RTO / stable entry point / fencing / regular drills). ⚠️ **Honest record (removes a self-contradiction within one sentence flagged by the external review)**: control-plane state is **not only DDB + IaC** — besides the Cognito / OM RDS / OpenSearch / Secrets / AgentCore Memory listed above there is scattered runtime state (such as the SSM wiring, `bin/app.ts:38-77`). This entry therefore **no longer records "all in DDB + IaC, cold standby across regions in phase two"** (that statement was withdrawn at the start of this bullet); the correct record = **"control-plane state is spread over ≥6 state domains, DR needs a per-domain solution, this design makes no promise"** (section 20.5).

**🟠 Two low-severity architecture debts recorded by the architecture review round (acceptable in phase one; must be declared explicitly so that phase two is not locked in):**
- **Debt two (the evolution ceiling of C2 schema-level isolation)**: the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 7bis L2, uses `proj_<id>` schemas inside **the same workgroup / database**. If phase two wants to move up to "an independent Redshift namespace per project" (true physical isolation / project-level cost attribution / region × project combinations), that is **a second data migration** (tables rebuilt in the new namespace; phase one already did public → proj_ once). **Statement: C2 stops at schema level in phase one; if phase two wants namespace level, the `proj_<id>` naming carries over but a second migration is needed** — made explicit in the design so that phase two does not discover the lock-in by surprise.
- **Debt three (the home exemption spread across 4 kinds of identifier)**: the `isHome ? old name : derived name` branch spreads across bucket names (section 3.2), FQNs (section 7.2), OM service names and dagId (section 5.2, home carries no suffix), and home cannot be deleted (section 4.4). Doing this for zero migration of existing deployments is right, but **the home exemption must converge into one authoritative decision function in the naming SSOT** (`isHome(env)`) rather than scattered ternaries — otherwise it is a replica of the 15 `bgp-workgroup` fallbacks (the convergence point must be enforced; the same applies to the A7 lint guardrail and section 6.1).
- **Model availability differs per region**: not unsupported, but the D5 prober of the model-configuration design must probe per region and the environment test suite must include a Bedrock probe fallback (section 4.2).

## 14bis. Change scope · impact scope · risk register (🌐 production-grade change review)

> This is **the largest single module of this platform so far**; it touches the strong assumption "one account × one region × one assembly" that runs through the whole platform. On a global-scale production system every step must satisfy: **the home environment stays byte-for-byte unchanged (zero regression for existing deployments), zero loss of customer data, an honest boundary for the control-plane single point of failure**. This section gathers the refactoring points scattered through sections 2–13 into a reviewable change scope / blast radius / risk register, **all anchored on the real current state of the code** (calibrated by the audit of 2026-08-26: the audit independently measured infra = 21 stacks and bgp-settings never implemented, consistent with this document).

### 14bis.1 Change-scope summary table (refers to the detail sections; this table only summarizes size and the nature of new / changed items)

| Domain | New | Changes to existing code (regression surface) | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| Registry | `bgp-execution-envs` table + `lib/exec-env/registry.ts` | 🔴 **Revision 3.7 correction**: the home descriptor is **seeded at deployment time** (the read path is read-only and fails closed when the descriptor is missing; the original "auto-register (read-only fallback)" is retired) | section 2 |
| Naming SSOT | `platform/naming.json` + re-export on both sides | Absorbs the **30 lines / 23 files** of `bgp-workgroup` fallback literals (revision 3.8 re-measurement; **belongs to the lossless-refactor batch**; its naming sub-batch delivers only the SSOT itself) | section 3 |
| Lifecycle | exec-env CodeBuild project (isolated deployment role) + buildspec + administration page + `/api/admin/exec-envs` (StartBuild / progress / supplementary deployment) | bgp-approvals type extended; the platform gains codebuild:StartBuild | sections 4 / 12.0 |
| Cross-cutting clients | `lib/aws/clients.ts` factory | Only the chains involved in multi-region change (**not** all 117 singletons) | section 6.1 |
| Artifact generation | — | the signatures of generateGlueScript / generateAirflowDag (V2) / ETL / EMR DAG gain env | section 6.2 |
| **runtime-lib dual region** | — | `_mk` → `_mk_exec` / `_mk_ctrl` (🔴 the most critical single point, 7 direct writes) | section 6.3 |
| task-runs dimensions | — | **13 row creations + 14 write-backs** gain `{region,projectId}` (same batch as the project-isolation design) | section 7.1 |
| FQN / OM region | — | lineage / quality / classification / grants: the authoritative fqn function injects region | section 7.2 |
| 4 atomic functions | — | gain an `env?` parameter | section 8.1 |
| **5 warehouse direct-query paths that bypass them** | — | quality/service:29, metrics/service:21, modeling/redshift:18, sensitive/scan:37, governance/catalog:79 each build a region client | section 8.1 correction |
| Consumption side | — | Ask-Your-Data / BI / FinOps / monitoring / governance gain the region dimension (the data service was retired 2026-09-05) | section 8 |
| IAM | — | `aws:RequestedRegion` 19 occurrences changed to a region list + netadvisor / paramadvisor 1 each | section 12 |

### 14bis.2 Impact scope / blast radius (based on the verified current code)

- **The runtime-lib dual-region split = the highest-risk single point**: today `_mk(spec)` feeds one `spec.region` to all three clients glue / redshift-data / dynamodb (runtime-lib.ts:104-114). After the split, DDB must **point fixedly at the control plane**, glue / redshift at the execution region. This is also the byte-alignment surface of the **cross-executor job lock** (Node run-lock.ts / runtime-lib / backfill execute.ts:90 — three parties writing the same table and the same attributes) — a wrong split = the lock fails = backfill DROP+CTAS collides on OIDs (the earlier incident of that kind). Requires a controlRegion unit test + headless cross-executor serialization verification.
- **The 13 task-runs write points = the largest single point shared with the project-isolation design**: see the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 8bis.2; both designs change `{projectId,region}` in one pass, including the 4 cross-executor direct writes of the MWAA bypass.
- **"4 atomic functions = full coverage" is an overestimate (corrected by the first review round)**: another 5 warehouse direct-query paths each build a region singleton and bypass the 4 functions; section 6.1's "the remaining singletons hit only the control plane, behavior unchanged" **does not hold** for these 5 (they hit the execution-environment warehouse). If missed, modeling / metrics / quality / sensitive sampling in region B cannot run.
- **JDBC source cross-region gap (a finding of the first review round)**: a Glue Connection is a region-level resource created only in home; in phase one JDBC sources execute only in home; DynamoDB / S3 / Kinesis / MSK are unrestricted. The acceptance of the second-region batch must not use a MySQL source.
- **Control-plane single-point-of-failure boundary (🔴 revision 3.0, layered correction confirmed by the independent architecture review — the original "control plane down, execution side unaffected" was too broad)**: the control plane remains single-region, but **two kinds of failure must be promised separately**:
  - **(a) Control-plane ECS / entry point (CloudFront · ALB · Cognito) down**: the console is unavailable, **scheduling already deployed on the execution side keeps running** (each target's MWAA is independent) — the original promise **holds**.
  - **(b) The whole home region / the control-plane DDB unreachable**: 🔴 **the execution side is not autonomous** — at run time `runtime-lib` **reads task definitions back from the control-plane DDB** (`:154 _exec_sync` reads `bgp-sync-tasks`) and **takes the run lock in the control-plane DDB** (`:208-211` **raises directly** on non-conditional errors) ⇒ **new tasks in other targets may fail to start** (instances already running are unaffected). **Hence it must not be claimed that "control plane down, execution side completely unaffected".**
  - **Phase-one mitigations (all done, section 20.5)**: ① **execution manifest snapshot** — at publish time the task definition is **baked into the pushed artifact** (DAGs / scripts carry a snapshot of the definition), so at run time **the DDB definition is no longer read back**, eliminating the "read definition" dependency of (b); ② **the lock must remain central** (single-writer semantics cannot be distributed), so while the home DDB is unreachable "mutual exclusion within a target" cannot be guaranteed ⇒ **fail-closed, refuse to start** (never allow a double run); the SLA states "while home is unreachable, new tasks are not guaranteed to start"; ③ state write-back uses **local buffering + catch-up after recovery** (outbox pattern) so that run results are not lost.
- 🔴 **Control-plane state is not only DDB (confirmed by the independent architecture review, correcting the original "almost entirely in DDB + IaC")**: the control plane's stateful domains also include the **Cognito user pool** (users / passwords / MFA), **OpenMetadata RDS MySQL** (`om-database-stack`), **OpenSearch** (`om-search-stack`), **Secrets Manager**, **AgentCore Runtime / Memory**, and **audit and deployment state**. Hence "DDB Global Tables in phase two is enough for cold standby" is **an oversimplification** — every state domain needs its own backup / replication / recovery solution with RPO / RTO; and Global Tables **do not provide** the single-writer semantics of the current distributed lock (**without epoch / fencing there would be two primaries**). On this basis the design **explicitly positions itself as a "federation of regional execution" and does not promise global HA / DR** (section 20.5).
- **Hidden side effect of enabling a new region**: updating the `aws:RequestedRegion` condition requires one control-plane stack update → the control-plane ECS of all existing regions rolls once (minute-scale jitter). Done by CodeBuild as part of the enabling flow; low-frequency operations, scheduled in a maintenance window.
- **Deployment automation = fully automatic by the platform through CodeBuild (decided on 2026-08-27)**: the platform is responsible for one-click deployment / supplementary deployment / destruction; humans only choose the region + components; deployment privilege is isolated in the CodeBuild service role (section 12.0). **The original "semi-automatic, hand-typed commands" approach is abandoned** — it contradicted the platform's reason to exist, "save human effort".

### 14bis.3 Risk register (P0 / P1 / P2)

> 📌 **Mapping between the two P0 numbering axes (to avoid confusion)**: this table uses the **numeric axis P0-1 … P0-9 (run-time / implementation risks)**; section 12 uses the **letter axis P0-A … P0-H (design fixes of the deployment approach)**. Mapping: **P0-5 in this table = P0-A of section 12 (data bucket RETAIN), P0-7 = P0-B (self-contained stack group), P0-8 = P0-C (deploy lock)**; P0-D / E / F / G / H of section 12 (IAM roles / idempotency / progress / buildspec / source acquisition) are design fixes specific to the deployment approach and are not repeated in this run-time risk table.

| # | Risk | Level | Trigger | Impact | Probability | Mitigation | Rollback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P0-1 | The runtime-lib dual-region split breaks the cross-executor lock | **P0** | the DDB client is not fixed to the control plane / lock attributes misaligned | backfill / scheduling DROP+CTAS collides on OIDs, run records corrupted | medium | controlRegion unit test (following the lock-parity precedent) + headless cross-executor serialization verification (following the earlier OID-collision fix) | single-point revert; home goes back to the original `_mk` |
| P0-2 | The home environment's behavior regresses | **P0** | the default of the parameterized refactor ≠ current behavior | all existing tasks | medium | the descriptor stores **real names**, not derived ones; the 4 functions default env to home; zero FAIL in the headless contract snapshot is the hard evidence. 🔴 **Revision 3.5 correction**: the original "default falls back to env" / "deleting the registry item restores the status quo" **conflicts with the fail-closed rule of sections 1.1 / 2.2** (building it that way would reintroduce a fallback surface) ⇒ the mitigation becomes: **the read path is read-only; a missing descriptor fails closed and points to the seed entry point**; true rollback relies on **not wiring** (the side-effect-free foundation batch has zero production callers), not on "delete the item to return to the status quo" | **Not wiring = status quo** (deleting the item ⇒ the new paths that depend on the descriptor fail closed) |
| P0-3 | One of the 13 task-runs write points is missed | **P0** | a point missed / the MWAA bypass not synchronized | monitoring / cost / alerting wrong per region | medium | changed in one pass in the same batch as the project-isolation design; the 13 points grepped one by one | attributes are pure additions; the read side is compatible with legacy |
| P0-4 | One of the 5 warehouse direct-query paths is not made region-aware | **P0** | only the 4 atomic functions changed, these 5 missed | modeling / metrics / quality in region B cannot run | medium (missed in the original draft) | the 5 files changed one by one; **refactoring and acceptance both belong to the lifecycle and push-down batch** (done on a second region with the redshift component installed, section 16) — ⚠️ the original "include in the consumption-side batch" conflicted with the acceptance of section 16 (lifecycle and push-down batch) and has been corrected: these 5 are **execution / modeling write paths** and belong to the lifecycle and push-down batch; only the read-only consumption queries of section 8 belong to the consumption-side batch | each file can be reverted independently |
| P0-5 | Deleting an execution environment deletes customer data by mistake | **P0** | the data bucket is emptied and deleted during `cdk destroy` | **permanent loss of customer data** | medium (a real defect in the current code!) | 🔴 Confirmed by the first review round: the current data bucket is `DESTROY+autoDeleteObjects` (platform-stack.ts:51-53), **not RETAIN** — the exec-env data bucket **must change to explicit RETAIN and drop autoDelete**; S3 Tables table buckets are runtime orphans outside the stack and must be handled separately (section 4.4); deletion requires approval; home can never be deleted | after the RETAIN change, destroy does not touch the data bucket; this code defect must be fixed before construction starts |
| P0-6 | Deployment-privilege isolation fails and is used for escalation | **P0** | the platform is compromised / the buildspec is modified as escalation in disguise | account-level resource abuse / privilege escalation | low | the platform has only `StartBuild` restricted to the project ARN; strong power is welded onto the CodeBuild service role that runs only the fixed buildspec; **the right to modify the buildspec / project is protected at the same level as deployment rights**; allow-list validation; full audit. ⚠️ Honest positioning = minimizing the **resident** attack surface, not "least privilege on the deployment path" (section 12.0) | revoking the platform's StartBuild permission cuts the path |
| P0-7 | The exec-env stack group wrongly references live control-plane constructs / touches control-plane stacks | **P0** | the stack group consumes home's VPC / KMS / ALB SG, which do not exist in region B; or stack names intersect with the 21 control-plane stacks | deployment failure / control plane modified by mistake | medium (that is how the current stacks are written) | refactor into a self-contained stack group (own VPC / KMS / SG) + stack IDs carry a region prefix so that the namespaces do not intersect + synth-level acceptance (section 4.1a / P0-B) | the stack group is independent; no impact unless deployed |
| P0-8 | Concurrent builds in the same region step on each other | **P0** | a double-click in the same region / supplementary deployment concurrent with initialization | CloudFormation `operation in progress` / bootstrap race | medium | environment-level deploy lock (run-lock.ts DynamoDB conditional write, section 12.2); while held, same-region deploys are rejected / queued | the lock's lease expires and releases automatically |
| P0-9 | **Residency track 2 convergence fails (the platform automatically moves business data back to the US-East home region in the background) / track 1 interactive queries leave no audit** (restated per the dual tracks of section 8.5.0) | **P0** (track 2) / P1 (track 1 audit) | track 2: OM Profiler computeMetrics / background sampling / telemetry still pull rows centrally in-process / CRR of regional buckets not disabled / agentcore falls back to the center; track 1: cross-jurisdiction interactive queries without disclosure + audit marker | track 2 = the platform **moves data out of the jurisdiction on its own** (compliance violation); track 1 = the user bears responsibility but there is no audit trail | medium | track 2: section 8.5.7 ③④⑥⑦ (cross-jurisdiction work computed in the region, the center receives only non-data values, CRR off, fallback confined to the region); track 1: the leading wrapper shows the disclosure banner + audits (sections 8.5.5 ① / 8.5.7 ①); the A7 lint guardrail; acceptance in section 16 | track 2: close the central automatic data-pull entry points; track 1 is normal (the user bears responsibility) |
| P0-10 | 🔴 **Revision 3.20 moves this to the customer's responsibility (decision 6quinquies): the platform has no legal gate** — the original "going to production across jurisdictions without a lawful legal mechanism = open = compliance violation (blocking)" is void | **—** (not under the platform's control) | the customer makes its own legal decision at the moment it registers a cross-jurisdiction target; the platform's duty = **auditability** (full regional record + central summary, refuse when the write fails, no modification or deletion) + **disclaimer + consent retention** (the admin's consent at registration goes into the descriptor; each user gets a dialog on first operation, consent is written to `bgp-residency-acks`, enforced server-side, queryable later) | the platform does not make the legal determination on the customer's behalf; the copy says only "the platform assumes no legal risk related to cross-border data transfer or data compliance; you must constrain and control it yourself under the laws and regulations of your local government" | — | built in its own construction work package |  |
| P0-11 | **Modeling landing has no multi-target fan-out** (axis A, section 19.1) | **P0** | code-table / dimension / fact-table landing is outside the push-down framework of section 5, `landed` is a single global boolean, the land route has no target parameter | a target references a `dim_code_*` / dimension table that exists only in home → explodes at run time (governance artifacts are more hidden than tasks) | medium | bring modeling landing into push-down: the definition carries **`targets[]`**, structural DDL is materialized per target, **`landedByTarget` (observed state including schemaHash, against false green)**, shown in the version dashboard | landing state is a pure addition; rollback per target |
| P0-12 | **grants pass for every target + masking silently fails** (axis C, section 19.3) | **P0** | grants keys have no target (a home grant unlocks the same-named cross-jurisdiction table); the masking lookup side has no target → after the storage side gains a target segment the lookup misses | cross-jurisdiction privilege escalation (violates residency) + **PII in plaintext** in non-home targets | medium | grants **per target** (key carries target + approval carries target + existing grants split per target); the masking lookup side threads target through + **moves down into the regional data plane** + **L3 / L4 fail-closed** (all 6 existing passthrough paths of `maskRows` covered) | the grant split is reversible; masking aligned on both sides |
| P0-13 | **Audit metadata itself crosses the border** (axis D, section 19.4) | **P0** | the audit of cross-jurisdiction operations (operator PII + activity stream + queryId) sinks into the single central table; requirement section 5 mandated central | personal data crosses the border (contradicting the results-stay-regional rule of section 8.5; the harder the audit wiring, the worse) | medium | audit split into two classes: non-PII summaries stay central / PII, details, SQL text and plaintext queryId **stay in the regional audit store for cross-jurisdiction targets** (RETAIN + tamper-proof); requirement section 5 amended with an exception | the regional audit store is independent |
| P0-14 | **The cookbook hard-wires home resource names** (consumption side, section 19.5; the original section 7.3 wrongly judged "zero change") | **P0** | seed recipes hard-code `bgp-workgroup` / `dev` → flow into 6 codegen exits | code generated in region B cannot reach the home warehouse, or boto3 is not pinned to a region → reads / writes the US-East home region from region B (violates residency); doubly blind (missing region + missing project) | medium | recipe schema / resource names injected from platform context (region + project), codegen without hard-coding, core-dist copies redistributed (jointly with the project-isolation design) | change the source + redistribute |
| P0-15 | **The network advisor's one-click fix changes infrastructure in the wrong region** (consumption side, section 19.5) | **P0** | region is a fake parameter; probe / apply clients pinned to home; apply runs Modify / CreateEndpoint on the HOME region's VPC | real infrastructure (VPC / endpoint) changed in the **wrong region** for a region-B connectivity problem | medium | `req.region` threads through probe / apply, per-region clients, apply hits the target region | client factory |
| P1-1 | IAM region-condition update and resource creation out of order | P1 | order misplaced inside the buildspec | platform access to region B denied | medium | pin the buildspec order: create resources + update the region condition → write back the registry to allow probing (section 12.1) | rerun the build (idempotent) |
| P1-4 | Supplementary deployment damages installed components / data | P1 | add-components called with wrong parameters or not idempotent | the core data lake / running tasks damaged | low | CDK idempotent, add only never modify; the full component set is passed; supplementary deployment creates only uninstalled stacks; the core data stack is RETAIN and not touched by update (section 4.6) | CloudFormation rolls back to the last stable stack |
| P1-5 | Tasks / canary / consumption side implicitly assume redshift exists; pushed down to a no-redshift region, they fail at run time | **P1** | after "Redshift moved out of core", tasks / queries hit a region with only core installed | warehouse-type tasks / Ask-Your-Data in that region explode at run time | medium (brought in by this round's large change) | component-existence pre-validation before push-down (section 5.2); no-redshift degradation on the consumption side (section 8.1); the canary takes the lake path, conditioned on components (section 4.2); the descriptor marks redshift* as optional (section 2.1) | recovers after the redshift component is added (section 4.6) |
| P1-6 | The model call of cross-jurisdiction Ask-Your-Data leaves the jurisdiction (a **known and accepted** gap in phase one) | P1 | in phase one models use `global.*` profiles | the prompt (schema + results) of cross-jurisdiction Ask-Your-Data / Copilot is routed globally | high (certain to happen in phase one) | decided on 2026-08-28: accepted in phase one, not gated off; disclosure in the UI without blocking (section 8.5.6); the pure data path is unaffected | closed in phase two by region pinning (the region-pinned-model phase of the [model-configuration design](./model-configuration.md)) |
| P1-2 | FQN / OM same-name collision across regions | P1 | one code base runs in many regions; workgroup / db share names | lineage / catalog overwrite and mix across regions | medium | non-home service names / FQNs carry a region segment (section 7.2); home keeps old names, zero migration | metadata side, can be re-ingested |
| P1-3 | S3 bucket names collide in the second region | P1 | bucket names contain the account but not the region | bucket creation AlreadyExists | high (if not renamed) | naming SSOT: non-home bucket names gain a region segment; home exempt (section 3.2) | naming rules are centralized and fixable |
| P2-1 | Fixed cost per region ($400+/month) | P2 | installing the MWAA + NAT components | cost | — | components optional; the wizard shows the estimate | remove components |
| P2-2 | Cross-region DDB direct-write latency | P2 | executors write to the control plane across regions | tens of ms of latency | low | the second-scale lock lease tolerates it; on disconnect, reconcile-on-read | fallback path unchanged |
| P2-3 | AgentCore per-region operating cost | P2 | Ask-Your-Data installs the agentcore component × N | image publishing / cost | — | component optional; without it, fallback to in-process | remove components |
| P2-4 | Service unavailable / insufficient quota in the target region makes enabling fail (a finding of a later review round) | P2 | the new region has not launched a service (MWAA / Bedrock models etc.) or the account's service quota in that region (Redshift RPU / VPC / EIP / number of MWAA environments) is insufficient | enabling / adding components in that region fails | medium | the probe suite of section 4.2 probes "service availability + quota headroom" up front (reusing the idea of the model-configuration design's D5 Bedrock probe, extended to MWAA / Redshift); the wizard's step 2 greys out components unsupported in that region with an explanation; failure offers the exits "request a quota increase / choose another region" | retry after changing region or after the quota increase |

**🔄 Staging is the risk stop-loss gate (the core of what makes this design feasible)**: the foundation batch is a **pure lossless refactor** (naming SSOT + task-runs dimensions + client-factory parameterization + read-only registry, everything defaulting to home); zero FAIL in the headless contract snapshot means it passes — **even if this design stopped at the foundation batch it would be a net gain** (eliminating the **30 lines / 23 files** of fallback literals, serving the model-configuration and project-isolation designs). The real resource and risk investment starts after the second-region batch (actually enabling a second region) and can be staged and stopped according to customer feedback; the whole blast radius need not be taken on at once.

## 15. Cross-matrix with the model-configuration and project-isolation designs

| Intersection | Content | Construction agreement |
|---|---|---|
| multi-region ↔ model configuration | per-region model = the `ai-model#<targetId>#global` key of D8 in the [model-configuration design](./model-configuration.md) (one default per region, not per capability) + the second parameter of `resolveModelIdRuntime(capability, targetId)`; 🔴 residency regions require region-pinned models (cross-region `us.` / `global.` profiles forbidden); phase one uses `global.*` and accepts the AI-path gap, phase two closes it in that design's region-pinned-model phase (its D9 / section 8.5.4 here) | model configuration lands first (table / resolver function with hooks, not activated); the consumption-side batch of this design activates them, zero table migration |
| multi-region ↔ project isolation | the extra dimensions of `bgp-task-runs` are **the same batch of write points** (including the 4 high-risk MWAA bypass writes) | **designed once, changed once** as the `{projectId, region}` attribute bundle; if the foundation batch of this design goes first then D2 of the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md) merges into it, and vice versa; never touch it twice |
| **🔴 multi-region ↔ project isolation (added by the architecture review round; the second shared refactoring surface)** | **credential / client resolution is the same piece of code changed by both designs**: `_arn` in `admin-secret.ts:13` is a **process-level single-value cache** (the header comment states "all Redshift calls of the platform execute as this one admin identity"), consumed in **16 places**; **⚠️ the deep-audit grep confirmed a second, structurally identical single-value cache `zeroetl-service.ts:69 _secretArnCache`** (`getZeroEtlSecretArn`, 6 consumers; interactive SQL `redshift/execute:16` forks to it with the ternary `wg===ZEROETL_WORKGROUP`); **this design, section 6.3, splits clients per region while the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 7bis L3, swaps credentials per project** — two single-value caches, two orthogonal axes. The two earlier drafts agreed only on the task-runs batch and had **zero cross-reference here** → whoever lands second overturns the first, and it collides with the lock's byte-alignment surface (the earlier OID-collision lesson) | 🔴 **Treated like task-runs: designed once, changed once**: the credential / client cache keys of `admin-secret._arn` **and `zeroetl-service._secretArnCache`** (both caches! missing zeroetl means zero-ETL workgroup queries bypass isolation) / `runtime-lib._mk` are **designed directly as the composite `(targetId, project, purpose)`** (not single-valued); the region axis of this design and the project axis of the project-isolation design resolve jointly to `(region endpoint) × (project DB credential)`; merged into the lock-parity unit-test surface (**including the hit semantics of the `(targetId, project, purpose)` composite key: same env, different project → different credential**). 🔴 **The residency jurisdiction gate (a review finding) is an independent leading middleware that runs before credential resolution (section 8.5.5 ①) and is not put into the credential composite key** — the row-fetch endpoint `redshift/result/[id]` works from statementId alone and does not resolve credentials, so putting the gate into the key would be bypassed by it; hence the credential composite key stays `(targetId, project, purpose)` and `jurisdiction` is guarded separately as a fail-closed **leading gate** (this is the correct landing point of the review finding "the jurisdiction gate precedes credentials, fail-open → fail-closed"). **Never change each separately and touch it twice; never miss the second cache (`zeroetl._secretArnCache`); a third process-level single-value cache of the same kind, `_sgIdCache` (SG discovery, section 6.4), must also become per-env — ⚠️ but it is **a pure region axis with no project dimension** (keyed only by **`targetId`**, **not part of the credential composite key**; do not wrongly add a project dimension to the SG cache), distinct from the two credential caches; missing any of the three caches means hitting the wrong region (SG) / the wrong project (credentials).** |
| model configuration ↔ project isolation | no direct coupling (model configuration is platform-level, not per project) | — |
| Implementation order | 🔴 **The authority = the staging in section 17; this cell does not restate it** (revision 3.1 correction: the original "model configuration → project-isolation control plane → multi-region → project-isolation data plane L2 / L3" was the **module-granularity** old order from the time of the architecture review round, replaced by the **batch-granularity final order** of section 17). 🔴 **The sequence is not restated in this cell** (revision 3.2: the single state source = section 17; every restatement is the source of the next drift; the external review's machine criterion is exactly "other places may only reference, never restate"). The architecture review round's motivation (phase one of the project-isolation design, including the C2 data plane, has reached the size of a full earlier module; per-project schema / credential resolution must be designed jointly with this design's per-region resolution) has been absorbed by section 17; "the project-isolation design's C2-L1 GRANT revocation goes first with its security batch" still holds (see section 17) | ✅ **Decided, nothing pending** (2026-08-26: model configuration first; 2026-08-28: "do all of phase one / go with the recommendation"; the GB / 5a / 5b split of section 17); the original "⚠️ pending confirmation" is removed |

## 16. Acceptance criteria (headless + real state sources; a headless contract snapshot before and after)

> **Testability principle (from the testability review)**: for **every P0** in the risk register (section 14bis.3) there **must be one acceptance item that can "assert headlessly + assert against the real state source" to prove it did not happen**; each acceptance item is annotated with the risk number it covers. ⚠️ **"Zero FAIL in the headless contract-snapshot comparison" only proves "the features covered by the existing 7 contracts did not regress"; its coverage is smaller than this design's risk surface** (the existing L2 contracts have no region dimension, no lock serialization, no 13-point matrix — see section 16bis) — hence the acceptance items below **cannot be replaced by "zero FAIL"** and must each be run independently.

- **Lossless-refactor batch**:
  - zero FAIL in the headless contract-snapshot comparison (**necessary but not sufficient** evidence of a pure refactor with zero behavior change); the naming / lock-parity unit tests pass.
  - [covers **P0-1**, broken lock]**Hard evidence of cross-executor job-lock serialization**: in the home region, a manual run × a scheduled run trigger the same stateful job (DROP+CTAS) concurrently → the timestamps of the real DDB lock items prove **serialized spacing** (as in the earlier fix: from "concurrent within the same second" to "≥ N s apart"), not "no error text".
  - [covers **P0-2**, home regression]the default behavior of the 4 atomic functions is byte-for-byte unchanged (headless smoke of GenBI / BI / Ask-Your-Data + before/after DOM diff; the data service was retired 2026-09-05 and is no longer verified); the DDB item of a new run carries `{region: home, projectId}`.
  - [covers **P0-3**, 13 points missed]**matrix acceptance of the task-runs write points** (not just 1): **trigger one by one** the 9 in-platform write points (start / start-realtime / workflow-trigger / etl / emr / backfill / dependency …) + the 4 MWAA bypass write points; the run items produced by each **all** carry `{region,projectId}`; any missing point = FAIL (the per-point grep list is in section 7.1; the acceptance script asserts the DDB item per point according to the list).
- **Second-region batch (minimal closed loop)**:
  - the full flow of enabling a second region (**core-only**) (wizard → StartBuild deployment → all probes green → canary succeeded) asserted on the page; a **lake sync chain** really runs in the second region (page succeeded + that region's Glue GetJobRun + **an S3 Tables / Athena query as hard evidence**); the task-runs item has region = the second region.
  - [covers **P0-7**, self-contained stack group]**synth-level acceptance**: the exec-env stack group's `cdk synth` for region B succeeds (no reference to home's live VPC / KMS / ALB SG constructs; all stack IDs carry the region prefix and have zero intersection with the 21 control-plane stacks), at CI-gate level (see section 4.1a).
  - [covers **P0-8**, concurrent builds]two deploys triggered concurrently in the same region (a double-click / initialization colliding with a supplementary deployment) → the second is **rejected or queued** by the deploy lock (the DDB lock item as hard evidence), without a CloudFormation `operation in progress` crash.
  - ⚠️ **Component constraint**: the second-region batch installs only core; acceptance takes **the lake path (Glue → S3 Tables → Athena)** and **does not require Redshift evidence**; Redshift query evidence is left until the redshift component is installed (the lifecycle / push-down and consumption-side batches). ⚠️ **Source-type constraint**: the sync chain must use a **DynamoDB / S3 / Kinesis / MSK source** (whose connection bag carries its own region); **JDBC sources execute only in home in phase one** — the acceptance script must not use a MySQL source for the second-region verification (the gap of section 6.4; an expected limitation, not a bug).
- **Lifecycle and push-down batch (full)**:
  - the same workflow bound to 2 regions and published → each region's MWAA has its own DAG (the S3 DAG text as hard evidence: baked values per region), each schedules and really runs to succeeded, control-plane monitoring sees both in one place (region column filter); the version dashboard matrix is complete / a deliberately failed push to one region → red cell + re-push recovers; the MWAA bypass writes directly back to the control plane (hard evidence: the item written back to the home DDB by the second region's DAG).
  - [covers **P0-4**, 5 warehouse direct-query paths]on a second region with the redshift component installed, **trigger one by one** the 5 warehouse direct-query paths that bypass the 4 atomic functions (modeling modeling/redshift, metrics metrics/service, quality quality/service, sensitive sampling sensitive/scan, catalog governance/catalog) → each hits **that region's workgroup** (CloudTrail / Redshift query history as hard evidence of the endpoint, not home); if one was missed, that path hits home or errors, which is FAIL.
  - [covers **P0-5**, data loss on environment deletion]**hard evidence of data protection on environment deletion** (the only "permanent loss of customer data" item in the risk table; mandatory): the deletion approval flow (a developer's request is refused / validation refuses when bindings exist / after admin approval → decommissioning) **asserted on the page**; and **after** `cdk destroy` of that region's stack group completes, the lake bucket / scripts bucket / DAG bucket **still exist with their objects undeleted** (AWS CLI `s3api head-bucket` + a `list-objects` count of key objects > 0 as hard evidence) while the compute / network stacks are gone — proving that the RETAIN change (P0-A) really works; the detached S3 Tables table bucket is confirmed separately to still exist. The home environment's delete button is hidden in the UI + hard-refused by the API (page + 403, double evidence).
- **Consumption-side batch (full coverage)**: Ask-Your-Data with region B selected → that region's CloudTrail / Runtime logs prove that the SQL hit workgroup B and that InvokeModel used the model configured for region B; a BI widget bound to region B produces data from a different source than A; the cost page groups by region at the first level and the two regions' amounts match independent Cost Explorer queries; governance-catalog region filter; same-named tables in two regions have non-colliding FQNs (the OM service name as hard evidence).
  - [covers **P0-9**, residency dual tracks (section 8.5.0)]**hard evidence of dual-track egress for a cross-jurisdiction region** (section 8.5.7; satisfies the testability rule of section 16, "every P0 gets a headless acceptance"): **track 2 (automatic background moves by the platform, must be controlled)** — ① background sensitive sampling / quality / metrics / modeling / OM Profiler scheduled tasks **compute in the region**; the central OM / result tables have **no cross-jurisdiction rows / no column-value profiles** (computeMetrics off); ② the regional result bucket has **CRR off** (`get-bucket-replication` shows no cross-region destination); ③ the agentcore fallback **stays within the region** (no fallback to central in-process); ④ results / memory / SQL history **land in regional storage** (regional DDB / S3 as hard evidence; no rows centrally). **Track 1 (user interactive queries, not blocked)** — ⑤ cross-jurisdiction interactive queries (execute / result[id] / athena / bi / genbi) **return results normally** (not rejected) but the center **keeps the disclosure + audit marker** (the audit table has who / cross-jurisdiction region / query id as hard evidence); `x-bgp-region` routes correctly to that region. **Any cross-jurisdiction row reaching the center on track 2 = FAIL; a missing audit marker on track 1 = FAIL (but returning results is not FAIL).** ⚠️ Not applicable to single-jurisdiction deployments (section 8.5 all off); the hard-residency option separately verifies that the interactive gate fails closed (section 8.5.6).
- **P0s not covered by headless acceptance (honest statement)**: **P0-6** (deployment-privilege isolation defeated by escalation) is a security property and cannot be falsified by a functional headless assertion — replaced by a **static check of the permission boundary**: confirm that the platform task role contains only `codebuild:StartBuild` restricted to the exec-env project ARN (assertion on the IAM policy text), that strong power exists only in the CodeBuild service role, and that the right to modify the buildspec is protected at the same level as deployment rights (section 12.0); and run once the negative CLI probe "a direct `cloudformation:CreateStack` with the platform identity must be refused" (boundary corroboration only, not the main acceptance evidence).

### 16bis. 🔴 Contract-snapshot contracts and test scaffolding to add for this design (added by the testability review, against a "zero-FAIL false green")

> **Motivation**: the existing headless contract-snapshot suite has only **7 L2 contracts** (lineage_table/column, knowledge_list, workflow_runs, home_charts, sync_list, ai_wiring) + a **29-page L1 sweep**, **all targeting existing single-region features**; the new `/exec-envs` page is not in L1 and no contract asserts the region dimension / lock serialization / the 13-point dimensions. **Without extending the contracts, a "zero FAIL" after this refactoring would be a false green** (the regression would happen in the contracts' blind spot). This list is the **test-scaffolding prerequisite task** before the lossless-refactor batch starts (still design: it lists the items; the script code is written when construction starts and, by rule, verification scripts stay local and are never committed).

| Addition | Layer | What it asserts | Risk covered |
|---|---|---|---|
| `exec_env_list` contract | L2 | the `/exec-envs` page loads, the home environment card is present, component health tags render; the new page enters the L1 pages.json | P0-2, regression of the new page |
| `run_dimension_matrix` contract | L2 | after triggering one run, its DDB task-runs item **contains `region` and contains `projectId`** (reads the real state source, not the page) | P0-3 (shared with the project-isolation design) |
| `cross-exec lock serialization` scaffolding | unit test + headless | **extends the existing** `runtime-lib-lock-parity.test.ts` (🔵 already exists, created with the earlier OID-collision fix; currently asserts Node run-lock ↔ python `_exec_sql` same attribute names / same millisecond unit / raw nodeId, **but has no region dimension**) — adds two items: ① after the `_mk` split the DDB client is still fixed to the control plane and lock attributes do not drift across regions; ② **hit semantics of the credential composite key `(targetId, project, purpose)`** (same env, different project → different credential / schema; the cache does not mix projects; both the `admin-secret` and `zeroetl-service` caches covered) — this is the landing point of the composite-key unit test of the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 9 (joint between the two designs, against "this design proposed it, the other did not wire it"); headless concurrent triggering asserts timestamp serialization | P0-1 + the architecture P0 (credential resolution) |
| `synth-gate` scaffolding | CI unit test | region-B `cdk synth` succeeds + stack IDs carry the region prefix + no reference to home's live constructs (section 4.1a) | P0-7 |
| `deploy-lock` scaffolding | headless | a second concurrent deploy in the same region is rejected / queued (DDB lock item) | P0-8 |
| `retain-guard` scaffolding | CLI hard evidence | after deleting an environment, head-bucket on the data bucket succeeds + object count > 0 | P0-5 |
| `5-path region` scaffolding | headless + CloudTrail | each of the 5 warehouse direct-query paths hits the second region's workgroup | P0-4 |

> **Landing constraint**: the two L2 contracts `exec_env_list` and `run_dimension_matrix` **must land in the lossless-refactor batch** (they guard the core invariants of the lossless refactor), following the suite's extension mechanism (`contracts.py` gains one `def fn(pg,url)->dict` + one line in `CONTRACTS`; `pages.json` is regenerated by `gen_pages.py`). The remaining scaffolding lands with its batch (the second-region / lifecycle and push-down batches). **New contracts themselves must also be verified by "one full prebuild run" to show that they do not produce false positives** (from the lessons on verification-script quality: weak assertions, locale, and taking bucket names for granted are the three pitfalls).

## 17. Implementation phasing

> - ✅ Decided on 2026-08-26: **phase one = the lossless-refactor batch, the second-target batch and the lifecycle batch in one go** (foundation + second-region closed loop + lifecycle / full push-down).
> - 🔴 **Revision (2026-08-28, coverage-audit decision 2, "do everything in phase one, leave nothing for a phase two")**: **the consumer-side batch is promoted from phase two to phase one**; the four axes of section 19 + the consumption / operations surfaces + the differentiators are **all brought
>   into phase one** and allocated across the four batches by the table below — 🔴 **the axis-B target storage keys of result-type data go with the consumer-side batch** (the former wording "with the lossless-refactor batch" was wrong: pk/sk is a **breaking key change** and conflicts with that
>   batch's "zero behavior change"; it comes with the home exemption + dual read and backfill, [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.8); **axis A, push-down of modeling landing**, goes with the lifecycle batch (in the same frame as task push-down; 🔴 **acceptance target =
>   a same-jurisdiction second target with Redshift installed**; cross-jurisdiction items move to the consumer-side batch together with the third test target, [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.7); **axis C access control / masking per region + RBAC × region + axis D
>   audit residency + the consumption surface (cookbook / advisors / retrieval; the Data API has been retired) + the differentiators (modeling push-down with a geographic dimension / one-screen cross-geography overview)** go with the consumer-side batch; **the operations surface (per-region
>   alerting / cross-region cleanup on project deletion / decommissioning of non-home regions / cross-region dependency fan-out / notification masking)** goes with the lifecycle batch (decommissioning / dependencies) and the consumer-side batch (alerting / project deletion). **There is no "phase
>   two" bucket any more.**

> - 🔴 **Revision 3.0 re-sequencing (decision on the first open question of [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.6: resolving two circular dependencies)** — the full topology is in the specification, section 6.6:
> - **Standalone security fix (queryHandle, independent) → 🆕 shared-foundation batch G → 🆕 first model-configuration batch → 🆕 second model-configuration batch → project-isolation guard batch → lossless-refactor batch (+ the project-isolation monitoring batch in the same batch) → second-target
>   batch → 🆕 project-isolation data-plane supply layer → lifecycle batch → 🆕 project-isolation data-plane migration layer → consumer-side batch (+ the third model-configuration batch in the same batch) → project-isolation sharing batch → project-isolation copy batch**
>
> 🔴 **Revision 3.2 correction (confirmed by a P0 finding of the external review on 2026-08-29): until then this sequence contained no model-configuration batch at all**, while this document declares itself "the sole authority on implementation order" ⇒ **building by the sole authority would have skipped the whole model-configuration design**. It is now included explicitly:
> - **The first and second model-configuration batches come after batch G and before the project-isolation guard batch** — model configuration is the first feature module to be touched (decided on 2026-08-26), but it must yield to the standalone security fix (a live privilege-escalation hole) and to batch G (the shared foundation; otherwise later batches keep overturning each other).
> - **The third model-configuration batch is in the same batch as the consumer-side batch** — it is not "phase two": the consumer-side batch's "per-target model for Ask-Your-Data" **hard-depends** on the activation of the `ai-model#<targetId>#global` key delivered by the third model-configuration batch; leaving it for phase two would make the consumer-side batch undeliverable (which is exactly what decision 2, "do everything in phase one, leave nothing for a phase two", requires).
> - **The fourth model-configuration batch (per-capability) is explicitly marked out of scope** — not "suspended to phase two": phase one does not build it and does not promise it; building it requires a new requirement. Only then is "everything in phase one" a verifiable, closed statement rather than a pile of "phase two" items nobody owns.
>
> **Batch G (new)** = builds only functions / SSOT / ledger writes, moves no data (lossless, reversible): G0 clean up naming leftovers + narrow the gate of section 20.4, G1 targetId registry + `isHome()`, **G2 the three factory classes of `getPrivilegedCred()`**, **G3 `authoritativeFqn()` + redefinition of `bareKey`/`fqnVariants`**, **G4 `projectSchema()` SSOT**, G5 table-creation ledger "write only, not yet read", G6 the three caches switched to composite keys.
>
> **Why batch G is indispensable**: ① axis A of this design must land into `shared` (created by the project-isolation data-plane batch) ⇄ that data-plane batch needs per-target credentials (created by this design's lifecycle batch) = **deadlock**; ② the metadata-FQN settlement of the project-isolation design needs this design's section 3.10 (the consumer-side batch) ⇄ the consumer-side batch in turn comes after it = **reverse order**; ③ `bareKey`/`fqnVariants` is **one and the same function** changed once by each of the two phases, violating the iron rule "change it completely in one go".
>
> 🔴 **Axis B phase correction**: the target segment in the pk/sk of the 5 result tables is a **breaking key change** and conflicts with the lossless-refactor batch's "zero behavior change" ⇒ **axis B belongs to the consumer-side batch** (with the home exemption + dual read and backfill, [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.8).

| Phase | Content | Nature |
|---|---|---|
| **Standalone security fix (first, independent)** | `bgp-query-handles` + `submitStatement()` + retrieval validation (including the isomorphic Athena IDOR); fixes a **live privilege-escalation hole** | Security fix; can ship / roll back on its own |
| **🆕 Shared-foundation batch G** | G0–G6 (see above) | **Lossless**: builds only functions / SSOT / ledgers, moves no data; the only way to break the circular dependencies |
| **Lossless-refactor batch (foundation, lossless refactor)**<br>🔴 **Split in revision 3.8**: **slice a** (the naming SSOT itself, purely additive with zero call sites, already landed) + **the remainder** (absorbing fallback literals / adding dimensions to task-runs / parameterizing the 4 atomic functions, which touches live paths). **Slice a must precede the second-target batch** (the stack-group resource names depend on it); **the remainder does not block the second-target batch** and may follow it | Naming SSOT + absorption of fallback literals; `bgp-task-runs` gains `{projectId, targetId}` (merged with the project-isolation batch); the 4 atomic functions + client factory parameterized (default home behavior unchanged); exec-env registry + automatic home registration (read-only) | Zero new features; contract-guard all green is the bar; **standalone value: even if this design stopped here, it is a net gain in code quality** |
| **Second-target batch (minimum closed loop in a second region)** | Execution-environment CDK stack group (conditional component assembly) + **a dedicated exec-env CodeBuild project + deployment-role isolation** (section 12.0) + platform StartBuild / progress read-back + management page (list / one-click deploy / probe / canary) + IAM region list; **fully automatic platform-driven enablement** of the second region (core); a single sync / ETL chain runs end to end in the second region | Prototype validating the architecture + deployment automation; the minimum credible artifact to demonstrate to customers |
| **Lifecycle batch (lifecycle + full push-down)** | Creation wizard / testing / deletion approval (destroy goes through the broker) + **supplementary deployment (section 4.6)**; `targets[]` binding for the 5 resource classes + per-target artifact generation and distribution + runtime-lib in two regions; version-consistency dashboard; target dimension in monitoring; **axis A, per-target materialization of modeling**; operations surface (per-target DAG decommissioning / cross-target dependency fan-out) + 🔴 **two hard gates already decided ([the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6)**: ① **cfn-exec-role narrowed in one go from the list collected in the second-target batch** (section 6.2); ② **switch to per-target execution roles before any cross-jurisdiction target goes live** (section 6.1; jurisdictions do not share an execution identity) | Core deliverable |
| **Consumer-side batch (full coverage of the consumption side)** | Ask-Your-Data per region (AgentCore component + activation per the model-configuration design's D8) + BI per region (the data service was retired on 2026-09-05) + FQN / OM regionalization + FinOps per region, first layer + **data-residency mode (section 8.5 dual track: `residency-api` component + **the dual-track front wrapper [track 1 informed + audited; fail-closed only for hard residency / track 2]** + track classification by caller type [triggeredBy] + proximity routing by endpoint origin + regionalization of central pull-style reads + results / memory kept in region + CRR off + tamper-proof audit; dual-track acceptance in section 16) + section 19 axis C (access control / masking per region + RBAC × region) + axis D (audit residency) + the consumption surface (cookbook / advisors / retrieval; the Data API has been retired) + the differentiators (modeling push-down with a geographic dimension / one-screen read-only cross-geography metadata overview)** | **Phase one** (2026-08-28 decision 2: the former phase two brought forward) |

## 18. Decision records

**Decided**:
1. ✅ (first set on 2026-08-26 → **corrected in revision 3.1 on 2026-08-29; the authority moved to section 17**) Implementation order. 🔴 **The original wording of this item — "model configuration → project isolation → multi-region execution; multi-region phase one = the lossless-refactor, second-target and lifecycle batches (consumer-side batch in phase two); the cross matrix of section 15 governs" — is withdrawn**; all three parts were wrong: ① the module-granularity order "model configuration → project isolation → multi-region execution" is superseded by the **batch-granularity settled order** of section 17; ② "**consumer-side batch in phase two**" directly contradicts decision 2, "**no phase two, finish everything in phase one**" (section 19.9); the consumer-side batch is already inside the phase-one sequence; ③ pointing the authority at section 15, whose cell was the same stale text (corrected in step). **The sole authority = the phasing in section 17**:
   > 🔴 **The sequence is in section 17 and is not restated here** (revision 3.2: the same sequence used to be written once each in sections 15, 17 and 18; as soon as section 17 gained the model-configuration batches, the other two copies became stale — exactly the root cause named by the external review's P0 finding).

   The two hard constraints behind this order (not preferences): **the shared-foundation batch G** and **splitting the project-isolation data-plane batch into a supply layer and a migration layer** are the only way to break two circular dependencies (the project-isolation data plane needs this design's per-target credential factory, while this design's per-target credentials in turn need the project-isolation per-project supply; only "supply layer first, migration layer second" resolves it). The history (the motive for the re-sequencing remediation of the architecture review: once the project-isolation data plane, its decision C2, was decided for phase one, its data-plane layers L2/L3 had to be designed jointly with this design) still holds and has been absorbed into section 17.
2. ✅ (2026-08-27) **Environment deployment is fully automated by the platform, with deployment privilege isolated through CodeBuild** (sections 4.1 / 12.0); a human only chooses region + components. **The "semi-automatic, type the commands by hand" option is abandoned** (it contradicts the platform's value of saving manual work).
3. ✅ (2026-08-27) **Component boundary**: core, mandatory = compute + storage + network + data lake; **redshift (warehouse) / kafka (MSK) / mwaa / dms / flink / agentcore are all optional and support supplementary deployment** (sections 2.1 / 4.6).

4. ✅ (2026-08-27; label corrected on 2026-08-28 with option B) **Data residency (read side · dual track: track 1 = a user's deliberate query whose result crosses the border = the user's responsibility / track 2 = the platform moving data automatically in the background = under platform control, section 8.5.0) = tier-one mainstream data residency + Option 1** (section 8.5): processing and storage of business / personal data staying in region is compliant; serving UI code / accessing metadata from the US East region is acceptable; the solution = one central console / front end + one "thin" data API per cross-jurisdiction region + regional result storage (the front end is not regionalized; built as the optional component `residency-api`, installed only in cross-jurisdiction regions; same-jurisdiction / home regions are served centrally).
5. ✅ (2026-08-27; wording corrected on 2026-08-28 with option B) **Residency = single account + architectural boundaries first, soft policy (not a hard "deny on violation")**: no Organizations multi-account / regional deny SCP (hard guard rails); it relies on **the dual track of section 8.5.0** (track 1 interactive query = informed + audited; track 2 background automatic movement = controlled) + `aws:RequestedRegion` + proximity to regional endpoints. The former wording "residency enforcement" was too strong (a review finding) and has been corrected to **soft residency**; hard guard rails (multi-account SCP / server-side hard verdicts) were listed as a phase-two option (section 8.5.6), not chosen for phase one.
6. ✅ (2026-08-27) **Cross-jurisdiction SQL history / Ask-Your-Data memory stay in their region** (accepting that there is no "global unified SQL history / cross-region query memory"; the center keeps only a metadata index).

6bis. ✅ (2026-08-28, a remediation item of the review, option abc-b) **The phase-one egress gap of the AI-model call for cross-jurisdiction Ask-Your-Data = accepted, not gated off**: in phase one the model uses the `global.*` profile and cross-jurisdiction Ask-Your-Data stays open as usual; region pinning (the third model-configuration batch) closes it in phase two (sections 8.5.4 / 8.5.6); **all read-side data-egress closure mechanisms are folded into section 8.5.7** (the first sub-item of that remediation: a front middleware with an allow-list on result-touching entry points [not the project-isolation L3 credential layer] / classification of data vs. control endpoints + origin router / regionalization of central pull-style reads / masking or in-region retention of SQL · answer · error / a read-only guard on execute that forbids data movement / CRR off on regional buckets / fallback confined to the region / no-store); the jurisdiction gate is a separate front gate, and the credential composite key stays `(targetId, project, purpose)` (section 15; corrected in revision 3.1 from `(env, project)`).

6ter. ✅ (2026-08-28, decision **B**) **A query result crossing the border = the user's own doing, and that is normal** — residency is split into two tracks (section 8.5.0): **track 1, a user's deliberate query whose result crosses the border = the user's responsibility; the platform informs + audits and does not hard-block** (consistent with the practice of the major cloud data-development suites: "choosing an overseas region = data export = the customer's responsibility"); **track 2, the platform moving business data automatically in the background = still strictly controlled by the platform** (OM Profiler / background sampling / telemetry / CRR compute cross-jurisdiction data in the region; the center receives only non-data values). Section 8.5 was restructured accordingly: the fail-closed hard gate on interactive queries is downgraded to an informational banner + an audit marker; `x-bgp-region` (a new header, not the `x-bgp-env` of the multi-environment release module) is downgraded to a routing + audit label; origin routing is downgraded to a proximity optimization; **the review finding that "the gate is soft against a malicious client" thereby turns from a defect into a design choice**; hard residency (SCP / server-side verdicts) was listed as a phase-two option (section 8.5.6).

6quater. 🔴 **Withdrawn (2026-09-05, superseded by decision 6quinquies: the platform has no legal gate)** — original text: ✅ (2026-08-28, decision **B, legal prerequisite**) **Cross-jurisdiction production go-live = forbidden by default, opened only once a lawful legal mechanism is in place**: the platform is a processor; cross-border responsibility is a fact, not something "a user click can waive" (a review finding corrected the earlier over-full "deliverable" claim in sections 18 / 13). An environment with `jurisdiction≠home` **does not open interactive queries to production by default**; it may be set active / opened for interaction only once ① `residency-api` is installed and ② a legally endorsed lawful mechanism (adequacy / SCC / BCR) is in place (a deployment-time front gate, [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 2.1; a deployment switch is kept). **A deliverable technical architecture ≠ a deliverable cross-jurisdiction production go-live** (the latter is blocked by the legal prerequisite); the informational text must not imply that the platform is exempt from liability. See section 14bis.3 P0-10 (blocking level), section 8.5.6.

6quinquies. ✅ (2026-09-05, decided: **no legal gate, auditability is all that is required**) The decision, verbatim: "Everything else goes by the recommendations; I have only one requirement: we do not make legal decisions. That part is left to the users to plan themselves; we do not intervene; we only provide the product. If their legal department believes there is a data-compliance problem, they avoid it from the very start rather than relying on a tool to solve it. We only need it to be auditable. Nothing else needs to be considered." Added in the same round: "A dialog may show a liability notice. That is necessary. And once the user agrees to such a dialog, the user's choice may be retained, to avoid disputes." "State that we assume no legal risk whatsoever; users must do their own data-compliance constraints and controls according to local government laws and regulations." ⇒ **Decision**: ① The platform **has no legal gate, makes no legal judgment, keeps no filings, and never refuses any operation for residency reasons**; cross-jurisdiction targets and same-jurisdiction targets take the same path; registering a cross-jurisdiction target = the customer's legal decision, which the platform does not review. ② The only cross-jurisdiction **technical prerequisite** = installing `residency-api` (regional audit / result storage) + per-target execution roles ([the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 6.1, second paragraph); the single-author criterion `residencyPrerequisite` lands in the admission kernel. ③ The platform's obligation = **auditability**: every entry point that touches the data of a cross-jurisdiction target writes a **full regional audit record** (operator / SQL text / statementId / entry / triggeredBy / authKind) + a **central summary** (sqlHash / targetId / rows / durationMs); if either write fails ⇒ the operation is not executed (audit integrity, not compliance intervention); audit records cannot be modified or deleted (explicit Deny on the TaskRole); personal data stays in region. ④ **Disclaimer notice + retained consent**: the versioned text has a single author ("This platform assumes no legal risk related to cross-border data transfer or data compliance. You must constrain and control data compliance yourself in accordance with local government laws and regulations."); two points in time = when a cross-jurisdiction target is registered (the admin's consent goes into the descriptor as `residencyAck`) + the first time each user performs a data operation on that target (a dialog; consent is written to `bgp-residency-acks`; the same [user, target, version] is never prompted again); server-enforced (missing consent ⇒ 409 `residency-ack-required`); viewable on the detail page. ⑤ Withdrawn as a consequence: decision 6quater, the legal prerequisite; the hard-residency option of section 8.5.6; the read-only guard of section 8.5.7 ⑤; the code-table residency gating of section 20.6-B; the track-based rejection of [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.2; the `residency-blocked` observed state; the direct-query "cross-jurisdiction merge forbidden" rule; `bgp-replications.residencyApproval`. Tracked in the roadmap ledger and in the residency work package.

6sexies. ✅ (2026-09-06, decided: **in-region scheduling**) The decision, verbatim: "Every region's scheduled tasks are executed by that region's own MWAA; cross-region scheduling is not allowed. Therefore the execution environments of the other regions, just like the home region, all need the MWAA service — otherwise how would the tasks of that region be scheduled? Orchestration and timers, for example, all need it. This is a bug / design hole that must be fixed right away at the start; you need not consider cost — that is my concern and I have already considered it; it must be done this way. If initialization is needed, then the user must be allowed to initialize an MWAA, and when a customer configures scheduling in a non-home region, it must be checked whether the MWAA service is installed and deployed; if not, prompt the user to add an MWAA service to that execution environment. This problem must be fixed properly and verified with a headless browser." ⇒ **Decision**: ① Timers / orchestration are the **execution plane**, not the control plane: every target's scheduled tasks are triggered by **that target's own MWAA**, the DAG files land in that target's DAG bucket, and unhooking / decommissioning / task logs stay in the same region; home is byte-for-byte unchanged. Until then `dag-fanout.ts` wrote the DAGs of all targets into the home bucket and had the home Airflow trigger them across regions = the hole this item fixes (the original statement of section 4.5, "a region that needs no timed scheduling may skip mwaa", still holds, but once installed it must be **used**). ② `mwaa` moves from "legal but not implemented in the stack group" to **implemented**: it can be checked in the wizard, added later from the detail page, and is really probed; stack `BgpExecEnv-<slug>-mwaa` (bucket / environment / execution role, all from the naming SSOT). ③ A non-home target lacking `mwaa` ⇒ **scheduling cannot be configured** (409 `MWAA_NOT_INSTALLED`, listing every missing region at once), and the person is taken **straight** to the add-component row on that target's detail page — this is the product capability "use what is installed", not a gate. ④ **Control-plane write-back = the only cross-region exception**: that region's MWAA DAG writes run records back to the home `bgp-*` tables (section 1.1, a single copy of the control plane; section 6.3 already settled that direct DDB writes go through IAM); the role is granted only the DDB ARNs of the home region + one `kms:ViaService=dynamodb.<home>` statement, while all other `*` statements remain bound to the local region (a dedicated synth-gate criterion). ⑤ Cost (mw1.small ≈ $350 / month / target) **is not a design constraint**; the wizard only shows it truthfully. ⑥ The target dimension of workflow (orchestration) (`bgp-workflows.targets`, never landed so far) becomes its own work package; this decision first brings the workflow's MWAA access under the same resolver. Contract: [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), revision 3.26, section 3.14; interaction: [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), revision 2.18, section 2.2a; acceptance: ten dedicated acceptance items (acceptance criteria revision 1.26); construction: its own work package; tracked in the roadmap ledger.

6septies. ✅ (2026-09-06, decided: **MWAA Serverless scheduling entry point**) The decision, verbatim: "My suggestion: keep the existing MWAA for now and add a new MWAA Serverless entry point; once scheduling runs end to end in every region, then consider whether to remove the existing MWAA — keep it for now and decide after the tests pass. The tests must likewise run end to end through a headless browser in at least two regions, and every scheduled task must run real EMR Serverless tasks and Glue Spark tasks and Glue Spark tasks. This must be implemented. Do you understand?" Guiding principle: "Our principle is to use serverless as much as possible at the level of these services"; "DataZone need not be introduced for now; keep it idle." ⇒ **Decision**: ① Decision 6sexies and [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.14 **stay word for word**; the classic `mwaa` component is kept; ② 🆕 optional component **`airflow-serverless`** (Amazon MWAA Serverless: no environment, billed per task-second, YAML definitions + a Python code package, EventBridge Scheduler for timers, no Airflow UI), alongside `mwaa` and independently installable; home installs it too; ③ any target with either scheduler component installed can configure scheduling; when both are installed, **serverless takes precedence**, and the detail page can switch the "Scheduler preference"; ④ acceptance = headless + **≥ 2 regions** + **every scheduled task really runs Redshift / Glue Spark / EMR Serverless** (the repeated "Glue Spark" in the decision is treated as the superset); ⑤ retiring classic MWAA becomes a separate package, to be decided after the tests pass. **Facts established by the spike (seven runs in us-west-2 on 2026-09-06, recorded in the work package)**: all three engines really ran successfully inside serverless (Redshift 30s / Glue 88s / EMR 250s); PythonOperator needs a `Code` package; 🔴 **the default network reaches only same-region AWS endpoints, so the cross-region write-back to the home ledger requires VPC mode** (that region's core private subnets + a self-referencing SG; NAT is already in base); the definition bucket must be in the same region, and a new bucket returns 307; cron really triggers with RunType=SCHEDULED; the `op_kwargs` parameter chain works; all quotas are adjustable. **Known constraint**: each execution environment's base stack has 2 NAT = 2 EIP, and the default quota is 5 per region ⇒ at most 2 execution environments per region (the quota increase was refused for the development account) ⇒ 🆕 `spec.networkProfile` standard / economy + a pre-deployment quota preflight + a failure converger that speaks plain language. **Why not "migrate straight to serverless"**: the decision explicitly keeps the classic one and makes a real two-region run the precondition for retiring it; serverless has constraints — a 60-minute single-task limit, 100 workflows per region, no callbacks / branches / cross-DAG sensors — which must be quantified in real runs before a decision.

6octies. ✅ (decided on 2026-09-06: **zero-egress execution environments**; **route settled** after the spike of 2026-09-08) The decision, verbatim: "Aren't these all private addresses? Only one web page service needs to be exposed to the public internet, and that too through CloudFront — why would an EIP address be needed at all?" "It definitely has to go through endpoints and private connectivity." "How large are the traffic charges, and the performance is unstable." ⇒ **Decision**: ① The execution-environment foundation has **zero public egress** — NAT count 0, EIP 0, private subnets `PRIVATE_ISOLATED` with no `0.0.0.0/0` in the route tables; all AWS API calls go through VPC endpoints (gateway endpoints for S3 / DynamoDB are free; the other interface endpoints are PrivateLink). ② 🔴 **The only cross-region path (a task in that region → the home ledger) = a cross-region DynamoDB interface endpoint + a Route 53 private hosted zone**; **no peering / TGW**. This was settled after the spike of 2026-09-08 overturned the original design: the CIDRs of the home VPC and of the three execution-environment VPCs **are all `10.0.0.0/16`** (neither VPC stack passes `ipAddresses`, so they take the CDK default), while peering strictly requires non-overlapping CIDRs ⇒ the original plan physically cannot be built; PrivateLink is an ENI inside my VPC, independent of CIDR, and **does not touch home, does not rebuild existing VPCs, and needs zero product-code changes** (the private hosted zone resolves the default FQDN to the endpoint ENI; `boto3` calls as usual). ③ **Global Tables remain vetoed**: the spike measured a cross-region conditional write `attribute_not_exists` succeeding the first time and **the second write of the same key being refused with `ConditionalCheckFailedException`** ⇒ the job-lock semantics are fully preserved through the endpoint; the ledger tables stay single-region, single-table, with no replication introduced (the reason — cross-region eventual consistency would break the lock semantics — is unchanged, and now there is positive evidence). ④ **No cross-region KMS endpoint**: the target table is CMK-encrypted, and the spike created no cross-region KMS endpoint at all, yet the write still succeeded — SSE-KMS has the DynamoDB service call KMS on the caller's behalf; the caller needs no network reachability. ⑤ 🔴 **Pre-authorization**: the cross-region variant of `ec2:CreateVpcEndpoint` additionally requires **`vpce:AllowMultiRegion`**, and that action **is not matched by wildcards** (both the `NotAction` wildcard of `PowerUserAccess` and the `Action:*` of `AdministratorAccess` were refused in the measurement) ⇒ it must be granted **verbatim**; **decided on 2026-09-08: "grant it only to the CDK cfn-exec role"** (the smallest surface; not to the platform TaskRole, not to the acceptance identity). ⑥ The "network size" tiers of the known constraint above (standard / economy = 2 / 1 NAT) **are retired** by this decision: the field is kept for compatibility with existing descriptors, the `-c exec-env-nat-gateways` input is removed (passing it fails synth, leaving no opening to turn NAT back on), and the wizard no longer shows network size or the EIP preflight. ⑦ **The NAT of the home control-plane VPC is not in this package** (it is the egress for ECS platform tasks pulling images / calling external LLMs; evaluated in a separate package). ⑧ Cost: an interface endpoint is about USD 0.01 / hour / AZ; 10 same-region + 1 cross-region × 2 AZ ≈ USD 145–190 / month / environment, saving about USD 65 / month of NAT + egress traffic; **stability comes first, cost is not the deciding factor** (decided). Contract: [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), revision 3.33, section 3.16; acceptance: eleven dedicated acceptance items (acceptance criteria revision 1.28, the zero-egress segment); construction: its own work package (including the ten measured spike facts); tracked in the roadmap ledger.
