9. ✅ (2026-08-28, the three decisions of the coverage audit) **① 1a**: the remediation of multi-region coverage across the whole platform **is folded entirely into this design as the new section 19, and the design is bumped to the major revision 2.0** (no separate sub-module); **② everything in phase one**: axes A–D of section 19 + the consumption surface + the operations surface + the differentiators (including the RBAC × region / cross-region dependency fan-out / notification masking / one-screen read-only cross-geography overview previously proposed for phase two) **all in phase one, all phase-two deferrals cancelled** (section 17 was re-sequenced accordingly and the consumer-side batch promoted from phase two to phase one); **③ question #2 confirmed**: modeling physical tables = **two dimensions, region × project** — the model definition is global, the structure is pushed down and materialized in every region, inside each region global-knowledge objects land in the `shared` schema (GRANT SELECT to the all-projects role) and project-private ones in `proj_<id>` (sections 19.1 / 19.3 jointly with [the project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 7bis). Basis: the region-coverage audit (the convergent paradigm of six external products without exception + a blind-spot scan of three internal domains). **Still no code is written; careful design + review (made explicit on 2026-08-28).**

**Remaining to confirm (before construction starts; each has a recommended default)**:
7. The approver for execution-environment deletion (default: platform admin);
8. Whether "one codebase, many regions" binds all regions by default or requires an explicit choice (design default = explicit choice; the default is home only).

---

## 19. Multi-region coverage across the whole platform (the remediation of the coverage audit of 2026-08-28 folded in; decisions: 1a fold everything into this document, 2 everything in phase one, 3 question #2 confirmed as two dimensions, region × project)

> - **Origin**: on 2026-08-28 the question was raised, "now that multi-region has been added, how many platform features have been overlooked? It must be benchmarked against mature external commercial products; do not invent on your own." Six parallel tracks (3 external benchmarks via the official
>   documentation + 3 internal blind-spot greps of the real code) produced the region-coverage audit (the full matrix and the file:line evidence are in that audit). **This section turns the audit's conclusions into design**: sections 1–18 make "**which region to execute in**" (the execution axis)
>   solid, but multi-region is a **dimension that cuts across the whole platform**, and 4 axes + the consumption / operations surfaces were systematically missed. **Decided: fold everything into this document, do everything in phase one (no phase two).**
> - 🔴 **The red line is unchanged**: pure design; no code is written and nothing is deployed without confirmation.

### 19.0 External paradigm and core principles (the guiding thread of every design in this section, from six benchmarks without exception)

**The unified paradigm (converged upon without exception by the major cloud data-development suites, cloud data warehouses, lakehouse platforms, cloud analytics warehouses and AWS native services)**: ① data is locked to a region and immutable; ② compute is placed close to the data; ③ **the center touches only metadata, never result data**; ④ cross-region goes through **explicit, governable replication / network connectivity** (not implicit global synchronization); ⑤ enforcement relies on **hard boundaries** (account / region); ⑥ interaction = a **regional portal** (switch region at the top, see that region after switching, no cross-region one-screen aggregation of results). Our existing dual-track residency direction of section 8.5 is fully on this line.

**Core design principles (running through all remediation in section 19)**:
1. 🔴 **Decouple "structure push-down" from "data replication"** (the most important cut; learning the declarative approach of a leading cloud data warehouse while **avoiding its pitfall of bundling DDL + data into one group that moves together**): DDL / structure / modeling definitions are **metadata**; pushing them across regions **does not violate residency**, and they may be pushed down consistently to all regions by default; **data stays in region by default**, and any cross-region physical copy must **pass the residency gate + explicit opt-in + audit**.
2. **The control plane stores only metadata / pointers + aggregates read-only metadata mirrored up per region; results are rendered in their region** (AWS has no global catalog, so fan-out orchestration / aggregated views **must be built by us**; the metadata of the cloud analytics warehouses and of AWS is isolated per region by nature).
3. **Regional-portal interaction + cross-region referenced data shown read-only with a type marker (learning from the shared-catalog pattern of a leading lakehouse platform) + a default-deny binding guard rail + residency validation inserted into the replication wizard (learning from a leading cloud data warehouse) + honest boundaries (lineage / permissions / audit independent per region)**.
4. 🔴 **Everything in phase one (decision 2)**: every item in this section (including the RBAC × region, cross-region dependency fan-out, notification masking and one-screen cross-geography overview previously proposed for phase two) **is phase one**; the phasing of section 17 places them in the four batches (section 19.8); there is no "phase two" bucket any more.

### 19.1 🔴 Axis A — multi-region fan-out of metadata materialization / landing (= the "multi-region modeling" that was asked about; P0, skeleton level)

**Problem**: modeling landing (code tables `dim_code_*`, materialization of dimension / fact table DDL) is an action that **writes physical tables**, yet it is **entirely outside the push-down framework of section 5** — `modeling/redshift.ts:82 landCodeTable` creates in a single workgroup only, `service.ts:254 landed` is **a single global boolean**, and the landing route `codetables/[id]/land` **has no region parameter**. → Region B references a code table / dimension table that exists only in home → **blows up at run time**.

**Design (modeling landing joins push-down at the same level as tasks, directly reusing the model already validated in section 5)**:
- **The model definition is globally unique (consistent with D2) + gains a `targets?: string[]` binding** (corrected in revision 3.1: the original wording was `regions?`; [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.3a-S1/S3, settled on `targets?`; default `[tgt_home]`), isomorphic with the single-definition-many-bindings of section 5.1; code-table / dimension / metric definitions live in the control plane (metadata) and **contain no data values**.
- **Structural DDL is distributed and materialized region by region** (principle 1: structure = metadata, may be pushed to all regions): the signatures of `landCodeTable` / `applyModelTableDDL` / the landing route gain `env: ExecutionEnv`, and **CREATE / COPY is executed region by region** over the bound regions (code-table values are "common language" reference data, small in volume, and may be distributed along with the DDL; the **business data of dimension / fact tables stays in region**, only the structure is pushed). This is precisely "if the table structure is consistent, push it down to each region to perform the modeling" (the original words of the decision) — on AWS, "the same DDL against different regional endpoints = a table created in each" is natural (section 19.0).
- **Landing status per region**: `landed:bool` → **`landedByTarget: {[region]: {version, at, status}}`**; the landing route writes back the per-region status.
- **Included in the version-consistency dashboard (section 5.3)**: modeling landing enters the Multi-Region Dashboard as a class of "push-down-able resource", so that **which region is missing a table or lagging in version is visible at a glance** (governance artifacts are more hidden than tasks; nobody had given code tables a dashboard — a risk the audit named explicitly).
- 🔴 **Jointly with question #2 (the project-isolation design) = two dimensions, region × project** (decision 3): inside each region, **global-knowledge objects (code tables / conformed dimensions) land in the `shared` / `common` schema with GRANT SELECT to the all-projects role (not PUBLIC, not proj_)**; project-private business data lands in `proj_<id>`. That is, option A of question #2, one set per region under multi-region (see section 19.3 jointly with [the project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 7bis).
- **Heterogeneity**: a model backed by a data source that exists only in one region → can only be bound to that region (reusing the data-heterogeneity pre-check of section 5.1 / [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 2bis); the other regions are greyed out.
- ★ **Differentiator A (beyond the major cloud data-development suites)**: their modeling reuse / materialization target axis **has no first-class "geography" dimension** (cross-geography degrades into a manual migration helper). Our **model definitions carry a `regions` dimension and are pushed down declaratively to each region for materialization** — a blank area for them.

### 19.2 🟠 Axis B — region storage keys for result / verdict metadata (added in one go in phase one, preventing silent cross-region contamination)

**Problem**: quality results (`quality/service.ts:255`, pk=fqn), metric values (`metrics/service.ts:210`, pk=metricId + the definition's lastValue), lineage edges (`lineage-service.ts:61`, composite key on fqn), classification (`classification.ts:107`, pk=fqn), the governance health score, sensitive-data findings — **not one storage key carries a region**. The same code running in several regions produces the same names → **they overwrite / interleave each other** (quality trends mix regions, a metric flips between two regions, lineage merges into one edge and impact analysis goes blind); **nothing blows up, the numbers are just wrong** (harder to catch in acceptance than a P0).

**Design (give this batch of "result / verdict" storage keys a region dimension uniformly, in one go)**:
- **Storage keys gain a region**: the pk or sk of QUALITY_RESULTS / METRIC_VALUES / lineage edges / DATA_CLASSIFICATION / findings gains a region segment (on the definition side "change in one place, effective in every region" is unchanged; **the result / verdict side is partitioned by region**); legacy records without a region are attributed to home.
- **Lineage parsing carries the region through** (adding a string suffix as in section 7.2 is not enough): `parseSqlLineage` carries the **execution region** into every parsed ref; take care not to break the invariant "compute-node FQN aligned with sync-node FQN" (handled uniformly by the authoritative FQN function of section 7.2).
- **The health score aggregates per region**: the normalization hack in `health-service.ts`, where `bareKey` strips the first segment of the FQN, **collides head-on with the region-suffix scheme** and must be redone as "group and aggregate by region", never mixing regions into a "global latest".
- **Semantics of definitions / consistency (layering / domains / standards / OM Glossary) gain no region**: the audit confirmed they are global business semantics (a correct single central copy) and they stay unchanged.

### 19.3 🔴 Axis C — per-region semantics of access control / masking (P0; not a suffix, a semantic change)

**Problem**: one grants row (`classification.ts:119`, pk=username sk=fqn) = **passage through every region and every jurisdiction** → a US grant unlocks the same-named EU table = cross-jurisdiction privilege escalation (violating residency); masking (`masking.ts:98`) looks up with a tableFqn that has no region → once the storage side gains a region **the lookup misses → masking silently stops working → PII is exposed in non-home regions**.

**Design (treated as an access-control design change, not stuffed into the FQN function of section 7.2)**:
- **grants per region**: the grant semantics change from "global" to **per region** (the grant key carries the region); **the approval flow carries the region**; **existing grants are migrated** (jurisdiction annotated, split by region); a cross-jurisdiction grant must be explicit (echoing the dual track of section 8.5: granting access to cross-jurisdiction data = explicit informed consent + audit). Learning from a leading lakehouse platform, "permissions do not cross regions; re-grant across regions" (the honest boundary of section 19.0).
- **Masking, two fixes**: ① **the lookup side carries the region** (consistent with the storage side, eliminating the silent failure); ② **masking as a whole sinks to the regional data plane** — cross-jurisdiction results **are not sent back to the center for masking** (masking is currently a central lib operating on result rows; section 8.5 speaks only generally of result egress and never names sinking masking, which is added here); cross-jurisdiction results are masked in the regional API before being returned (track 1, the user's responsibility, still informed + audited).
- 🔴 **RBAC × region promoted to phase one (decision 2; the former gap ④ of section 14 / [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 6.2, had proposed phase two; the deferral is cancelled)**: the authorization axis widens from "project × environment" to "**project × environment × region**"; **regional administrators / separation of duties by region** (who may operate / grant which jurisdictions' regions); a project editor **cannot indiscriminately** push tasks down to any cross-jurisdiction region (regional authorization is required). Lands as a region dimension in `guard.ts` / `constants.ts` + a per-region authorization UI added to the interaction design. ⚠️ This closes the gap found by the audit that "any global admin can operate any jurisdiction".

### 19.4 🔴 Axis D — residency of the audit metadata itself (P0; the only compliance hard fault that had been completely ignored)

**Problem**: `bgp-audit` is a single central table storing operator PII (email / sub) + action / path / detail, and the specification's `auditEgress` writes `{who,region,endpoint,queryId}` into **the same central table**; the requirements, section 5, decree "metadata is reported uniformly to the control-center region". → The audit of cross-jurisdiction operations = **operator PII + the activity stream of access to cross-border data + a queryId that can be traced back to that data**, all sinking into us-east-1 = **a cross-border transfer of personal data**, self-contradictory with section 8.5's "results do not stay at the center". 🔴 **The harder the audit egress wiring is made (the more complete and delete-proof it is written), the more serious this becomes** (section 8.5.0-2 makes audit the sole control of track 1, which is exactly what fixes more cross-jurisdiction access details at the center).

**Design**:
- **Audit is stored in two classes**: ① **non-PII summaries / verdicts / counts / status** (query-id hash, region, row count, duration, conclusion) → may stay at the center (metadata; unified monitoring / compliance dashboards consume it as before); ② **details containing PII / data-subject identity / anything traceable back to the data** (operator email / sub, SQL text, plaintext queryId) → **for cross-jurisdiction regions, kept in a regional audit store per jurisdiction** (in the same frame as the regional result storage of section 8.5.7 ④, RETAIN, deletion protection of section 4.4).
- **Amend the wording of the requirements, section 5**: "metadata is reported uniformly to the center" **gains an exception** — the PII / detail dimensions of the audit stay in region for cross-jurisdiction regions; the center receives only a non-PII summary index (fed back into the hard-boundary table of section 8.5.3 + the specification's `auditEgress`).
- **Tamper-proofing holds per region on its own** (the write-only + deny-delete / deny-update of section 8.5.0-2 is applied equally to the regional audit store).
- ⚠️ **Honest boundary**: audit residency and "audit is the sole control of track 1" are in tension — regional audit adds an operations surface (one tamper-proof audit per region), but this is a hard compliance requirement (section 19.0 ③, the center does not touch personal data).

### 19.5 Consumption / AI surface (cookbook hard-wired to home resource names [P0] + the advisors' fake region parameter [P0] + retrieval / Data API)

- 🔴 **Cookbook (P0; moreover sections 7.3 / the design had judged "recipes" to be "zero change" = a misjudgment, corrected here)**: the seed recipes **hard-wire** home resource names (`bgp-workgroup` / `dev`; `seed/glue.ts` repeatedly insists "write them literally; placeholders strictly forbidden") → they flow into 6 codegen outlets (requirements / GenBI / Flink / ETL / EMR / Copilot). Code generated for region B hard-codes the home warehouse → cannot connect (it blows up the moment the naming SSOT renames anything), or, if boto3 is not pinned to a region, **reads and writes US East data from region B (violating residency)**. **Design**: the schema / workgroup / bucket names in recipes become **platform context injection** (region + project, unified with the naming SSOT of section 3 + the `shared` / `proj_` of the project-isolation design); codegen prompts change to "schema / resources are injected by the platform; do not hard-code; do not GRANT PUBLIC"; **the compiled core-dist copies are redistributed by core-prebuild when the source changes** (this conflicts with the conclusion of section 7.3, "configuration-type, zero change", so section 7.3 must be corrected accordingly). **Doubly blind**: the same place misses both region and project (same root as the project-isolation design's cookbook-public issue; fixed jointly by both designs).
- 🔴 **network-advisor (P0)**: region is a **fake parameter** (declared at `types.ts:44` but ignored); the probe / apply clients are pinned to home at module level; **the one-click fix at `apply.ts:56` performs `ModifyVpcAttribute` / `CreateVpcEndpoint` on the HOME region's VPC** to solve region B's connectivity = a real infrastructure change in the wrong region. **Design**: `req.region` is carried through to `probeEnvironment` / `apply`; a per-region client factory (section 6.1); apply targets the **target region**; the "network advisor extended to probe per region" already mentioned in section 12 must land as probe / apply pass-through, not IAM alone.
- **param-advisor (P1)**: same as network-advisor (fake region parameter, reads home history) → `req.region` pass-through + per-region client.
- **GenBI retrieval filtered by region (P1)**: business definitions / skills (`context.ts:30` / `skills.ts:77`, by projectId with no region), OM descriptions (indexed by bare table name) → same-named tables in several regions collide; a region-B question eats region-A descriptions / skill SQL containing home tables. **Design**: retrieved content is filtered by the **target region** (section 8.2 only solved "where definitions are stored / memory residue"; "filter the content by region at retrieval time" is added here).
- **Anonymous Data API (P1)**: `/api/public/data` is anonymous, the key has no region dimension, the definition has no region dimension → no signal for track 2's "where it computes / how to fail closed" across jurisdictions. **Design**: the DataApi definition + API key carry a **region scope**; the region of an anonymous call is derived from the key / definition (not trusted from a header).

### 19.6 Operations / scheduling surface (do not let regional outages be hidden by aggregation [P1↑] + cross-region cleanup on project deletion + decommissioning / dependencies / notifications)

- 🔴 **A regional outage hidden by cross-region aggregation (P1, may rise to P0)**: alerting `evaluate.ts:124` computes success-rate / no-run by `win#bucket`, **kneading cross-region runs into one number**; the baseline `learn.ts:98` pools them; incidents `store.ts:14` files them together across regions → **a stalled region is averaged away by the healthy ones; no alert, no incident**. **Design (the single-region assumption is baked into the aggregation algorithm; adding a column cannot fix it)**: ① alert-rule scope gains a region, and **success-rate / no-run are bucketed per region**; ② the baseline storage key gains a region, and **an SLA target can be bound per region**; ③ incidents / occurrences are tagged with the region (the same signature in different regions does not merge into one incident); ④ users can configure an alert at the level of "region X has stalled".
- **Cross-region, cross-jurisdiction cleanup on project deletion (P1, a residency-violating residue)**: `deleteProject` deletes only 2 central tables; the per-project `proj_<id>` schema / role / secret of the project-isolation design × the regions of this design → after deletion every region (including cross-jurisdiction ones) **retains schema / credentials / data** = the EU data of a "deleted" project is still in the EU region. **Design**: project deletion **reclaims across regions and jurisdictions** (aligned with the binding=0 pre-check of environment deletion in section 4.4 + clearing proj_ schema / role / secret region by region); deletion of data in cross-jurisdiction regions leaves a trace (echoing the compliance "right to be forgotten").
- **Scheduling decommissioning splits DAGs per region (P1)**: `mwaa-offline.ts:10` deletes from the home bucket / pauses the home MWAA → DAGs in non-home regions **cannot be taken offline → orphan schedules keep running**. **Design**: offline / delete dismantles the DAG separately in **each region the task is bound to** (delete the file in that region's DAG bucket + pause that region's MWAA), symmetric with the per-region distribution of section 5.2.
- **Semantics of cross-region dependency fan-out (P1/P2)**: same-region dependencies are owned (section 6.4); for cross-region dependencies (A@r1 → B@r2) + a downstream twinned in several regions, **which region to trigger / whether all regions** is undefined. **Design**: define the fan-out rules for cross-region dependencies (default: downstream "same region first"; a multi-region twin triggers in each bound region; the boundary of section 9, "no cross-region dependencies in phase one", is refined accordingly into "a cross-region dependency must declare its fan-out strategy explicitly").
- **Notification content masking / per-region channel (P2, a residency side branch)**: at `evaluate.ts:84` the alert message embeds the error text of a cross-jurisdiction run (which may contain data fragments) and leaves the jurisdiction through home SES. **Design**: for cross-jurisdiction runs the notification content **masks the error text / routes through a per-region channel** (regional data does not leave through home SES).
- **Diagnostic evidence, supplementary check (covered, pending verification)**: section 12 and section 8.4 (three CloudWatch read paths) are owned; ⚠️ confirm that **Glue GetJobRun + Redshift DescribeStatement (not CloudWatch) + the region name in the prompt** in `evidence.ts` also build their clients by the execution region.

### 19.7 ★ Differentiator B — one-screen read-only cross-geography metadata overview (beyond the major cloud data-development suites and every benchmarked product)

**Motivation**: every benchmarked product is cut at a single geography (the data map / lineage / quality of the major cloud data-development suites are all locked to the "current Region", with no cross-geography single screen) — a global governance lead **cannot see "which assets and which lineage panorama I have in which regions"**. This is the positive opportunity of section 19.0 ②.

**Design (filling this hole while strictly honoring "results do not cross borders")**:
- **The center mirrors each region's metadata read-only, per region** (AWS has no global catalog; it must be built): looping over the active environments in the registry, **pull read-only** the database / table / column / lineage **metadata** (not data rows) of each region's Glue / LF / OM, and store it at the center **keyed per region** (mirror / pointers, no result data) — **only metadata moves, zero data egress**, in keeping with section 19.0 ③.
- **One-screen cross-geography view**: the data map / lineage / asset inventory offer an "all-region overview" (faceted / colored by region), with drill-down into one region; cross-region data **aggregates no results, only metadata profiles** (asset counts / lineage edges / quality scores side by side per region, learning from the version matrix of section 5.3).
- **Presentation of cross-region referenced data** (learning from the shared-catalog pattern of a leading lakehouse platform): if one region's data is referenced by another region (explicit replication / datashare), it is shown **read-only + with a type marker**, so that "this lives in another region; it is a reference, not a copy" is understood at a glance.
- ⚠️ **Honest boundary**: the one-screen overview is a **metadata panorama**, not "cross-region result aggregation"; opening one region's data still goes through that region's data plane (section 8.5).

### 19.9 Honest note on the scope and workload of "everything in phase one" (expectation management)

Decision 2, "no phase two, finish everything in phase one" — **this section widens phase one of this design from the "execution axis" to the full set of axes "execution + metadata + governance + access control + audit + operations + consumption"**; on top of sections 1–18, which already make this "the largest single module of the platform to date", the phase-one workload **rises markedly by another step**. Honest note: ① axis A modeling push-down + axis C per-region access control + axis D audit regionalization are **new work surfaces** (not parameter additions); ② the joint surface with the project-isolation data-plane layers L2/L3 widens (shared schema + per-region credentials); ③ closure still relies on the registry of section 2 / the naming SSOT of section 3 / the atomic functions of section 8.1 / the guard rails of section 6.1 — the new closure points added by the 4 axes (the modeling-landing push-down entry point, metadata region keys, the access-control region gate, the regional audit sink) must be brought into the mechanical guard rails of section 6.1 (the A7 lint) as well, otherwise the new blast radius runs out of control. **This is the price of the scope decision; it was known and decided to do everything.**

## 20. 🔴 Revision 3.0 foundation-correction ledger (independent architecture review adopted after verification; decided on 2026-08-28: "adopt and unify")

> - **Sources**: an independent architecture review produced by a different AI model (12 priority-0 items and 12 priority-1 items) plus our own self-review in section 19 — two inputs. **Every item was verified one by one by grepping the real code and fetching the official AWS documentation**; only
>   items whose status was **CONFIRMED** were adopted; exaggerated items were downgraded and typographical slips were annotated (see the end of section 20.1).
> - 🔒 **Evidence hygiene**: while verifying the official AWS StartBuild documentation page we found that the page body **contained a prompt injection** (inviting the reader to run a certain CLI command); it was recognized, **neither executed nor trusted**, and only the API facts were taken from the
>   page.
> - 📌 **Naming transition (narrowed in revision 3.0, aligned with the acceptance gate of section 20.4)**: in the **narrative prose** of section 19 and similar sections, the "region dimension" is to be read as the target dimension; but **wherever a field name, key name or type name appears, [the
>   specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md) is authoritative and the text has already been aligned word for word** (`landedByTarget`, `targets[]`, `audit#<targetId>#<day>`, the OpenMetadata service `bgp-redshift-<targetId>`, and so on; the summary table of section 10, the two blocking
>   items of section 14bis.3, section 7.2 and section 2.1 were each corrected in revision 3.0). **The exemption covers prose only, never contract identifiers** — otherwise the "machine-comparable" gate of section 20.4 would fail forever.

### 20.29 🔴 Revision 3.24 **Candidate-region pre-authorization for execution regions (zero manual authorization steps) + root fix of the control-plane guard that broke execution-environment builds for a non-US home region + the region-condition wiring gap of the agent and EMR stacks** (construction batch, 2026-10-19; driven by the record of adding a Frankfurt execution region in the customer's production environment)

- **Trigger**: the customer hit a wall twice while creating an execution region from the web page — ① **the bootstrap-time guard failure**: the two control-plane guards in `infra/bin/app.ts` ran before the execution-environment fork, so for a home region outside the United States the CodeBuild job was rejected by the "default-model geography guard" as early as `cdk bootstrap` (fixed in the development branch: the guards were moved into `assertControlPlaneGuards()`, which is called only on the control-plane branch; three cases were added to section 1c of the offline synth gate, and swapping the old `app.ts` back in turned them red in a real run); ② after the stacks were built, an operator still had to run `redeploy-platform.sh` on the command line to authorize the region (the original design of section 12.1), so the customer's experience was half web page, half CLI. The customer's words: "Even though the new region was created, the authorization step still has to be done by running code by hand — far too cumbersome. This also has to be automated, and it has to be very rigorous and must not affect live functionality."
- **Why not "let CodeBuild authorize the region on the way"**: sections 12.0 and 20.19 settled this — the execution-environment deployment role must never hold write permission on control-plane stacks, and the synth gate asserts that the buildspec never touches the control plane; any path in which the platform triggers a change to its own IAM violates the isolation. The only legitimate automation is to **move the human decision earlier** (naming the candidate regions at install or upgrade time), not to remove the human decision.
- **Implementation** (the revision 3.24 passage of section 12.1): `scripts/lib/execution-regions.sh` (single author of: regions in use per the registry / candidate regions from SSM / regions enabled in the account / their union / validation / choice of the probe region for the IAM gate) · `deploy-all.sh` gained a new step 2.6 and an explicit `-c` in step 6, `redeploy-platform.sh` step 1b accepts `EXEC_REGION_CANDIDATES` (step 3c creates the federated catalog only for regions that really have an execution environment), `redeploy-agents.sh` step 3 uses the same list, and all three scripts `export BGP_EXECUTION_REGIONS` · a second input channel for `requestedRegionCondition()` · platform side: the candidate-regions route returns `controlPlaneEnabled`, the wizard annotates each region and shows one `wizard-region-preauth` line, `regionNotEnabledHint` explains how to declare a candidate region, and the probe gained the item `lake:s3tablescatalog` (reusing the `ensureS3TablesFederatedCatalog` helper introduced when fresh accounts were found to lack the S3 Tables federated catalog; the account is taken from the descriptor; if the catalog is missing it is created, and if creation is refused the item turns red with the service's own message) · `34-add-region-headless.py` skips the authorization step when the region is already authorized and records the page's own wording.
- **Evidence (tests)**: 42 cases in `infra/test/execution-regions/test_lib.sh` (a fake `aws` drives every branch; **after the IAM-gate probe-region fix of 2026-10-22, the probe region for the IAM gate = a region the account has not opted into first / a fixed fallback list / if none can be chosen ⇒ an empty string, which the caller passes to the gate as `none` instead of dying**, cases 17–19 and 24b–24c; including "an unreadable value must not be treated as absent" — the first version put a fallible read inside a pipeline, so `die` only ended the left-hand subshell, the `grep` on the right filtered the error text away and a "home only" list was produced as if nothing had happened; caught on the spot and changed to `v=$(f) || return 1`) · synth gate section 1c (three cases for the bootstrap-time guard failure; mutation check turns red) / section 9c-env (both channels produce the same template, `-c` takes precedence) · vitest: two probe-runner cases (catalog exists ⇒ not created; missing ⇒ the CreateCatalog input points at this region; refused ⇒ red with the service's message and the whole round is not aborted), two control-plane-regions cases, and the seventh seam check of s3tables-catalog · a real-environment rehearsal (a test account, candidate region us-west-1: create from the web wizard → no authorization run → "Check initialization" → "Enable"; results recorded in the project's acceptance records on 2026-10-19).
- **The region-condition wiring gap of the agent and EMR stacks (found incidentally, fixed in the same batch)**: `netadvisor-iam.ts`, `paramadvisor-iam.ts` and `emr-stack.ts` use the same `requestedRegionCondition` exit as the platform stack, but steps 5 and 9 of `deploy-all` and `redeploy-agents.sh` never passed the list ⇒ the skill roles of the network advisor and the parameter advisor and the home EMR role were always authorized for the home region only (when a data source lives in another region the advisors could not probe it). The second channel lets every `cdk deploy` of the same deployment see the same list; when upgrading to a version that contains this batch, **`redeploy-agents.sh` must be run once** (the stacks outside the platform stack are deployed by it).
- **Deliberately not done**: candidate regions are not defaulted to `all-enabled` (rigor over convenience; the operator names them, and `all-enabled` is an explicit option); step 3c does not create federated catalogs for candidate regions (there is nothing in such a region yet, and no object is created for "might be used later"); not a single rule of section 12.0 is changed.
- 🆕 **Revision 3.25: the real-environment rehearsal (2026-10-19 13:3x–14:39Z, a demo-mode test account, candidate region us-west-1) and the probe-contract seam defect**: every criterion was met — the wizard annotates each region / `controlPlaneRegionEnabled` set at registration / build 11 minutes / **authorization step skipped** / "Check initialization" 18 items with 0 red (the federated catalog created by the probe) / enabled to active / post-deploy truth check 16/16. **It caught a seam defect for which the unit tests on both sides were green**: `probe-runner.ts` had gained `lake:s3tablescatalog`, but the single author of the "probe item → component" mapping, `component-contract.ts#CORE_PROBE_ITEMS`, had not registered it ⇒ `isGreenProbeForComponents()` returned false for every environment ⇒ `reconcileAfterProbe` returned `probe-coverage-mismatch` (the new region's probe was all green yet it stayed in provisioning with "Enable" greyed out) and `deriveHealthFromProbe` judged the probe not fully green (existing in-use regions dropped to degraded after a few ticks). A comment in that very file had predicted this consequence, but the only test was a **hand-copied** list of item names. Fix = register the one line + two ratchets (the contract test scans the probe runner's source literals directly; the probe-runner test uses the real run artefact as an assembly check, mocking only AWS); after redeployment the first leader tick of the new code repaired the three environments without any manual action. **Design conclusions**: ① adding a probe item = changing the probe runner and the contract together, and "one fact represented in two places" needs a mechanical consistency check (not a hand copy); ② an upgrade that includes "adding a region within the candidate list" must be walked through the complete web flow in a test account — fail-closed silent defects of this kind are visible only in a real environment, and shipping them to production would degrade every in-use region; ③ any release that contains candidate-region pre-authorization must also contain this fix.

| # | Correction | Verdict | Where it landed (in place) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **An immutable `targetId` replaces "region as primary key"** (the common root of rows 2 and 3) | CONFIRMED | section 0 terminology / section 2.1 sort key + location attributes / section 2.2 registry / section 5.1 `targets[]` |
| 2 | **task-runs primary-key collision**: two targets on the same cron → identical `(taskId, runId)` → the later write overwrites; adding attributes does not solve it | CONFIRMED (stronger than the review stated) | **section 7.1a** (decision: value-level composite **`runId=<raw>#<targetId>`** (suffix) without rebuilding the table + `parseRunId()` + **three read-side closures (sorting moves to a GSI / prefix matching tests `rawRunId` / lexicographic comparison moves to `startedAt`)** + 3 GSIs; alternative: primary-key restructuring) |
| 3 | **The run lock lacks target scope**: different targets competing for the same lock → the ETL `AirflowSkipException` **silently skips the whole run** | CONFIRMED (worse than stated) | section 6.3 + [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.6 (lock identity = **the attribute axis `runLock_<targetId>[_<nodeId>]`**, target carried in the attribute not in the key; the four writers byte-aligned; invariant: same target mutually exclusive / different targets in parallel), section 9 |
| 4 | **The execution plane is not autonomous**: at run time it reads definitions from the home DynamoDB and takes locks in the home DynamoDB (errors raise directly) | CONFIRMED (needs layering) | section 14bis.2 (a)/(b) promised separately + phase-one mitigations (manifest snapshot / fail-closed lock / outbox retransmission) |
| 5 | **Control-plane state is not only DynamoDB** (Cognito / OpenMetadata RDS / OpenSearch / Secrets / AgentCore) | CONFIRMED | section 14bis.2, the DR hook of section 14 (the claim "cold-standby cost is manageable" withdrawn) |
| 6 | **StartBuild overrides pierce the deployment isolation** (`buildspecOverride` can be inline) | CONFIRMED (official AWS documentation) | section 12.0 (IAM deny of overrides + narrow-permission broker + negative probes; the deployment-isolation item of section 16 upgraded) |
| 7 | **`result/[id]` has zero ownership check = an IDOR in the live system** | CONFIRMED | **section 20.2** queryHandle (uniformly replacing the mapping of the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md) specification, section 3.6) |
| 8 | **A single audit partition + a bounded Scan silently misses rows** | CONFIRMED | section 7.1a (GSI + Scan→Query + audit sharded by region/tenant/day) |
| 9 | **Contradiction in the masking layer**: [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md) masks before rendering in the browser vs section 19.3 pushing masking down to the regional side | CONFIRMED (internal inconsistency) | **section 20.3** authoritative masking = the server side of the regional data plane; the browser is defense-in-depth only |
| 10 | **No single authoritative contract among the documents** ([the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md) still cited revision 1.6 as its basis; RBAC phase-one / phase-two conflict; wording on cross-region dependencies) | CONFIRMED | **section 20.4** frozen contract (section 9 wording disambiguated; synchronization of the specification and the interaction design in section 20.4) |
| 11 | The lifecycle lacks failed/draining, deletion has a TOCTOU, the deploy lock has no lease renewal or fencing, non-home must never fall back to home | CONFIRMED | section 2.2 (fail-closed, no fallback to home) + **section 20.5** |
| 12 | Data-source location ≠ permission to read across regions (separate source / exec / sink positions + an egress policy) | PARTIAL (reasonable reinforcement) | section 19.8 merged into the joint surface with project isolation; a `source-type × engine × target` capability matrix listed as a specification deliverable |

**Downgraded / rejected (not accepted wholesale)**: ① **"six vendors with zero exceptions" = a typographical slip** — the "6" refers to **six research paths** (three external fetches of official documentation + three internal greps); the comparison table actually lists **five vendors**; a genuine nitpick, not load-bearing, annotated in the region-coverage audit note. ② **A leading lakehouse platform's managed disaster-recovery feature (reported able to replicate ACLs across regions since 2026-08-20) = a post-cutoff, web-only, point-in-time claim that cannot be verified against code**; it is **not** taken as a verdict that "permissions do not cross regions" is outdated, and is downgraded to "an external signal in doubt". ③ **Half of the review's track-classification item — "the track is determined entirely by forgeable headers" — is exaggerated**: the highest-risk boto3/MWAA path is already guarded by **a DAG-level region pin + an independent audit channel** (not by guessing headers), and `classifyTrack` also carries Cognito / API-key identity bits; **but the other half, "retrieving results has no ownership check", holds completely** (section 20.2). ④ Most priority-0 items of the review are **"design not finalized / not aligned"** (0 of the 4 multi-region construction phases implemented) and are handled as **pre-construction closure items**, **without overturning the skeleton** (the review's own conclusion was also "the direction is feasible").

### 20.2 Result ownership: a server-side queryHandle (fixes the live IDOR, unified with the project-isolation specification, section 3.6)

**Current defect (unrelated to multi-region, present today)**: `redshift/result/[id]/route.ts:11,28` **fetches results by statementId alone, with no owner / project / target check**; `resolveIdentity` is used only for column masking ⇒ **any logged-in user who knows a statementId can read another user's query results**.

**Decision**: when a statement is submitted, the server issues an **unforgeable queryHandle** stored as **`{handleId(PK), statementId, targetId, projectId, ownerSub, jurisdiction, expiresAt(TTL≈36h)}`** (🔴 **`handleId` is the partition key and must not be omitted**); result retrieval **must carry the handle**, the server checks owner / project / target, and a mismatch is **403**; **every submission path that produces a "retrievable result" is uniformly wrapped by `submitStatement()`** — the criterion is **whether the statement id leaves the server toward the client**: **class A (leaves the server, must issue a handle) = `redshift/execute`, `athena/query`, `sync/execute-ddl`, asynchronous data retrieval of Ask-Your-Data, data-service/execute**; **class B (does not leave the server, ledger only) = modeling / metrics / quality / sensitive-scan**; on the retrieval side, besides `redshift/result/[id]`, **`athena/query/[id]/results` must be fixed in the same batch (an isomorphic IDOR)** (implementation level in [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.7), otherwise legitimate paths would be wrongly rejected by fail-closed (this is exactly the same problem as a blocking item of the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md); the two are merged into one mechanism).
- **Replaces and unifies** the `statementId→projectId` mapping of the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md) specification, section 3.6 (same mechanism, extended with `targetId`, `ownerSub` and `jurisdiction`).
- **Track classification does not use forgeable headers as an authorization criterion**: background and service calls switch to an **independent workload identity** (service principal / API key / signed task token); `x-bgp-region` and `Origin` serve only as **routing and audit labels** (consistent with section 8.5.0, "the track criterion depends on the caller type").
- ⚠️ **Can be fixed independently first**: this IDOR does not depend on the multi-region rework; it is recommended as an **independent security fix, scheduled first** (code is still written only after confirmation).

### 20.3 Unifying the authoritative masking layer (resolving the contradiction between the interaction design and section 19.3)

**The contradiction, confirmed**: [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md), section 4bis.2, says "dynamic masking in the presentation layer, masked before rendering (less exposure even if results reach the browser)"; section 19.3 says "masking is pushed down to the regional data plane, the regional API masks before returning"; and **the current code, `result/[id]:37`, masks centrally** (across jurisdictions = raw rows have already entered the US East region).

**Decision (three layers, each in its place)**: ① **authoritative masking = the server side of the region where the data lives** — across jurisdictions it **must** happen in the regional data plane, and raw rows **enter neither the control plane nor the browser**; ② **central masking** is kept only for home / same-jurisdiction targets (status quo); ③ **browser masking = a defense-in-depth backstop**, **not the only means and not admissible as a compliance basis**.

**Hardening**: `masking.ts` is currently **fail-open** (on an exception it returns the raw rows) ⇒ declare the **cross-component invariant** "the region/target of the classification write side and of the masking read side must agree", and **when they disagree, switch from fail-open to fail-closed for sensitivity levels L3/L4** (section 20.6, row E).

### 20.4 Freezing the authoritative document contract (an item of the independent architecture review)

- **Division of responsibility**: **the design = decisions and invariants** (this document); **[the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md) = the single implementation-level contract** (tables / APIs / state machines / migrations / acceptance); **[the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md) = interaction only, and may not set a scope of its own**.
- **Follow-up synchronization in this round**: ① the header of [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md) changes its basis from **revision 1.6 to revision 3.0** and takes over sections 19 and 20 (tables gain `targetId`, the composite `runId`, GSIs, queryHandle, broker, lock identity, draining/fencing); ② [the interaction design](./multi-region-execution-interaction.md): region **RBAC moves from "phase two" to "phase one"** (settled in section 19.3), the masking layer is corrected per section 20.3, and **the region scope no longer treats localStorage as authoritative** (a priority-1 item of the independent review: the `targetId` is carried by the URL / the resource identity instead and cross-checked by the server against the resource; localStorage holds only the default preference, to prevent cross-talk between browser tabs); ③ the feature map gains rows for sections 19 and 20; ④ the section 19.8 ledger is closed item by item.
- **Acceptance gate (🔴 wording narrowed in revision 3.0 so that it is genuinely reachable)**: the scope of machine comparison = **contract-level identifiers** — ① table names / key names / field names / type names (such as `targetId`, `targets[]`, `landedByTarget`, `runLock_<targetId>`, `audit#<targetId>#<day>`); ② construction-phase codes and acceptance-item codes; ③ counting assertions (12 privilege-escalation surfaces, 6 `GRANT PUBLIC`, 16 fail-open sites, 9 metadata FQNs, 13 row-creating write points + 14 write-backs). **Narrative prose is excluded** — if old wording such as "region dimension" appears in the prose of section 19 and similar sections of the design, it is read as "target" under the naming transition of section 20 (**but wherever it appears in a field / key / type-name position, the specification is authoritative and the text must match word for word**).
  - 🔴 **This gate must be carried by an acceptance item (a gap left open by an earlier spike)**: the multi-region acceptance criteria gain a **doc-consistency sweep** (a script compares the three identifier classes above across the five documents — design, specification, interaction design, acceptance criteria and feature map — and any conflict is a FAIL).

### 20.5 Lifecycle draining/fencing + an honest statement of product positioning

- **State machine completed**: `registered → initializing → testing → active → degraded/failed → draining → decommissioning → deleted` (adding **failed** [a failed build no longer sits in initializing indefinitely], **degraded**, **draining** and **upgrading**).
- **Closing the deletion TOCTOU**: initiating deletion **atomically enters `draining`** (new bindings / new schedules / new runs are refused); **re-validated** during approval; **a third validation + a fencing epoch** before destruction (even if an old operation resumes, the expired epoch makes it **unable** to write state or destroy newly created resources).
- **Deploy lock**: `deploy-lock#<targetId>` gains **lease renewal + a fencing token** (a long build is not taken over because its lease expired); `operationId` is idempotent; desired/observed are separated with continuous reconciliation (sections 2.1 / 2.2).
- 🔴 **Product positioning (adopting the grading of section 1.2 of the independent review; honest externally)**: this module delivers **"a federation of regional execution" + "soft residency (informed consent + audit) / optional hard residency"**; **it does not promise global HA/DR** (single-region control plane; Cognito / OpenMetadata RDS / OpenSearch not replicated; no RPO/RTO or failover rehearsal). **Product copy and customer commitments must not imply DR or hard residency**; hard residency (the option in section 8.5.6) and DR are each **separate projects**.

### 20.6 Our own section 19 self-review folded in (not covered by the independent review; complementary)

| # | Problem | Where the correction lands |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | 🔴 **The per-region privileged (admin/DDL) credential factory is missing** — `getRedshiftSecretArn` is **pinned to home + a process-level single-value `_arn` cache + a single secret `redshift!bgp-namespace-admin`** (`admin-secret.ts:10-25`); yet per-region materialization of tables in section 19.1, per-region schema cleanup on project deletion in section 19.6, and the per-region `shared`/`proj_` schemas of the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md) **all hang on it**. **This is a new load-bearing blocking item created by section 19 itself** | Promoted to a **first-class mechanism**: the credential factory resolves and caches by **`(targetId, project)`** (unified with the composite key of section 15); **establishing and rotating regional admin/DDL credentials joins the new operations surface** (merged with the operations-plane credential item of section 8.5.7 ⑨) |
| **B** | 🔴 **Section 19.1 "code-table values shipped together with the DDL" violates principle 1 of section 19.0** (data stays in its region by default; cross-region copies must pass a gate): `landCodeTable` pushes `INSERT VALUES` (real enumeration data) together with the DDL, without any classification gate | 🔴 **Withdrawn in revision 3.20 (2026-09-05, decision record 6quinquies: no legal gate)** — the original correction, "code-table values pass the same residency classification gate as business data before being shipped across regions (per-code-table opt-in / tagged 'reference data shareable in all regions')", is void: landing a code table on a cross-jurisdiction target follows **the same path** as on a same-jurisdiction target, and the landing action writes the regional audit (`entry=modeling-land`); the `residency-blocked` observed state is removed accordingly |
| **C** | **Landing has no structural-drift check**: tables are created with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` (`redshift.ts:68,180`), so a pre-existing heterogeneous table of the same name on the target is **silently skipped**, while `landedByTarget` still records "landed v3" → the dashboard of section 5.3 is **falsely green** | After landing, **compare the physical schema/version** and write back the **real** (observed) `landedByTarget`; mismatches become red cells on the dashboard |
| **D** | **An unacknowledged conflict with the cookbook doctrine**: the seed recipe is **a standing rule that mandates hard-coded real values and strictly forbids placeholders** (the outcome of the root fix of an earlier crack, crack B), while section 19.5 asks for "platform context injection" = a direct reversal of that doctrine | Mechanism made explicit: injection happens **at generation time** — the platform substitutes `targetId` → the real workgroup / schema / bucket names **before** handing over to the LLM / artefact; **the artefact still contains real values and no `<placeholder>`**, so the two coexist |
| **E** | **`maskRows` is fail-open + no contract for region consistency across the 4 hops**: if any hop of scan→findings→classification→masking loses the target, PII leaks silently | Declare the cross-component invariant + **L3/L4 become fail-closed** (section 20.3) |
| **F** | **Axis B has not decided pk-vs-sk per table; the region/target segment of the FQN must sit at the same position in both the OpenMetadata and the platform conventions** (the `bareKey`/`fqnVariants` hard bridge would break) | Section 19.2 adds "**per table**, decide whether the target goes into the pk or the sk + the read path threads the target through"; the FQN segment is **inserted at the same position** and `bareKey`/`fqnVariants` are redefined |

### 20.8 🔴 Revision 3.2 external-audit remediation ledger (2026-08-29; the audit report is the first external review of the three module designs and the standalone security fix)

> **Nature**: an external session was asked to independently audit the three module designs + the already-started code of the standalone security fix; its conclusion was **No-Go**, listing 11 P0 / 11 P1 / 3 P2 items. We re-verified each one against the real code and the real documents (**not by reasoning**): **all 14 sampled items hold, zero false positives**; two are even more serious than reported. **We agreed with its No-Go verdict.**
>
> 🔴 **The one thing most worth remembering**: it found **a whole class of things that our own "six rounds of convergence verification, all green" had missed** — our version-consistency check compared only the version line of each document header and **never the basis line** (measured: all 5 wrong); the negative list lacked `byTarget(` (and it is a **substring** of `byTaskTarget`, so a crude grep is masked), the phrases "no new tables", "section 15 is authoritative" and "5 class-A items"; the `region→targetId` back-propagation was grepped by function name and missed `resolveWithProvenance`. **So the phrase "all green" must itself be discounted: green means only the criteria we thought of at the time.**

| Severity | Audit item | Our re-verification (evidence) | Disposition in this round |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Attaching a Flow Log to an **imported** shared VPC | ✅ Holds, and **more serious than reported**: the report judged "risk" from static CDK evidence; in reality `BgpAuditStack` was already CREATE_COMPLETE and a flow log `fl-<id>` **ACTIVE / TrafficType=ALL** was attached to a VPC owned by another workload's stack. While `vpc-stack.ts` said "import mode never touches someone else's VPC", `audit-stack.ts` attached it unconditionally | ✅ **Fixed and removed from the live account**: `AuditStackProps.vpcImported` + import mode does not attach by default (`-c vpcFlowLogOnImported=1` is an explicit opt-in); `cdk deploy` had CloudFormation delete that flow log; the measured flow-log count in the account is back to **0** |
| P0 | cancel/stop bypassed by omitting `handleId` | ✅ Holds (**a hole we wrote ourselves**): `if(handleId){validate}else{editor fallback}` — the criterion was "whether the client is willing to send the parameter" | ✅ **The fallback was deleted entirely**; the handle is unconditionally required. Cost: at the moment of rollout, pre-existing in-flight statements cannot be cancelled through the API (statements are short-lived; worst case they run to natural completion) |
| P0 | The submission entry point authenticates but does not authorize | ✅ Factually holds (`redshift/execute` had only `resolveSubmitContext`). ⚠️ On degree we add one sentence: it is a **pre-existing defect**, listed long ago in the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md) specification (line 67) as an item of the project-isolation security phase, not a regression introduced by the standalone fix; **but we accept the conclusion** — the standalone fix only sealed "how results are retrieved", not "who may make SQL run", so claiming "the IDOR is fixed for good" was an overstatement | ✅ Per the contract at lines 67–68 of the project-isolation specification, added `requireProject(editor)` + `recordAudit` to `redshift/execute` and `sync/execute-ddl`; and **deleted `resolveSubmitContext()`** — it treated "what the header claims" as an authorization basis, and keeping it would be a trap |
| P0 | The residency guard fails open to home | ✅ Holds: [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md) (line 185) literally read `registry.getTarget(targetId) ?? homeTarget`, directly opposed to sections 1.1/2.2 "a non-ready target always throws" ⇒ an unknown / deleted / stale target is treated as home and **the whole cross-jurisdiction guard is skipped** | ✅ Changed to `requireReadyTarget(targetId)`. ⚠️ Noted against ourselves: the first version overcorrected and also removed the `jurisdiction` default of home (a non-home same-jurisdiction target would be misjudged as cross-jurisdiction = functional collateral damage), fixed back on the spot |
| P0 | Three credential classes unimplementable + raw secret returned | ✅ Both hold: `stmt` is validated although it is not an input parameter; the final `return cached(admin)` hands the raw admin secret to the caller ⇒ a one-off check of one statement **cannot constrain subsequent SQL**, and the allow-list becomes decoration | ✅ **New section 3.9a `executeAdminDdl()`**: validation and execution are closed inside one function, the credential never leaves the function, the allow-list is by **operation enumeration** rather than by guessing at SQL; the admin-ddl branch of `getPrivilegedCred` now **throws directly** (so nobody can write it back from the old draft) |
| P0 | The target dimension of model configuration is not threaded through | ✅ Holds, **our previous back-propagation was not done cleanly**: line 114 passed `region` while the signature already said `targetId` (an undefined variable), `resolveWithProvenance(scope, region?)` still used region, and the probe key `ai-model#probe#<modelId>` **had no target dimension** (Bedrock enablement is account × region scoped ⇒ different accounts in the same region would mix probe results) | ✅ All three fixed; the probe key is fixed as `ai-model#probe#<targetId>#<modelId>`, **shaped on target from phase one** (avoiding a key migration at consumption-side activation) |
| P0 | The authoritative order of section 17 omits model configuration | ✅ Holds (we had noticed it ourselves in the previous round but did not fix it) | ✅ Section 17 now explicitly includes **the first two model-configuration phases after the shared-foundation batch**, **the per-target model-key activation in the same batch as the consumption-side phase** (which hard-depends on it), and **the per-capability phase marked out-of-scope** (no longer left dangling as "phase two"); and the **4 restatements of the sequence** in section 15, section 18, the [model-configuration design](./model-configuration.md), the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md) and the feature map were all turned into pure pointers (the sequence may exist only in section 17) |
| P0 | The lock is still released at run time while the backend is running | ✅ Holds, **the evidence is harder than the report's**: the code's own comment says "release the job lock on terminal state / **or when polling is exhausted** (finally)" (`job-service.ts:285,423`); `LEASE_MS=90min` with no renewal; windows Redshift≈4min / Glue≈40min / EMR≈60min. **One more key fact the report did not mention**: the Python executor (`runtime-lib._exec_sql_once` on MWAA) polls **without bound** in `while True` and releases only after confirming a terminal state ⇒ **this defect is specific to the Node side**, exactly those 4 bounded loops | ✅ **Core fixed**: new `renewRunLockIfDue()` (renews during polling, so the lease cannot expire while we are still watching) + `keepRunLockForReconcile()` (**never release when polling is exhausted** — at that moment the backend state is unknown and the only safe action is to keep holding the lock, with the lease as backstop); all 4 Node loops (Glue / Redshift / backfill / EMR) changed to "**release only after a confirmed terminal state**"; if renewal fails (lock taken over) stop writing back the terminal state, so as not to corrupt it; EMR lock ownership moved from the caller's `.finally()` down into `poll()` (the SPI gains an optional 4th parameter). 4 unit tests pin the semantics (one asserts directly that "after exhaustion a second run still cannot take the lock"). ⚠️ **Still owed**: the fencing generation (`lockEpoch`) and a durable reconciler — only with them can "unknown" converge to "confirmed terminal" and allow a safe early release |
| P0 | The `#` of the composite runId enters the HTTP path unencoded | ✅ Holds: `workflow/[id]/page.tsx:78,90,121` concatenate it raw | ⬜ Not fixed (belongs to the first multi-region construction phase, the lossless foundation refactor; registered) |
| P1 | The GSI projects `durationMs`, but the real code writes `duration` | ✅ Holds (`job-service.ts:295,397,432`) ⇒ projecting an attribute that does not exist | ✅ Fixed |
| P1 | An acceptance item points to a non-existent item (the referenced list ends one short) | ✅ Holds (the definitions go only up to item 11). **One more the report did not mention**: in the same module, the shared-foundation construction batches and the acceptance items **share the same letter-number scheme — two sets of identically named codes side by side** | ✅ The dangling reference fixed; the naming collision recorded |
| P1 | `redshift/schemas` has no guard | ✅ Holds (`requireProject` / `resolveIdentity` zero hits, and it deliberately enumerates the whole database with the shared admin superuser) | ✅ **Added `requireProject(viewer)`**; and the project-isolation list of privilege-escalation surfaces **grew from 12 to 13** (this endpoint was not on the list before). ⚠️ Still owed (project-isolation security phase): **trim the returned set** by the project's schema allow-list — members can still see every schema today; real closure waits for the `proj_<id>` data-plane isolation to land |
| P2 | Version pointer drift | ✅ **5/5 all hold** | ✅ All fixed, and "basis-line version consistency" promoted to a mechanical gate in section 8ter |
| P2 | `byTarget` residue / "4 vendors" yet listing ①–⑤ / reference to a non-existent `lib/workflow/run-lock.ts` | ✅ All hold (the real code confirms that workflow reuses the generalized `etl/run-lock.ts`; there is no separate lock module) | ✅ All fixed |
| Report section 8 | The acceptance criteria still say "5 class-A items" | ✅ Holds (the multi-region acceptance criteria, line 29; the project-isolation acceptance criteria, lines 43/103) | ✅ All changed to 3 |

**Items we did not verify one by one, honestly recorded as "likely to hold but not confirmed"**: the Athena / S3 / Lake Formation cross-project chain (citing 6 files), the reverse order of permission revocation and the migration state machine (two priority-0 items), and seven further priority-1 items. Given 14/14 sampled with zero false positives, we lean to believing that most hold.

**Still unfixed, to be scheduled as separate batches (not hidden)**: the unencoded `#` in runId; splitting expand / switch / revoke in the project-isolation security phase; the migration state machine; Athena / S3 / Lake Formation project isolation; metadata leakage through status polling; the failure semantics of `issueHandle` (two-stage PENDING/ACTIVE); separating read and cancel permissions; the targetId length budget (44 + `bgp-redshift-copy-role-` = 67 > the IAM limit of 64); the hollow promise of cross-account / cross-partition support; checking the acceptance scripts into the repository; the fencing / reconciler tail of the lock fix.

### 20.9 🔴 Revision 3.3 external audit **round 2** (code review of the first construction batches) remediation ledger — options A + B + C decided

> **Nature**: the external auditor performed a second round on **code already landed** (the target-identity registry batch, and the controlled-execution and composite-key cache batches),
> report: the second external review, conclusion **No-Go** (P0×4 / P1×4 / P2×3);
> remediation proposal: the root-remediation proposal for the two blocking items that remained open (the regex guard and the legacy fallback).
>
> **We re-verified each one against the real code: 11/11 hold, zero false positives.** We also ran a reverse credibility check: the report's figures for "items passing on our side"
> are **exactly right** (7 files / 92 tests, per file 18/23/13/13/13/10/2), and "16 fail-open sites" measured 16 on our side too.
>
> - 🔴 **The one thing most worth remembering**: the lesson of the previous round was "all green only means all the criteria we thought of are green"; this round proves
>   that **we even pick the soft ones among "the criteria we thought of"**. Physical evidence: we wrote a test called "the reverse: a keyword hidden after a literal must still be caught",
>   **and it is green** — because the payload we chose was `'x'; SELECT…` rather than `'--'; SELECT…`; our implementation
>   **strips comments first, then masks strings**; the former never reaches that ordering, only the latter does. **Code and test come from the same mental model,
>   so the test necessarily inherits the code's blind spots**; mutation testing too only proves that "the damage I thought of is caught by the tests I thought of".

| Severity | Audit item | Our re-verification (against real code, not by reasoning) | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0** | The admin-DDL regex guard can be bypassed; operation and statement are not bound | ✅ **All 5 payloads measured `ALLOWED`**, confirmed. And **more serious than reported**: `buildLandStatements` (code-table landing) genuinely generates `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` / `DELETE FROM` / `DROP TABLE` / `INSERT INTO … VALUES`, while our `assertNotUserDataRead` **blanket-bans `INSERT`/`DELETE`** ⇒ **whoever wires this guard into code-table landing kills that feature on the spot**. In other words "too weak against attack, too strong against legitimate function" — wrong in both directions at once | ✅ **Contract changed** (section 3.9a, revision 3.3: the public interface accepts only a structured `AdminDdlRequest`, SQL is generated from module-private templates, the regex is downgraded to non-authorizing defense-in-depth; section 4.6 splits code-table landing into two layers). ⬜ **The rework of the controlled-execution batch is still to do; this item explicitly remains open** |
| **P0** | The legacy fallback is non-monotonic + permanently preserved by a process cache without TTL | ✅ Holds. Probe measured: after a target's capability switched from missing to ready, **the second call still returned the old admin with `degraded=true`** (cache hit, no re-evaluation). The root is that we used **negative inference** (`!capabilities.includes(...)`) to decide a permission | ✅ **Contract changed** (new section 3.9b credential-isolation state machine + four fields in section 1.1; the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 3.4, synchronized word for word). ⬜ Code still to do; **this item remains open** |
| **P0** | Registry positive caching + eventually consistent reads bypass draining; the stale check does not exist | ✅ Holds. Probe measured: after another instance set the target to `draining`, this instance's second call **still returned `active` with 0 additional DynamoDB calls**, and `ConsistentRead=undefined` | ✅ **Fixed (remediation batch, revision 3.4)**: the admission path **never caches + `ConsistentRead:true`** (simpler than "split into two layers": the resource bag comes back in the same read, and the second-layer cache serves only callers that skip admission); [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.1, gains an **executable definition of stale** (`probe.at`→`updatedAt`, 15 minutes, non-home only, a missing time source ⇒ stale); the list gains `ConsistentRead` + pagination; **GSIs forbidden for admission** written into the contract and the comments. 🔴 **A cross-instance invalidation bus is explicitly not built** — section 3.9b offered "cross-instance invalidation **or** a strongly consistent cutover gate"; with the latter, admission has no cross-instance staleness window (and incidentally keeps the `credentialEpoch` of section 3.9b always fresh). ⬜ **Conditional updates on the administrative write paths belong to the second-region minimal-loop phase** (those routes do not exist yet; not adding write functions with no callers just to close an item = not repeating the "zero callers" mistake) |
| **P0** | Zero production calls into the controlled-execution batch; the closed executor does not exist ⇒ the first-round credential item cannot be closed | ✅ Factually holds. ⚠️ On degree we add: **zero callers is the design scope section 17 gave that batch ("idle")**, not a deviation from plan; the real defect is **our claim** — this design's version header said "fixed the three credential classes with a controlled executor", which reads as closed, while in fact the design draft changed, the code was not wired, and **the 16 old paths are untouched** | ✅ The claim corrected (this section + the wording of option C). ⬜ Real closure in the project-isolation supply-layer phase / the multi-region lifecycle phase |
| **P1** | On its first read request, home is written as an incomplete but `ready=true` permanent descriptor | ✅ Holds, and **more serious than reported**: `REDSHIFT_SECRET_ID` appears **0 times** in `infra/lib/platform-stack.ts` — we were reading an environment variable CDK **never injects** ⇒ the home descriptor necessarily lacks `redshiftSecretArn` ⇒ `legacySingleAdminSecretArn()` throws as soon as it is called. `AWS_ACCOUNT_ID` is injected (line 757) but we ignored it and wrote an empty account | ✅ **Core fixed (remediation batch, revision 3.4)**: ① `getHomeTarget()` is **read-only**; a missing or incomplete descriptor fails closed and points at the seed entry point (the unit test asserts that `putMock` **was never called**); ② `ensureHomeTarget()` → `seedHomeTarget(complete descriptor)`: **the logic that guessed the resource bag from env was deleted wholesale** (which also eliminated "reading the `REDSHIFT_SECRET_ID` that CDK never injects" ⇒ **no env injection needs to be added**); the full-package validation of section 3.1a runs before writing, **and nothing is written if validation fails**; ③ `ConditionalCheckFailed` **is no longer always treated as success** — a strongly consistent read-back is compared and `created / already-complete / conflict-incomplete` is returned; ④ new `reconcileTarget(patch,{expectedVersion})` conditional update (identity fields and `ready` may not be changed). ⬜ **The real deployment-time caller belongs to the second-region minimal-loop phase** (`emrSecurityGroupId` must be queried at run time; `components` depends on the real deployment manifest — `infra/bin/app.ts` builds Realtime / Flink / EMR / OmIngestion conditionally by context). **Until it lands, home is not seeded and `getHomeTarget()` always fails closed; this is deliberate** (zero production callers ⇒ zero behaviour change) |
| **P1** | The implementation unilaterally shrank the targetId limit to 16; a legal id produces an illegal DNS slug | ✅ **Both hold**: [the specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.1a, **still says `{1,40}` to this day** (we changed the implementation without synchronizing the authoritative text); `tgt_a_`→`tgt-a-`, `tgt__`→`tgt--`, **not legal DNS labels** (first and last must be alphanumeric). Our budget test asserted **our own constant**, so it could never discover this kind of drift | ✅ **Fixed (remediation batch, revision 3.4)**: the specification, section 1.1a, fixes the format **`^tgt_[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9_]{0,14}[a-z0-9])?$`** (40→16 unified into the authoritative text + the body must start and end alphanumeric); `toTargetSlug()` **additionally asserts that the product is a legal DNS label** (not just a replace); added `isDnsLabel/isS3BucketName/isCfnStackName` and real-name assembly tests for the three exits; 🔴 new **`__tests__/contract-drift.test.ts` parses the specification directly** and compares the regex / lengths / thresholds / core list / component enumeration / state machine / GSI naming — the mechanical countermeasure to the root cause "comparing implementation constants against themselves" |
| **P1** | `governance-read` is only a forgeable declarative marker | ✅ Holds: the [project-isolation design](./project-isolation.md), section 3.4, requires four closures (read-only identity / return-shape constraint / strong RBAC / end-to-end audit); we did only (a) | ⬜ To fix: change to an opaque, controlled read-only execution capability |
| **P1** | Descriptor read-back is only a TypeScript cast, with no runtime validation | ✅ Holds (`as ExecutionTarget`), and compounds with the home-descriptor item: the home seed itself could fabricate an incomplete descriptor that passes the checks | ✅ **Fixed (remediation batch, revision 3.4)**: new [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md) **section 3.1a** + `descriptorViolation()` (zod full-package + cross-field invariants): three-way identity equality (`pk`/`sk`/`targetId`/requested value), the home invariant **in both directions**, 12-digit accountId, region form, **partition↔region cross-check**, components must contain core, **core resources named item by item**, **the partition / region / account segments of ARNs in resources must belong to this target**; unknown extra attributes allowed (forward compatibility); invalid ⇒ fail-closed + `console.error` alert; 🔴 `getTargetRaw` is the exception and **does not validate** (operators must be able to see a bad item in order to repair it) |
| **P2** | The `components/capabilities` contract says SS while the implementation uses `string[]`; the GSI sort-key attribute name disagrees with the contract; the list has no pagination | ✅ All three hold (`registry.ts:73-74` / `database-stack.ts:393` uses `sk` / no `LastEvaluatedKey`) | ✅ **Fixed (remediation batch, revision 3.4)**: ① contract `SS`→**`L`** (fixing on the implementation: the DocumentClient reads a DynamoDB Set back as a JavaScript `Set`, and `JSON.stringify` yields `{}`, which would make the control-plane API return empty; also a DynamoDB Set may not be empty). ② GSI naming: **change the contract, not the IaC** — the sort key reuses the base table's `sk` attribute (whose value is the targetId), avoiding storing the same value under two attribute names + no index deletion and recreation; and stating that **GSIs are eventually consistent and forbidden for admission**. ③ Both list functions page through `LastEvaluatedKey` to the end, and **throw** beyond 100 pages rather than returning a possibly incomplete result |
| **P2** | The "no cross-target value mixing" of the cache batch is currently proven by "non-home refused", not by resolving two real targets | ✅ Holds | ✅ **Added (remediation batch)**: each of the three sites completed — `admin-secret` (serial convergence + isolation of two project slots), **new `zeroetl-secret-cache.test.ts`** (composite key / non-home refused / negative-result caching / **invalidation after write** / in-flight concurrency), **new `platform-env-sg-cache.test.ts`** (positive results cached, **negative results not cached**, recovery after jitter, in-flight concurrency). 🔴 **Writing the tests caught and fixed a real defect**: zero-ETL invalidation after write cleared only the slot whose `project` is empty ⇒ the `null` cached per project stayed, and that project kept falling back to IAM without an error — changed to `invalidatePrefix(targetId + "#")` (**invalidation granularity must be ≥ cache granularity**). ⚠️ **Two things honestly recorded as untestable**: (a) "two real targets" still cannot be tested (all three sites still accept only home; non-home is refused outright); (b) **in-flight concurrency** of `admin-secret` cannot show its real behaviour in this harness (the resolver uses a dynamic `import()`; under vitest the second concurrent import is refused by the mock registry and swallowed by the site's `catch{}` into "no secret") ⇒ in-flight concurrency is tested at the `ProcessCache` layer instead, rather than writing a case that "looks like it tests concurrency but actually asserts a harness artefact" |
| **P2** | Our commit "specifically correcting the call-surface count" **is still wrong** | ✅ Holds. The truth is **16 sites / 14 files**; we wrote 17/15; the over-count is exactly `privileged-cred.ts` — it mentions that function name only in a **comment**. **We got the same number wrong twice, and both times the auditor caught it**; we grepped without excluding comment lines, the auditor used an AST | ✅ **Closed (remediation batch)**: new **`platform/scripts/ast-count-callsites.mjs`** (TypeScript compiler AST; the counting rule fixed in code: count only CallExpression, exclude tests and self-references in definition files by default, report both "expression count" and "file count"). The real run matches the auditor **exactly**: `getRedshiftSecretArn` = **16 sites / 14 files**; and incidentally measured `getZeroEtlSecretArn` = 2/2 and **`getPrivilegedCred` = 0** (the mechanical evidence that the controlled-executor and legacy-fallback items "remain open"). The two wrong 17/15 counts in the code were corrected with the counting rule noted |

**🔴 Delivery wording under option C (must be stated this way, no hedging)**:
- **The current state of the two blocking items (the regex guard; the legacy fallback) = contract unified, code not wired ⇒ both "still open".** The rework of the controlled-execution batch delivers only side-effect-free type contracts and template foundations.
- Real closure requires all of the following at once: credential supply complete + the closed executor implemented + **migration of the real call surface** + end-to-end and cross-project negative tests (see the definition of done in section 6 of the remediation proposal).
- 🔴 **Functional conservation (an explicit requirement + the rejection list in section 5 of the proposal)**: **the SQL editor / code-table landing / DMS bootstrap / legacy fallback / the 7 operations — not one may be deleted**; "removing the entry point / a permanent throw / 501 / no-op" may not be passed off as fail-closed.
