`active` after a supplemental deployment. The fix I had proposed in the previous revision — "replace the broker's `initializing` with `upgrading`" — was rejected on the spot: it is not a root fix:
`upgrading` is likewise not in `RUNTIME_USABLE_STATUS`, so after the change "a region that is serving is judged unable to accept new work" just the same; only the label looks better.
It was stressed three times that "**root-fix it, do not patch, or it never stops**", and under a **5-day schedule** the decision was "root fix + parallel acceleration".

**Single root cause (the four holes are symptoms of one thing)**: **there is no "desired state vs observed state" model, and no single controller.** One field, `status`,
carries **three orthogonal meanings — lifecycle × health × in-flight operation**; and it is written by **6 places, each hard-coding its own precondition state** (the broker sets it unconditionally /
seed drops straight to `active` / register drops straight to `registered` / `markTargetTested` accepts 2 states / `enableTarget` accepts 1 state /
the probe route copies another set of 2) ⇒ every new business path is bound to miss one precondition combination. The two defects are **the same mechanism firing twice**; the real environment had already produced
the **impossible shape** `status=initializing ∧ ready=true` (8/8 green yet unusable to the runtime, and no way back from the page). And
the three writers `drain` / `decommission` / `degraded` **are not written yet** — growing them the old way would repeat the mistake for certain.

**Root-fix model (contracted in the [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.1, revision 3.15)**: split into two groups of orthogonal fields + a single writer.
- **`spec` (desired; written only by the API/broker)** = `{intent: serve|drain|delete, desiredComponents[], desiredTemplateVersion?}`
  — supplies the "desired" that section 3.5 of the specification promised but that had nowhere to live; the broker still takes `desiredComponents` only from the allow-listed input, **no new input parameter**.
- **`observed` (observed; written only by the reconciler)** = `{phase, health, components[], templateVersion?, probe, operation?, lastReconcileAt?, lastError?}`
  — `phase` has an authoritative transition table; `operation` is the in-flight deployment / drain session (opened by the broker, cleared by the completer / reconciler),
  `disruptive` says whether it touches the core stateful stacks (section 12.6, the baseline-trust review item; always true until baseline validation lands).
- **Admission = one derived verdict** (replaces the enumerated-subset check of `unusableReason`; the audit surface does not grow):
  `intent=serve ∧ phase=ready ∧ health≠down ∧ fresh(probe) ∧ no disruptive operation`.
  🔴 **After the split, `resumeStatus` is no longer needed** — during a non-disruptive upgrade `phase` stays at `ready` throughout, and admission recovers automatically as soon as `operation` is cleared;
  "where to go back to" simply does not exist in the correct model.
- **`status` / `ready` / `components` are demoted to derived compatibility projections** (value domains unchanged) so that existing readers / UI / the broker's `DEPLOYABLE_STATUS`
  keep working during the transition; AST gate: write sites of `observed.phase` / `spec.*` outside `lifecycle.ts` = **0**.

**Risk-reduction finding from a parallel scan (2026-09-01; two read-only work streams checked the whole repository, not by inference)**: **no production code anywhere reads this table** —
the admission readers (`getTarget` / `listActiveTargets` / `requireReadyTarget`) have **zero production callers** today (only tests +
an idle `privileged-cred`); all live read sites are in exactly three places, **admin routes / admin UI / tests**, with no data-plane / execute / Ask-Your-Data fan-out.
⇒ Splitting spec/observed **cannot silently interrupt any serving data plane**; the risk is far below the concern of a "big-bang rewrite of the admission gate".

**Additive migration (no scripts, no hand-editing of production DynamoDB)**: new fields coexist → `status` / `ready` demoted to derived projections → admission re-derived (same function)
→ **the controller "adopts" existing rows** (the first time it meets an item lacking `spec` / `observed`, it infers them from the legacy fields and writes them; `tgt_usw2_a`'s
`initializing ∧ ready=true ∧ probe green` ⇒ phase=ready / health=healthy / intent=serve, **self-healing**). Only 2 existing items (confirmed by a scan).

**Batching (designed once; at the end of each batch the system is self-consistent / acceptable / rollbackable)**: batch 1 = the transition table / single write channel in `lifecycle.ts` + schema fields +
rewrite of admission derivation + rework of the 6 writers + compatibility projection + adoption + the full gate set (**this batch**); batch 2 = periodic reconciler + `degraded` writer +
a real writer of `observed.templateVersion` (resolves the **hard prerequisite of the data-plane batch**: the only writer of `probe.at` today is "a person clicking a button", so once the data plane is wired,
a non-home target nobody clicks for 15 min is automatically judged stale and dead); batch 3 = baseline-trust changeset preview + approval gate + waves.
The `drain` / `decommission` writers come with the deletion flow (section 4.6) of the data-plane batch (by then it is only adding edges to the transition table + calling `transitionPhase`; nothing can be missed).

**Attribution of the two defects**: same root (a missing path in the state machine / a missing channel in the maintenance-state gate), **absorbed by this batch's state-machine root fix and no longer listed separately**; the
`mergeComponentResources` narrow channel of section 20.22 **stays** (later folded into the `observed.components` write), and the "suggest switching to
`upgrading`" idea for the stuck-status defect is withdrawn (that was a patch).

**Gates that must be updated together with the implementation (located by the parallel scan, against "changed the core, forgot the pins")**: synth-gate section 10 (the broker status-write anchors
`SET #s = :init` / fencing `lockEpoch = :epoch` / the stale-lock double binding; once the broker writes spec/operation instead, these anchors must follow),
`finalize-lifecycle.test.ts` (per-writer ConditionExpression assertions for the six writers), `registry.test.ts` (seed + reconcile matrix),
`contract-drift.test.ts` (state-machine chain parsing + verbatim `RECONCILE_ALLOWED_FIELDS` + version pointers),
`exec-env-routes-guard.test.ts` (the `["initializing","testing"].includes(target.status)` anchor of the probe finalize precondition).

🔴 **No relaxation** of these conditional-write semantics that four external review rounds watched (reconcile / markTargetTested's `(attribute_not_exists(#r) OR #r = :false)`,
enable's `#p.#ok = :true`, merge's `#r = :true` + map-path, writeProbe's `attribute_exists(sk)` only and no touching of version).

**Honest boundaries (self-reported by work stream B, acknowledged and recorded by the main thread)**:
- `components` is now **purely observed** ⇒ a component that was "ticked at registration but failed to install" disappears from the registry; the
  **intent** "I wanted redshift" currently exists only in the build log. The desired/observed separation fields of the specification, section 3.5 are done **in a separate batch**;
- `flinkSubnetIds` is not in `COMPONENT_REQUIRED_RESOURCES` ⇒ even if the component stack emits that Output it does **not** enter the resource bag (to land it, it must first enter the contract in the specification, section 1.1);
- the detail page's `DEPLOYABLE_STATUS` is a **presentation-layer copy** of the broker's `validate.mjs`; that build boundary has **no test anchor** pinning it ⇒ recorded as debt.

#### 20.23-R 🔴 Convergence with the second external review (2026-09-02; decisions A–E all approved; the review's lifecycle root-remediation note)

The external review **confirmed the root-cause direction + independently reproduced a deterministic path to the stuck-status defect + confirmed that the lock is not released after success**; it did not overturn the skeleton; decisions **A–E were all approved**.
This section records **corrections to the batch-1 text above** (this section prevails):

- **Decisions A/B (write-authority model)**: the API writes only `spec`; **the broker = the only authorised "cross-runtime controller adapter"**, which only opens / attaches
  `observed.operation` and **never writes `phase`**; phase completion / legacy adoption / compatibility projection are all committed by `lifecycle.ts` (the reconciler).
  🔴 **A partial `spec` / `observed` is always `invalid`, fail-closed, and guessing from legacy is forbidden** (`classifyDescriptor` has three classes:
  both absent = legacy / both present and valid = modern / only one side or a broken field = invalid).
- **Decision C (broker boundary)** + **write order**: no import across TS/MJS, no added build step; the broker holds only the zero-dependency `planDeployOpen()`,
  proven equivalent to the TS side by behaviour vectors + real execution in the synth gate; **`operation` must be persisted before `StartBuild`**; a throw from `cb.send` = acceptance unknown
  ⇒ record `startUncertain`, **do not clear operation / lock** (sections 4.2 / 4.5). [Delivered in Step 2]
- **Decision D (completion atomicity)** + **operation.epoch**: `lockEpoch` moves into `operation`; completion = after finalize succeeds and this round's candidate is all green,
  one conditional write keyed on `version` + `phase` + `operationId` + `operation.lockEpoch` + **the identity of this round's `probe` (`probe.at`)** closes it,
  **clearing `operation` and the deployment lock in the same write** (atomic); if any condition fails, the block stays and the caller re-reads and retries. [Completion path delivered in Steps 3–4]
- **Contradiction B (phase failure edge) corrected**: the batch-1 text / plan wrote `provisioning→{verifying, registered (fall back on failure)}`, which **does not match the implementation** —
  the implementation **leaves `provisioning` + `lastError` on failure (projected as `failed`) and retries as an idempotent self-transition**; it neither falls back to registered nor adds a `failed` phase.
  The specification, section 1.1 has been corrected to keep the self-loop. **A self-transition (`from===to`) is not an ordinary edge**; it needs restricted authorisation (completion / legacy / enable fence) and is not opened globally.
- **Contradiction C (health admission) corrected**: the batch-1 admission rule `health≠down` **let `unknown` through** ⇒ changed to **`health∈{healthy,degraded}`**
  (unknown fail-closed). `serviceabilityReason` additionally **refuses any `operation`** (not only `disruptive` — corrupted data writing
  `disruptive=false` is refused too; section 20.2 of the review note / refusal-list item 13); a residual top-level deployment lock is checked **before** the legacy/modern split
  (`activeDeployLockReason`; otherwise legacy-stable bypasses the operation check).
- **adoptLegacy made conservative (refusal-list item 12) — correction**: the batch-1 text said `tgt_usw2_a` (`initializing ∧ ready ∧ stored probe.ok`)
  **self-heals to ready through adoption** — the review explicitly ordered that **ready must not be materialised from a stored probe**. Changed to **conservative adoption** (only a genuinely serving
  `active/degraded ∧ ready` becomes ready; a stuck item lands in `provisioning` / `health=unknown`, fail-closed), and **the legacy-adoption acceptance item is materialised only by this round's fresh green from the probe route
  through `adoptLegacyAfterFreshProbe` (carrying a `LegacyCompletionFence`)**. [Fresh-probe path delivered in Step 4]

**The review's reference implementation (section 20.3 of its note) immediately exposed two real bugs that my green batch-1 tests could not detect** (fixed in Step 1):
① the first write of `transitionPhase` with `ifAbsent=true` still passed the unused `#ph` / `:from` ⇒ **a real DynamoDB `ValidationException`**
(undetectable with unit mocks; the adoption path for existing rows was certain to blow up); ② `serviceabilityReason` only checked `operation?.disruptive` = fail-open.

**Step 1, "converge the contract", delivered (Step 1 of the remediation plan in section 12 of the review note; zero regressions across 1312 unit tests / tsc 0 / naming-parity / contract-drift all green)**:
the contract text in the specification, section 1.1 lands A/B/C/D + write order + completion state + the three classes; `lifecycle.ts` converged (serviceability refuses any op; first write constructed per branch
to fix unused names; guard discriminated union `{equals}|{absent}` + component-index alias; adoptLegacy made conservative); `registry.ts` gives lock priority in unusableReason
+ declares the `deployLock*` fields; 9 C/D unit tests added (three classes / first write without unused names / guard absent · undefined · bad path / unknown health).

🔴 **The fence types and the fence GATE of transitionPhase, the broker's `planDeployOpen`, the two-axis finalize, the probe's `reconcileAfterProbe`,
and the AST writer-allow-list gate = Steps 2–5, wired serially per the plan in section 12, each step keeping the system runnable** (the contract is converged first; broker and probe are not left to guess the semantics in parallel).

**Honesty (decision E)**: the two defects are **not yet truly closed** (the closing point is Steps 2–4); Step 1 only converged the contract and the foundation into fail-closed.
`disruptive` stays `true` until the baseline-trust batch (batch 3); **the production No-Go on the multi-region lifecycle is not lifted until the periodic reconciler (batch 2) + failure recovery + baseline trust have landed**
(section 15.2). `register` / `seedHomeTarget` are deliberately left unchanged (creation ≠ transition; the downstream first write adopts).

### 20.24 🔴 **Lifecycle construction batch 2a: periodic reconciler (in-process lease singleton)** (construction batch; the hard data-plane prerequisite once the stuck-status defect was closed)

**Trigger**: Steps 1–4 closed the two defects (double page-level evidence in a real environment for supplemental deployment on an active target and for legacy adoption; `tgt_usw2_a` is now active with 4 components); but the only writer of `probe.at`
is still "a person clicking two admin routes" ⇒ a non-home target nobody clicks for 15 min is judged stale and dead (the stale table in the [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 1.1), and the data plane (the data-plane construction batch) cannot be built on manual probing
⇒ the periodic reconciler = hard prerequisite of the data-plane batch (the first-ranked risk on the roadmap).

**Architecture choice (decided on 2026-09-02: in-process lease singleton)**. Three candidates were laid out before deciding:
- **① In-process lease singleton (chosen)**: inside the existing always-on platform service (ALB + Fargate, desiredCount 2–6), start a ≤5 min loop through Next 14 instrumentation
  + leader election via a short DynamoDB lease. Zero new infrastructure / IAM / image / HTTP endpoint; probing already runs in this very process today (the probe route) ⇒ same code, same ambient credentials, zero drift.
  Cost: enabling `experimental.instrumentationHook`, parameterising the lease / creating a new short lease.
- **② A separate scheduled ECS task (rejected)**: the om-ingestion convention `events.Rule+EcsTask`; but running the reconciler needs the platform image, and pulling a large image every 5 min (Fargate does not guarantee a host cache)
  is costly with high cold start, plus a whole new task definition + task role + Rule IAM/infra surface.
- **③ External schedule → internal endpoint (rejected)**: one more machine-to-machine-authenticated internal HTTP endpoint (which must be locked down) + a scheduler; a larger attack surface than ①.
- 🔴 The correctness of all three is independent of the trigger: `reconcileAfterProbe` is idempotent + fenced ⇒ leader election / triggering is only about efficiency; ① is chosen for the **smallest surface**.

**Three deliverables**: ① the driver `platform/src/instrumentation.ts` (`register()` starts only when `NEXT_RUNTIME==='nodejs'`) + `reconciler-loop.ts`
(every tick isolated by try/catch; pure I/O, low CPU) + the hook enabled in `next.config.js`; ② a dedicated leader-lease lock row `pk="lock"/sk="reconciler"` (short lease via DynamoDB conditional write,
reusing the semantics of run-lock.ts; 🔴 lease TTL + tick < the 15 min stale threshold, tick ≈ 4 min / lease ≈ 8 min ⇒ gap ≈ 12 min); ③ a shared `reconcileOneTarget()` (the probe route body extracted into an
SSOT; the manual click and the periodic tick run the same code ⇒ automatic finalize of a terminal build comes for free) + a new `reconcileTelemetry` in `lifecycle.ts` (does not bump version, phase unchanged, guarded by `attribute_not_exists(observed.operation)`).

🔴🔴 **The most load-bearing invariant of this batch = the ban on fail-open reversal**: once the reconciler refreshes `probe.at` automatically, `fresh(probe)` is always true ⇒ **refreshing probe.at without writing back health
= a region whose probe turned red stays "fresh + active" forever = fail-closed reversed into fail-open**. Hence a tick must derive health from this round's probe (green → healthy / red → unknown, fail-closed
/ degraded → degraded), downgrading only after `HEALTH_DOWNGRADE_STREAK=2` consecutive reds (against flapping) and recovering on a single green; the downgrade window ≤ 8 min < 15 min ⇒ fail-closed faster than the old "wait for stale".
**This is not polish; it is a red line that this batch would introduce fail-open without.**

**Batch boundary (lifecycle batch 2 split into 2a/2b)**: 2a = this batch (driver + leader election + telemetry health / observedVersion + periodic adoption) ⇒ lifts the stale-death verdict;
2b (immediately after) = crash recovery for startUncertain / attachUncertain / no-build-window (sections 19.7–19.8 of the review note) + CFN rollback / partial success + deploy lock-lease renewal.

🔴 **The production No-Go is not lifted by 2a** (decision E): a ≤5 min reconciler is necessary but not sufficient; 2b + baseline trust (batch 3) are still required.

**Gates + acceptance**: vitest (lease / tick / health flip / telemetry no-op / adoption) + tsc + the AST write-site gate still green (health is written in lifecycle.ts) + mutation + naming-parity
+ synth-gate (only next.config changed, zero infra regression) + redline scan; after remediation a further review round is mandatory. 🔴 **Headless page-level acceptance: hands-off for >15 min, observing probe.at refresh every tick
+ admission not judging stale** + an injected red probe verifying that the fail-open reversal does not happen; unit tests / API are only auxiliary. Contract: the specification, section 3.5a, and the reconciler section of the acceptance plan.

**✅ 2a closed with real-environment evidence (2026-09-02 / 09-03)**: the hands-off acceptance item passed at page level (`probe.at` advanced monotonically over three ticks with nobody operating); **the multi-replica item** (desiredCount=2 / runningCount=2,
two replicas × the lock row held by a single token × a single ~4 min cadence ⇒ multi-replica single writer confirmed); **the template-version item** (`observed.templateVersion=2026-09-02T13:35:42.571Z`,
previously always null ⇒ a dead field brought back to life). **The injected-red-probe item is honestly recorded as covered by unit tests only** (injecting a red probe is destructive and was not done in the real environment).

### 20.26 🔴 **Lifecycle construction batch 3: baseline trust** (construction batch; the only hole that can destroy data)

**Trigger**: the `add-components` claim "pass the full set, CDK is idempotent and only adds" **holds only while the source template is unchanged** (section 12.6). As soon as the control-plane infra evolves,
adding a component to a **serving** region may produce a changeset against the existing core stack, or even REPLACE stateful resources
(Redshift namespace / MWAA environment / S3 bucket / KMS key) ⇒ downtime or **irreversible data loss**. Before baseline trust, `disruptive` is always true
(section 10.2, fail-closed), at the price of "adding a component to a live region requires taking it out of service"; what baseline trust must do is **give that verdict a real basis**.

**Four things** (contract: the [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.5c): ① changeset preview (create only, never execute) ② an impact-classification pure function ③ a replace approval gate (two locks: platform + build)
④ baselineRef anchoring + `desiredTemplateVersion` waves (**eliminating the root cause "source drift" itself**).

**Delivered (first step; deliberately the part with zero runtime behaviour change)**:
- The verdict `platform/src/lib/exec-env/deploy-impact.ts` (zero-dependency pure function) + its **build-side twin** `infra/scripts/deploy-impact.mjs`.
  The verdict takes the strictest reading: 🔴 **cannot see clearly = strictest** (unknown action / missing resourceType / unreadable change set ⇒ disruptive ∧ approval required) ·
  `Remove` (stateful ⇒ approval required) · `Replacement ∈ {True, **Conditional**}` (Conditional = CloudFormation itself cannot tell;
  **never be more optimistic than CloudFormation**) · `Modify` is harmless only if it touches Tags/Metadata alone · only `Add` ⇒ non-disruptive (this is the real "only adds, never modifies").
- `planHash` = sha256 of the canonically sorted, line-by-line concatenation ⇒ **approval is bound to the plan** (one changed byte in the plan automatically invalidates the old approval);
  the `why` text is **not part of the fingerprint** (changing wording should not invalidate an approved plan).
- Gates: 27 exhaustive unit tests (including **all 23 stateful types actually run one by one** for "replacement must require approval", not inferred from "it is in the set")
  + **840-cell cross-boundary parity** (action × replacement × scope × resourceType, comparing disruptive / approval / planHash / reasons cell by cell).

🔴 **Why two implementations**: the verdict must serve both the platform side (filling disruptive / blocking unapproved plans / page presentation) and the **build side (the last line of defence)** —
deployment power lives only on the CodeBuild service role, and the platform **can be bypassed** (someone invoking the broker directly). The platform image only COPYs `platform/`,
so code cannot be shared across the build boundary (the same situation as the broker's `validate.mjs` and the two-sided export of `naming.json`) ⇒ the same solution: two copies + a parity gate.

🔴🔴 **This parity gate caught a real defect on its very first run, worth recording on its own**: the TS verdict I wrote contained **5 literal NUL bytes** (which should have been spaces).
`tsc` all green, unit tests all green, invisible in a visual review — it only changes **the content of string literals**: spaces in the canonical lines became NUL ⇒
**planHash would never equal the build side's ⇒ baseline-trust approvals would never match** (the approval gate degrades from "guarding the door" to "blocking forever", and only shows in a real environment).
⇒ The whole class was pinned while at it: a new `source-hygiene.test.ts` scans the TS/mjs under platform/src + infra, forbidding C0 control characters and zero-width characters,
with positive and negative self-check samples; U+00A0 / U+3000 are deliberately **not** forbidden (legal in Chinese typography; the first version forbade them and immediately bit existing Chinese pages).
The **intentional** literal UTF-8 BOM in `api/bi/export` was changed to the `\uFEFF` escape (byte-identical value) ⇒ the gate stays **absolute**, with no exception list.

**Second / third steps delivered**: the build-side last line of defence `infra/scripts/p0e-gate.mjs` (changeset preview → normalisation → the single verdict → machine-readable ruling
→ 🔴 **clean up thoroughly** [delete the change set + delete the `REVIEW_IN_PROGRESS` shell stack that `--no-execute` leaves behind for a not-yet-existing stack; otherwise
the recovery gate of batch 2b would judge "unstable" forever ⇒ that target could never be unlocked again — a trap where two batches bite each other, found while reasoning it through] → approval required but no matching planHash ⇒ build fails)
+ the buildspec wires the gate after bootstrap and before the real deploy + the two-phase `EXEC_ENV_MODE=preview` + the broker passes approval parameters through and provides a read-only preview bypass.

#### 20.26a 🔴 **④ Correction of the baselineRef mechanism** (found by re-checking during construction: the approach written in the original contract **cannot be done, and is something we ourselves had forbidden**)

The original contract, item ④ of section 3.5c of the specification, said "adding components **replays** `spec.baselineRef` (the buildspec receives `EXEC_ENV_BASELINE_REF` and **checks it out**)".
Re-checking the code facts against sections 12.0 / 12.5 before construction, **two independent reasons ruled this mechanism out**:

1. **It cannot be done**: the CodeBuild source is `codebuild.Source.s3({bucket, path: sourceBundle.s3ObjectKey})`
   — a content-hash-immutable **CDK asset (S3 zip)** (the source-acquisition decision of section 12.5); and the first entry of the source-bundle exclusion list is `.git`
   (`SOURCE_EXCLUDE` in `deploy-stack.ts`, to shrink the asset from 509 MB to ~2 MB) ⇒ **there is no git repository inside the build container**, and no ref to check out.
2. **It must not be done**: the only mechanism that can point a build at an "old source" is `StartBuild`'s `sourceLocationOverride`,
   and **we ourselves blocked it with an explicit IAM Deny in fix ① of section 12.0** (`codebuild:source.location`,
   listed alongside `source.buildspec` / `serviceRole` / `environment.image` / `privilegedMode`, one independent statement per key).
   The reason was written down at the time: swapping the source bundle = borrowing CodeBuild's `cloudformation:*` + `iam:PassRole` to run arbitrary code.
   🔴 Opening that door "to prevent source drift" would widen the blast radius from "deploying the real template" to "**taking over the whole account**" —
   **trading a P1 governance problem for a P0 privilege-escalation hole**, the opposite direction from "root fix, no patching".

⇒ Corrected to "**drift made visible + explicit wave authorisation + fail-closed**" (item ④ of section 3.5c of the specification rewritten accordingly, with 5 verdict rules):
`observed.baselineRef` (which source is actually installed; written only by the success fence) · `observed.operation.sourceRef` (the source used by the in-flight run; set by the broker) ·
the existing `spec.desiredTemplateVersion` (explicit wave authorisation by the administrator; **no new spec field**) · `EXEC_ENV_SOURCE_REF` (project-level env, locked by CDK).
The key trade-off is recorded honestly: **this does not buy "a lagging target keeps deploying with the old source"** — to add a component while refusing the new source, the only compliant path is
to roll the control plane back to that source first and then add the component. The contract states this limitation explicitly instead of pretending it can be done.

🔴 Another construction-time re-check: `observed.templateVersion` **cannot** double as baselineRef — its real meaning today is
"the maximum `LastUpdatedTime` across that target's stacks" (`describeObservedTemplateVersion` in deploy-control.ts),
a **timestamp**, not a **source identity**. Conflating them would yield a "baseline" that looks populated but never compares equal — worse than none.

#### 20.26b Breakdown of the fourth step (wrap-up) — the constraint that the verdict be single-sourced on both sides dictates the order

| # | Content | Side | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `classifyBaselineDrift` pure function (the 5 verdict rules above) + `planHash` includes `sourceRef` | **Both sides** (`deploy-impact.ts` / `deploy-impact.mjs`) | 🔴 The shared verdict **must change on both sides in the same batch**, and the parity gate is extended to the cross product of refs; "landing one side first" is never allowed |
| 2 | Platform-side preview route `POST .../preview` + reading `P0E_IMPACT_VERDICT` from the build log | platform | Read-only; opens no operation, takes no lock (the broker bypass is already in place) |
| 3 | `deploy` / `add-components` accept `{approvalId, approvedPlanHash}`; mismatch ⇒ 409 | platform | The platform-side lock (the build-side one already stands independently) |
| 4 | Fill `observed.operation.disruptive` with the real value + the success fence advances `observed.baselineRef` | platform | The single-writer principle is unchanged: carried in that one conditional write of the success fence |
| 5 | Approval UI (plan presented line by line + explicit confirmation + audit) | platform | Interaction follows the interaction principles of the [model-configuration](./model-configuration.md) and [project-isolation](./project-isolation.md) designs |

🔴 **Ordering constraint (not laziness)**: items 1–5 are all inside `platform/` or span both sides, and `BgpPlatformStack` uses
`ContainerImage.fromAsset("../platform")` — **CDK rebuilds the image from the working tree at deploy time**. During an environment rebuild (deploy-all),
a failed wiring check at step 13 automatically calls `redeploy-platform.sh` (which contains `cdk deploy BgpPlatformStack`) ⇒
changing `platform/` at that moment would **bake a half-finished build into the image** and contaminate the real-environment verification of the reconciler and failure-recovery acceptance items.
The fourth step is therefore scheduled after the environment rebuild finishes and the failure-recovery item is verified; during the window, only contract corrections and the infra-side pass-through pipeline
that **do not enter the image context** are done (this section + the three infra items below).

### 20.27 🔴 **Lifecycle construction batch 4: drain / return to service + item-by-item closure of the production definition of done (section 15.2)** (construction batch)

#### 20.27a Drain / return to service — previously **the transition table had the edge but nobody could write it**

`ready→draining` has always been in `PHASE_EDGES`, but **no code could change the intent to drain** ⇒ an operator wanting to "stop taking new work and let in-flight runs finish"
could only edit the status field by hand, or simply not drain (the latter is more dangerous). This batch completes it:

| Deliverable | Key trade-off |
|---|---|
| 🔴 `draining → ready` **edge added** | The original table only had `draining → decommissioning` ⇒ draining was a **one-way door**: once drained, the only way out was destruction. Draining is an **operational action**; when it is over, the environment must be able to come back; irreversibility would scare people away from using it. **Returning to service still follows the same evidence path (a fresh all-green probe)** — flipping the flag back alone does not count |
| `setSpecIntent` (**the only writer of `spec.intent`**) | Touches only `spec` + the materialised projection, **never `observed.phase`** (phase belongs to `transitionPhase`) ⇒ it does not become a second lifecycle writer. The conditional write is bound to `version` ∧ `attribute_not_exists(observed.operation)` |
| 🔴 **Only `serve ⟷ drain` is allowed** | Opening `delete` would push the phase towards `decommissioning/gone` while **all the resources are still there** — a lie worse than doing nothing (a tombstone that deceives). Real destruction follows section 4.6 (impact preview → type the name to confirm → approval → destroy; data buckets RETAIN) and belongs to the data-plane construction batch |
| 🔴 **Always refused while a deployment session is in flight** | The completion fence asserts `post.spec.intent === "serve"`; changing the intent mid-deployment ⇒ that deployment **can never close its fence** and lingers as a session needing the manual recovery of batch 2b. **Two mechanisms bite each other, so it must be blocked at the entrance** (in-memory check + conditional write, double block) |
| 🔴 **home cannot be drained** | Draining the control plane's own execution environment = the platform cutting off its own legs; same rule as "home shows no delete entry" (403 + no button on the page) |
| The reconciler drives the phase | `drain ∧ ready ⇒ draining`; `serve ∧ draining ∧ this round's probe all green ⇒ ready`. 🔴 `draining → decommissioning` is **deliberately not driven** (that step means real destruction) |

**Closed loop in a real environment (2026-09-03, page level)**: click "Drain" ⇒ `intent=drain` takes effect synchronously, `status` is **immediately** `draining`
→ next tick `phase=draining` → click "Resume accepting new work" ⇒ `intent=serve` → next tick (**all-green probe**) `phase=ready` / `status=active`.
All four negative cases refused: enable on `ready` ⇒ 409 (**the enable-on-ready negative acceptance class**) · `intent=delete` ⇒ 400 pointing to section 4.6 · draining home ⇒ 403 · button disabled while a session is in flight.

🔴 **This batch also caught and fixed a P0 along the way (the projection ignored intent)**: `deriveStatusProjection(spec, observed)` **accepted `spec` but never read it**
⇒ after draining, the top-level `status` was still `active`. This is not merely a display problem: **the compatibility projection exists precisely for the old readers** (which judge usability by
`status ∈ {active,degraded}`) ⇒ fail-open for an environment that **has been drained**. Fixed on both sides (platform + broker twin) in the same batch +
4 **correctness** assertions added. 🔴 **The 252-cell parity gate did not catch it** — parity proves "both sides agree", not "the verdict is right";
at the time both sides ignored intent **together**. Remember this one: **parity is no substitute for correctness assertions**.

#### 20.27b Item-by-item closure of the production definition of done, section 15.2 (the reviewer's 8 items, **honestly** annotated)

| # | Reviewer's wording | Closure |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A ≤5-minute periodic reconciler | ✅ Batch 2a (tick=240s); measured in a fresh environment, `lastReconcileAt` of both targets keeps advancing |
| 2 | Lease **renewal** for long builds and crash recovery | 🔴 **Honestly rewritten**: **renewal is deliberately not done** (settled in 2b: `deployLockExpiresAt` is a **diagnostic quantity**; a negative gate pins that it must never be compared with the clock — "the lock expired, so it can be cleared" is exactly the easiest fail-open to write). Crash recovery relies instead on a **structurally reachable boundary** (CodeBuild `timeoutInMinutes` + margin ⇒ that session cannot possibly still have a build running) + an administrator recovery endpoint to make the final call. ✅ Delivered and verified by the failure-recovery acceptance item |
| 3 | **Automatic convergence** after a terminal build / CFN rollback | 🔴 **Honestly rewritten**: this **conflicts with** item 5 of section 19.8 of the review note itself ("a failure **must not** clear the lock automatically"). The delivered rule = **the success path converges automatically (free with 2a) + the failure path is classified automatically and a human makes the final call**. ✅ The failure-recovery acceptance item is exactly the evidence for this (build failed → automatically classified as "Manual recovery required" → cleared only after the administrator explicitly confirmed) |
| 4 | An administrator recovery entry for `startUncertain` past its window / attach-ambiguous + explicit confirmation + audit | ✅ Delivered in 2b; verified by the failure-recovery acceptance item (banner → second confirmation → audit of the before/after state) |
| 5 | Closed semantics for health unknown/down | ✅ 2a: `unknown` is **fail-closed**, not serviceable · downgrade only after 2 consecutive reds (against transient false kills) · after recovery `lastError` is cleared while `lastFailure` stays |
| 6 | Baseline-trust changeset preview / baselineRef / replace approval | ✅ Fully closed on 2026-09-03 (the baseline-trust acceptance item in both the positive and negative direction + the five follow-on baseline items, one of them tightened) |
| 7 | Proof of **automatic stale / recovery** on two real targets | ✅✅ **Closed (2026-09-03, page level in a real environment)**: both targets were **deliberately made stale together** (three time sources moved back 22 min) ⇒ the non-home card shows the red tag "Not accepting new work" + the drawer gives the **real seconds** `descriptor is stale (1325s since last probe/update > 900s)`; home has **no tag and still accepts work** (the exemption works; the control plane does not fail-close itself) ⇒ then, with **zero clicks**, the controller's next tick automatically refreshed both probe times (`09:54:34` / `09:54:33`) and the page returned to "Accepting work" by itself (21s fresh). 🔴 The groundwork is in section 20.28 (the dead verdict was originally invisible on the page) |
| 8 | Real-environment evidence for supplemental deployment on an active target, legacy adoption and the enable-on-ready negative case | ✅✅ **Closed (2026-09-03, the original supplemental-deployment script re-run)**: the component chosen was **flink** (redshift/dms already installed; flink was, at that moment, "the only implemented component never installed" on tgt_usw2_a = literally what the script intended). The preview judged "adds only / no-drift" → confirm → `operation` opened while **`phase` stayed ready** → build SUCCEEDED → Check initialization → badge back to "Running"; in DynamoDB `status=active` / `operation` cleared / lock released / `flink` in `components` / `flinkRoleArn` in the resource bag. Legacy adoption and the enable-on-ready negative class had been verified earlier |

⇒ **The production No-Go on the lifecycle: 8 of 8 items ✅, all closed ⇒ formally lifted on 2026-09-03** (consistent with decision E: claim it only once it has actually run).

🔴 **The boundary of the lift must be stated clearly**: what is lifted is the **lifecycle** dimension (register / install / probe / failure recovery / drain / return to service / freshness).
**The data plane** (jobs really running in a second region) has not started = the data-plane construction batch; **production go-live across jurisdictions** was additionally subject to a legal gate (section 18, decision 6quater) [🔴 withdrawn on 2026-09-05: no legal gate, see decision 6quinquies].
"Lifecycle No-Go lifted" must not be read as "multi-region is production-ready".

### 20.28 🔴 **Lifecycle construction batch 4, wrap-up: read-only presentation of serviceability + two supplementary verifications of phase A** (construction batch)

#### 20.28a Why "making it stale" forced out a product defect

To deliver **page-level** evidence for item 7 of the definition of done (section 15.2), the very first step got stuck: **the "dead" verdict is simply not visible on the page**. Checked item by item:

| Read entry point | Passes the admission gate? | Page consumers |
|---|---|---|
| `getTargetRaw` / `listTargetsRaw` | ❌ Deliberately not (operators must see a broken item in order to fix it) | List page / detail drawer / deployment progress / probe |
| `getTarget` / `requireReadyTarget` (through `unusableReason`) | ✅ Yes | **Only `privileged-cred`, which has no page-level caller yet** (wired only when the data-plane batch lands) |

⇒ The **admission-dimension** verdicts — stale / residual lock / drained — are today completely invisible to the page. And the `status` badge is
a projection of **lifecycle × health**, **without freshness or locks** ⇒ the page would confidently show "Running" while the data plane in fact refuses everything.
This is exactly what was hit the time the IAM region snapshot went wrong: **"the worst thing is not that it cannot be used, but that you cannot see why it cannot be used"**
(root-fix item E of the roadmap).

**The fix = collapse the verdict into a single kernel, instead of writing another one for the page**:
- Extract `serviceabilityVerdict()` (whole-package validation → serviceability → freshness; the order means "most fundamental cause first"),
  shared by three callers: `admit()` (single point), `listActiveTargets()` (fan-out), `describeServiceability()` (read-only for the admin page).
  🔴 A real latent hazard removed along the way: `listActiveTargets` used to **compose `descriptorViolation + unusableReason` a second time on its own**
  — two compositions are two chances to rot; that is exactly how the crack "the single point refuses, the list admits" grows.
- The read-only outlet returns `{usable, reason, ageSeconds, freshnessMaxAgeSeconds, staleExempt}`; **zero writes**, triggers no probe;
  the `status` projection semantics are deliberately **left untouched** (that is the load-bearing surface of the 252-cell parity).
- Page: the card gets the red tag "Not accepting new work" when it is **known not to accept work** (reason in a Tooltip); the drawer gains a row "Accepting new work"
  — when accepting, it shows age + threshold (home additionally notes "exempt from staleness check"); when not, it shows **the verbatim refusal reason + the real seconds**.
  If the fields are missing (old backend) it honestly shows "Unknown": **it neither pretends to accept nor falsely reports not accepting**.

**Gates (the post-remediation review round has been done)**: a new `serviceability-view.test.ts` with 20 cases — the load-bearing part is **6 verbatim parity checks**
(`describeServiceability().reason === the why thrown by requireReadyTarget()`, not a weak assertion like "both contain the word stale")
+ each refusal reason triggered by one real scenario + threshold boundaries + the home exemption + read-only, no writes + **set-level parity**
(the fan-out admitted set === the set the page judges as accepting, and the set contains both admitted and refused members, against a false green where both sides are empty);
routes-guard gains a structural gate (the two read routes must **delegate** to the kernel and may not touch
`descriptorAgeMs` / `TARGET_FRESHNESS_MAX_AGE_MS` / `unusableReason` / `descriptorViolation` themselves), 🔴 **2 mutations each turn red for the correct reason** (a route fabricating a constant `usable=true` / a route that delegates but then computes freshness once more on its own).

#### 20.28b 🔴 Something measured in a real environment that had previously rested on arithmetic alone: **after a deployment the reconciler can stall for up to 12 min**

On the first run of the stale/recovery acceptance item, **6.5 min passed after injection without recovery**, and I nearly recorded it as a defect. The causal chain, once verified:
① this platform-stack redeployment **killed the old ECS task holding the leader lease midway through its lease**;
② `acquireOrRenewLeaderLease` **deliberately does not steal a live lease** (stealing could make two replicas probe at the same time);
③ ⇒ the new replica has to wait for the lease to expire naturally before taking over ⇒ worst-case stall = **lease 8 min + tick 4 min = 12 min**.

**This is not a defect; it is the design margin being exercised in a real environment** — the multi-replica acceptance item had pinned "12 min < the 15 min stale threshold" by arithmetic only,
this time it was measured: probe.at stopped after `09:41:03` and recovered at `09:50:32` (9.5 min, within the 12 min budget).
⇒ Two conclusions: (a) the margin really exists and is sufficient; (b) **the waiting window of an acceptance script must be sized by lease + tick, not by a single tick**
(my first version allowed 6.5 min and gave up **2 seconds** before recovery — the classic "product right, script wrong").

📌 Incidentally, the multi-replica item was upgraded from "pinned by arithmetic" to "a takeover window measured in a real environment".

#### 20.28c Two lessons from the acceptance scripts (once again the script broke before the product did)

1. 🔴 **Do not bet on class names.** The first re-run of the supplemental-deployment item located the preview dialog by `.ant-modal-content`; **the dialog was plainly there** (screenshot as proof;
   the ruling content was entirely correct) yet nothing matched, and a whole round was wasted. After switching to `role=dialog` + text-based location it passed first time.
   Same family: `Descriptions bordered` renders as a table (`tr.ant-descriptions-row` + `th/td`),
   and has **no** `.ant-descriptions-item` wrapper layer.
2. 🔴 **A timeout must name what it was waiting for.** The same rule extends to scripts: on a locator timeout, print the `message` and the text of every dialog verbatim +
   take a screenshot; do not just throw a `TimeoutError`. In the first round it was precisely because nothing was named that someone had to open the screenshot by hand to learn the product was fine.
3. Addendum: the original supplemental-deployment script had one line `assert any(k.startswith("redshift"))` that was **not parameterised** (everything else had been),
   so switching components was bound to go falsely red — now asserts on the `COMP` prefix and prints the resource keys added in this run.

### 20.26c 🔴 **Two P0 defects found in a real environment on 2026-09-03 (same family: byte-for-byte comparison of values that are not byte-stable)**

Both were **hit first during live testing in a real environment**, while on my side **all 1526 unit tests were green** — worth a section of their own, because what they expose is
the systematic blind spot "what unit tests cannot detect", not two isolated bugs.

| # | Symptom (as reported) | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0 ①** | "Check initialization used to work; now the probe fails: `legacyCompletionFence: probe/desired/observed/input invalid or not an exact cover of the components`" (server message, translated) | The completion fence compared three **component lists** with `JSON.stringify`, and the same list exists in two orderings: the canonical order of `orderComponents()` vs the producer's original order (legacy adoption uses `[...t.components]`; the broker stores the **client's order**). The real home = `core,redshift,mwaa,dms,flink,agentcore`, canonical order = `core,redshift,dms,flink,agentcore,mwaa` ⇒ **never equal**. Second symptom: periodic adoption could never write into `observed` (the bare probe was writing, `reconcileAfterProbe` threw). **Latent in the same family**: a non-canonical client order on the modern path ⇒ the deployment completion fence fails permanently | New SSOT `componentSetKey` / `sameComponentSet`, **normalising at the comparison point** (not patching `orderComponents` into every producer — that would require every future producer to remember, and forgetting one brings it back). Different sets **are still judged unequal**; the partial-green defence loses nothing |
| **P0 ②** | Clicking "Enable" on the page **always returns 409**, and the refusal reason only says `enable fresh probe/axes/input fence changed` (server message, translated) | The last item of `enableTarget`'s fence, `JSON.stringify(current.probe)!==JSON.stringify(fence.probe)`: `fence.probe` is the **in-memory object** from `runProbe()`, `current.probe` is what came back from a **DynamoDB round trip** — a DynamoDB Map **does not guarantee key order** ⇒ never equal ⇒ **fail-closed self-lock**. Second spot in the same family: the enable **post-image** check of `transitionPhase` mixes sources the same way | Export the existing `canonicalJson` (the byte-identical copy in registry now **delegates** to it) and set the rule: **across sources (memory ↔ DynamoDB) always canonical; bare stringify only within one source**. All 17 `JSON.stringify` comparisons in the family were examined one by one: only these two enable spots cross sources; the rest are "snapshot vs re-read" TOCTOU guards and **stay byte-for-byte** (normalising would let real changes through) |

🔴 **Why all 1526 unit tests were green** (this matters more than the two bugs themselves):
① the component lists of the existing fixtures **happened to all be in canonical order** (mostly `["core"]` / `["core","redshift"]`) ⇒ the two orderings happened to coincide;
② the mocked DynamoDB **does not reorder keys** ⇒ the memory / round-trip difference simply does not exist in unit tests.
⇒ Same root as the lesson of section 20.24 (mocks cannot detect DynamoDB runtime validation errors or fail-open). Remediation:
**fixtures use real data copied verbatim from a real environment** + **all permutations** (6! = 720) + a **"test still effective" guard**
(asserting canonical order ≠ original order; should the two ever become the same order, the test file declares itself obsolete instead of silently going inert).

🔴 **An engineering rule set as a by-product: a giant `||` verdict must name each failing item.**
Both times, 9 conditions were crammed into one `if(a||b||c…)` and the error did not point at any of them, costing two wasted rounds each to locate (paging through DynamoDB and ruling them out one by one).
The enable precondition now collects `failures: string[]`, naming each item together with its key values; the error from `validateExactGreenLifecycleEvidence`
carries the four canonical keys `desired=/observed=/input=/probeOk=`. **"Wrong, but not saying what is wrong" is itself a defect**; every fence written from now on follows this rule.

### 20.25 🔴 **Lifecycle construction batch 2b: failure-path recovery (a single failure writer)** (construction batch; a release-blocking item of section 19.8 of the review note)

**Trigger**: after 2a the "success path" is fully automatic (the reconciler runs the same completion sequence every tick ⇒ a crash after a successful build needs no click), but **the failure path has not an inch of it**.

**Single root cause (not four bugs — one)**: the **only clearer of `observed.operation` + the top-level deployment lock is the "successful completion fence"**.
So a build `FAILED/FAULT/STOPPED/TIMED_OUT`, a CFN rollback, `attachUncertain`, `startUncertain` past its 5 min window, a process crash after open
— **all** fall into the same hole: the target is permanently "locked ⇒ not accepting new work", and the only way out is hand-editing DynamoDB (forbidden by a red line).
⇒ Root fix = **give the failure path exactly one writer** (`recordOperationFailure`), which clears the lock in one fully fenced conditional write only when both are present: "**a verified external disposition** (exact build terminal +
CFN with no action in progress / no unexecuted change set) + **an explicit administrator decision** (decisionId + the operationId named)".
Contract: the [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.5b; the review's reference implementation in sections 19.7 / 19.8 of its note.

**Three judgements that "look incidental but are the crux of this batch"**:
1. 🔴 **phase is never touched.** A failure is not a lifecycle event; it is a health event. A failed first installation keeps `provisioning` + `lastError` (projected failed; explicit retry required);
   a failed upgrade keeps `ready` but `health=unknown` (serviceability fail-closed, projected degraded), and **"whether it can return to serviceable" is left to the next probe**
   (the reconciler runs within ≤4 min; green ⇒ automatically active). ⇒ The rule of section 19.8 of the review note, "return to ready only after verification back to baseline", is satisfied **structurally**:
   serviceability is decided by a real probe, not by human judgement; and an illegal edge like `ready→provisioning` can never be produced at all.
   This is the spec/observed axis split (section 20.23) paying its dividend — in the old single-`status` model, failure recovery would inevitably have had to "guess a previous state to go back to".
2. 🔴 **Lock-lease renewal is deliberately not done** (the roadmap had originally listed it under 2b). Verified by line-by-line grep: **nowhere** in the repository is `deployLockExpiresAt` compared with the clock
   (the `OR deployLockExpiresAt < :now` that section 19.5 of the review note had asked to remove is gone completely; the table also has **no DynamoDB TTL**, so items are never silently deleted) ⇒ renewal has no reader;
   more importantly, **renewal would break the exact read-back in the broker's `matchesOpenedDeploySession`** (which verifies verbatim that
   `deployLockExpiresAt === Date.parse(startedAt) + LOCK_LEASE_MS`) ⇒ a concurrent renewal would turn "the strong read-back right after open"
   into `locked`. **Renewal is a net negative.**
   📌 **Correction on re-review** (the original wording "the no-build 5 min resend path would be invalidated by renewal" misattributed the cause): the resend path `resumableUnattachedStart`
   only requires that field to be "an integer > 0" and does not compare the derived identity; what is affected are the two ALL_NEW read-backs in `openDeploySession`.
   The conclusion is unchanged (no reader + would break the exact read-back ⇒ not done), but the mechanism must be stated correctly.
   The real error was `LOCK_LEASE_MS=90min < the CodeBuild project timeout of 2h` — a legitimate 100-minute build would show as "expired",
   tempting a successor to add that fail-open condition back. ⇒ Corrected to `LOCK_LEASE_MS ≥ project timeout + margin` + a **negative gate pinning** "must not be compared with the clock".
   (The meta-pattern is item 3 of the root-fix-not-patch rule: prove that "runtime state that looks like it needs maintenance" is in fact **a diagnostic quantity derived from the source of truth**, and eliminate the maintainer.)
3. 🔴 **The reachable boundary for a missing buildId (startUncertain) is argued structurally, not guessed**: when there is no build to look up, recovery is allowed only after
   `now − startedAt ≥ project timeout + 5min` — on the basis that CodeBuild kills a build at `timeoutInMinutes` without exception,
   ⇒ past that point the session cannot possibly still have a build running. The API/UI explicitly returns `earliestRecoverAt` (instead of a vague "try again later").

**The key difference from the progress page (an easy mistake)**: `fetchStackStatuses` **degrades** a DescribeStacks failure to UNKNOWN and keeps displaying (the progress page must not return 500 because of a permission hiccup);
the recovery gate's `describeStackDisposition` **never degrades** — cannot see clearly = unsafe = refuse (fail-closed). The same API with two rules, hence **two functions** rather than one with a parameter.

**Deliverables**: `lifecycle.recordOperationFailure` (the single failure writer; a modern/legacy discriminated-union fence) · `deploy-control.describeStackDisposition`
+ `EXEC_ENV_BUILD_TIMEOUT_MS` (verbatim contract test against the infra `timeout: Duration.hours(2)`) · **read-only** failure classification in `reconcile.ts`
(`ReconcileOutcome.recovery`) · new route `POST .../[targetId]/recover` · UI blocking banner + recovery button (second confirmation) + deploy button disabled (no silent retry)
· IAM adds `cloudformation:ListChangeSets` (narrow: the same existing `BgpExecEnv-*` statement, zero new resource surface) · the broker's `LOCK_LEASE_MS` corrected.

**Gates**: expression-parity extended to the new writer (two modes × buildId present/absent × resources/agentcore present/absent, zero unused names —
the class that mocks cannot detect and that only blows up in a real environment, proven twice already, sections 20.3 / 18) · new `operation-failure-recovery.test.ts` (SUCCEEDED refused / IN_PROGRESS refused /
UNKNOWN refused / unexecuted change set refused / fence mismatch refused / buildId missing and window not elapsed refused / window elapsed + CFN stable admitted / phase unchanged / lock cleared cleanly / health=unknown /
legacy clears the lock only) · routes-guard gains the recover anchor + a **negative assertion "deployLockExpiresAt must not be compared with the clock"** · a 2a regression pin (SUCCEEDED must finalize).

🔴 **Headless page-level acceptance of failure recovery** (real environment: issue one deployment → an external `StopBuild` produces a real terminal `STOPPED` → the detail page shows "Manual recovery required" → click recover + confirm →
lock cleared cleanly, deploy button usable again → the next tick's probe is green and it returns to active). Contract: the specification, section 3.5b, and the failure-recovery section of the acceptance plan.

**🔁 Re-review after remediation (two read-only audit passes) — found 2 P0 + 4 P1/P2, all fixed** (the fix-then-re-audit loop confirmed for the nth time):
1. 🔴🔴 **P0 (a "permanent stuck" that I had reintroduced myself)**: for a failed upgrade `recordOperationFailure` writes `ready + health=unknown`,
   and the broker's `assertBrokerModernInvariants` rejects this shape as **corrupted data** (`bad-descriptor` → HTTP 500) ⇒ while the probe is not green
   **that target can never start a deployment again**, and the only way out is once more hand-editing DynamoDB. **And this trap had in fact been laid in 2a already** (a core probe red for 2 consecutive ticks
   produces the same shape ⇒ a region whose probe turned red cannot even be "redeployed to fix it").
   The root cause is not the new writer but the broker's **outdated invariant**: when it was written, `ready` could only be reached through the "completion fence (which requires healthy)".
   ⇒ Fix: delete that assertion (division of semantics = **not accepting business push-down ≠ not allowing operational deployment**; the former is held by `serviceabilityReason`).
2. 🔴 **P1 → in fact a P0 of the same family**: the two projection functions diverge on `ready+unknown` (platform `degraded` / broker `active`),
   so the broker's assertion "the top-level projection must agree with the two axes" would **independently** throw `bad-descriptor` once more. It was unreachable before only because item 1 blocked first.
   ⇒ Fix: the broker projection now uses the same expression as the platform, and a new **cross-boundary parity gate** was built (vitest really `import`s the broker's `validate.mjs`,
   and compares the **full 252-cell cross product** `intent × phase × health × operation? × lastError?` cell by cell). Mutation measured: reverting to the old expression ⇒ 3 of 4 tests immediately red.
3. 🔴 **P0 (found by the second audit pass)**: `describeStackDisposition` only blocked `*_IN_PROGRESS`, while `UPDATE_ROLLBACK_FAILED` /
   `ROLLBACK_FAILED` / `CREATE_FAILED` / `DELETE_FAILED` **do not end with it** ⇒ "CloudFormation explicitly says the rollback did not succeed" was judged stable and the lock released.
   ⇒ Fix: changed to an **allow-list of stable terminal states** (enumerated admission; anything outside the list is blocked) + 8 positive/negative unit tests. The meta-lesson is the same as in section 20.23: **on a security load-bearing surface a deny-list always leaks**.
4. **P1**: `readBuildStatus` flattened "CodeBuild cannot find that buildId" into `phase:"none"` ⇒ classified as "status undetermined" ⇒ yet another
   permanent-stuck door of "locked + no way out + no alarm either" (triggered as soon as the build record expires or the project is recreated). ⇒ A new narrow reader **dedicated to the recovery path**,
   `readExactBuildPhase` (a separate `not-found` = the record is gone ⇒ structurally no build can be running ⇒ recoverable); it also stops needlessly pulling per-stack status + logs.
5. **P1**: fail-open in the UI presentation layer — when `getRecoveryStatus` failed, the banner was removed and **the deploy button was unlocked**, silently failing exactly when it was needed most.
   ⇒ Changed to three states (not fetched / fetch failed / verdict); a failed fetch is always fail-closed, deployment disabled with an explicit explanation; and refreshed after deploy / supplemental deployment / probe.
6. **P2**: `blockers` leaked the raw AWS text (including the 12-digit account id + role ARN) through HTTP + audit ⇒ `sanitizeAwsErrorMessage` added (red line: account ids must be masked);
   `brokerCodeToHttp` missed the four new `needs-*` codes of section 19.7 of the review note (all fell to 502) ⇒ 409 added; the recovery note adds "components declared but not landed need an idempotent redeploy";
   `RECOVERY_NO_BUILD_MARGIN_MS` 5 min → 15 min (must be > the broker's 5 min resend window, otherwise zero margin in the worst case);
   `lastError` is cleared when the probe returns to healthy (otherwise an old failure text hangs on active forever; `lastFailure` is kept as history).
7. **P2 (a fake gate I wrote myself)**: `expectNoLockClearingWrite` was **an empty assertion + an alias leak** (a bare for loop that never asserted "must be empty";
   the regex only matched the literal `deployLockToken`, while the real write uses aliases, `REMOVE #token, #expiry, #owner`) ⇒ rewritten **with its own self-check samples**.
8. 🔴 **The gate that section 24.3 of the review note had kept asking for is finally in place**: a new `platform/scripts/execution-env-writer-gate.mjs` —
   enumerates every DynamoDB write command in the repository by AST, resolves by `TableName` the ones that hit the execution-environment table, and checks each against a `(file, named function, command kind)` allow-list;
   a dynamic table name (within that table's blast radius) is always refused; **a rotting allow-list is refused too** (an entry declared but no longer existing ⇒ red, against "a same-named function getting a free pass").
   Current state: **11 registered write sites**; `writer-topology.test.ts` proves with a temporary probe file that "sneaking in an unregistered write site is guaranteed red".
   ⇒ With this, "there are only these few write sites" is upgraded from a **comment** to a **structural guarantee**.

### 20.21 🔴 Revision 3.15 **Real-deployment construction batch + closing the remaining management-page slice** (construction batch; driven by feedback from live testing + three bugs found in real runs)

**Deployments are executed by me and approved one at a time** (a rule confirmed in person on 2026-09-01). The entire value of this batch lies in the class of **"only exposed by a real run"**:
synth / 1228 unit tests / the full synth gate — all three gates green, and not one of the seven bugs below could have been detected by them.

| # | Bug hit by the real deployment | Root cause (the sentence to internalise) | Fix + pin |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The broker hits **KMS AccessDenied** the moment it reads the registry; StartBuild is never reached | The `bgp-*` tables are encrypted with the data-domain CMK (`CUSTOMER_MANAGED`), and **DynamoDB decrypts customer-managed keys with the caller's credentials (FAS)** — the header comment in `kms-stack.ts`, "service-side transparent encryption ⇒ the caller needs no kms permission", **does not hold for DynamoDB** (platform-stack had long granted it to the TaskRole, but nobody reminded the newly written broker) | `deploy-stack` takes a `dataKey` prop + `grant(Decrypt/GenerateDataKey/DescribeKey)`; synth-gate **section 9.2b** pins the grant's presence (mutation: remove the grant ⇒ red) |
| 2 | The first build blows up on **the first line of PRE_BUILD** (`set: Illegal option -o pipefail`) | **CodeBuild runs commands with `sh` (dash) by default**, while the buildspec is all bash syntax (`set -o pipefail`, `${!v}` indirect expansion). "Which shell runs the command" is a **runtime property**: synth does not look at it, and neither does the broker validation matrix | buildspec adds `env.shell: bash`; the gate pins that line (mutation: delete it ⇒ red) |
| 4 | The AZ preflight judges the **available** `us-west-2a/b` as "not available in this account" | The list from `aws --output text` is **TAB**-separated, while `case " $AZS "` matches word boundaries with **spaces** ⇒ never hits. | **Yet another buildspec shell trap** ⇒ this time pinned at the root: synth-gate **section 11 extracts the pre_build command block verbatim and really runs it** (`aws` replaced by a stub on PATH; zero credentials, milliseconds), 4 cases (TAB-separated positive + three negatives: missing input / account mismatch / missing AZ). Mutation: remove the `tr` normalisation ⇒ the positive case is immediately red |
| 5 | The whole core stack **ROLLBACK**s: the EMR security group cannot be created | `GroupDescription` of `AWS::EC2::SecurityGroup` **accepts ASCII only** ("Character sets beyond ASCII are not supported"). 🔴 **This is the same kind as "IAM description accepts Latin-1 only"**, and back then I only changed the one spot that broke and **did no systematic sweep** — "sweep a whole family of traps at once" was said and not done | All resource text in the exec-env stack group changed to English (Chinese moved into comments); synth-gate **section 5b scans the synth output** and forbids non-ASCII in `Description` / `GroupDescription` (asserting on the output rather than on how the source is written ⇒ variables / concatenation cannot escape it). Mutation: change the SG description back to Chinese ⇒ red, naming the resource + field + original text |
| 6 | The **empty table bucket** left by the previous failure blocks every subsequent retry (409 `AlreadyExists`) | **The inherent tension between RETAIN and stack re-creation**: data resources are deliberately RETAIN (the retain-data-resources item of section 12), so once the core stack ROLLBACKs the table bucket stays where it is and the rebuild is bound to collide. Not a typo — a design consequence — but it makes "the first failure poisons every retry" the norm (this batch lost two extra rounds to it: after deleting the bucket one still has to wait for S3 Tables deletion to propagate, during which it reports transitional state…try again later) | **Decided on 2026-09-01**: a preflight in the buildspec before deploying, **clearing the empty shell only when all three hold**: ① the core stack of that target **does not exist** (nobody owns it) ② the table bucket has **0 namespaces and 0 tables** (guaranteeing by construction that there is no customer data) ③ the name is **this target's** name derived from the SSOT; and it writes a loud audit entry. If any one fails ⇒ fail fast and print the exact cleanup command, never guess |
| 7 | The platform has **no permission** in the new region: the `tableBucket` probe reports `s3tables:ListTableBuckets` AccessDenied | 🔴 **Not a code bug — I forgot to pass a parameter**: the `aws:RequestedRegion` list introduced by the IAM-region slice is written into IAM **statically at deploy time**; rolling the platform stack without `-c execution-regions=` authorises only home. Hence "registered, stacks really built, yet the platform cannot see it" | The symptomatic fix is to add the parameter; **the root fix is not to rely on anyone remembering** — `scripts/redeploy-platform.sh` (the single entry point for the platform stack) now **derives the region set from a scan of the registry** (home ∪ the regions of all registered targets), consistent with that script's existing rationale "wiring truth is read from the live environment". 🔴 The lesson generalised: **any configuration that is "a static snapshot at deploy time + grows at runtime" must be derived from the source of truth by a single entry point**, otherwise the next person (or the next me) will certainly miss it |
| 3 | The progress area shows "No build in progress" **forever** | After the wizard triggers, the first `build-status` fetch **races** the broker's write-back of `lastBuildId`; losing the race yields `phase:"none"`, and `"none"` is treated by the frontend as a **terminal state** ⇒ **polling stops** and never resumes | The wizard passes `buildId` explicitly to `BuildProgress` (the route had long supported `?buildId=`); the [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 2.1 gains that parameter |

**Delivered in the same batch (decisions taken + my own seam self-review)**

| Deliverable | Description |
|---|---|
| Section 12.0, **fix ①** (closed in this batch; originally recorded as "verify the key names in a later slice") | The broker role gains **5 Deny statements**: `codebuild:source.buildspec` / `source.location` / `serviceRole` / `environment.image` / `environment.privilegedMode`, with the condition `Null:{key:"false"}` (= refuse whenever the request carries that field). 🔴 **Key names verified against AWS authoritative data** (the CodeBuild `conditionKeys` of `awspolicygen`; at design time they were deliberately not written down from memory — the official HTML page is a JS-rendered empty shell from which a fetch cannot extract the table; the real list came only from a machine-readable source). **One independent statement per key** (multiple keys inside one condition block are AND = "refuse only if all are present", which is meaningless); `environment.environmentVariables` is **deliberately not blocked** (the legitimate channel through which the broker injects `EXEC_ENV_*`; blocking it would cut the legs off). Synth-gate section 9.2c, three assertions |
| **Stale-lock takeover** (a seam I introduced myself) | Exposed only after the "Redeploy" button was added: the build fails in 30 s, the lock is held for 90 min per the lease ⇒ **the button is guaranteed to 409 in exactly the scenario where it is needed most**. The broker verifies the lock holder's build terminal state and then takes over with a conditional write double-bound to the observed values (see the contract and the **residual-risk register** in the specification, section 3.5); the pure function goes into `validate.mjs` ⇒ the broker validation matrix grows **22 → 28 cases**; the gate gains two text anchors, "must bind (lock holder, epoch) together" and "a takeover must leave an audit entry" |
| **The home agentcore gap** (live-testing feedback: "after initialization the execution environment lacks msk/agentcore") | 🔴 **A real gap**: `BgpAgentCoreStack` had long been deployed, all three runtimes `READY`, the platform using them daily — and the seed assembly never probed it at all. Added: three real probes in probe-runner (runtime `READY` / memory `ACTIVE` / gateway `READY`; the status literals verified in a real environment) + gated assembly in seed-home (**`gatewayUrl` is fetched dynamically through `GetGateway`** — that field comes back `null` from `ListGateways` and is only present on a single lookup; measured). **MSK is not claimed, as decided** (there genuinely is no cluster in the account; the sync capability with MSK as a **data source** connects to external / customer clusters and is unrelated to this item) |
| "Trigger deployment / Redeploy" on the detail page | Previously deploy existed only in the wizard; an environment whose `register` succeeded but whose trigger failed **had no way forward on the page**, while the wizard's error text promised "you can retry from the detail page". The button's status set shares the semantics of the broker's `DEPLOYABLE_STATUS` (a UI convenience check; **the authority stays with the broker**); home and the deletion-flow states get no button |

**🎯 The end-to-end add-region acceptance flow closed in a real environment (2026-09-01, walked through on the page)**: wizard, pick a region → Start deployment → (broker → CodeBuild →
bootstrap → create the base/core stacks) → detail page "Check initialization" → "Enable" ⇒ `tgt_usw2_a` **status=active / ready=true /
probe 6/6 all green** (datalakeBucket / scriptsBucket / tableBucket / athenaWorkgroup / emrSecurityGroupId / glueRoleArn).

🔴 **Honestly recorded as not closed**: the lake-chain acceptance item (the data-lake chain really running in the second region) and the **canary** both wait for the **data-plane push-down batch**
— today jobs still cannot be pushed to a second region, and the canary endpoint is not implemented; likewise the no-fallback item (non-home never falls back) has only registry-level fail-closed unit tests,
and the real dispatch path does not exist ⇒ not claimed.

🔴 **The deployment lock is not released automatically after a successful build** (the buildspec may not write the registry; the lesson recorded in section 20.11) ⇒ cleared by lease expiry or by **stale-lock takeover**;
this batch verified that the takeover engages automatically in a real environment (epoch 1→2 and 5→6, once each), so it is not blocking, but it is recorded as "tidiness debt".

**A process hole (self-disclosed in this batch)**: the platform image is built from the **working tree** ⇒ one redeploy shipped code a parallel work stream had just written and **not yet committed**.
Although 1252 unit tests were green and the page showed zero errors, "what is deployed is not in git" violates the spirit of the red line "HEAD must be pushed before deploying".
⇒ Rule strengthened: **do not deploy the platform stack while parallel work streams are running**; to deploy, converge and commit first.

**Acceptance evidence (real environment, not reasoning)**: the deploy-lock item — a concurrent second deploy gets **409 `locked`** with the lock holder's operationId in the response;
the first half of the privilege-isolation item — the platform TaskRole gets a double **`implicitDeny`** on `codebuild:StartBuild` / `UpdateProject` (proven with `iam simulate-principal-policy`,
not by reading the policy text and judging "it looks absent").

🔴 **Three traps in the acceptance scripts themselves** (confirming once more that "what gets re-run over and over is the script, not the product"):
① `/tmp/kf-creds-new.env` **had no `export`** ⇒ after `source`, child processes could not read the password, the acceptance helper library fell back to the default password and login failed 3 times in a row (root-fixed with `set -a`);
② the UI language follows the **IP-based suggestion** (the machine is in the US ⇒ switched to `en`, the button became "Next"), so every Chinese selector came up empty ⇒ the script must force `localStorage['bgp-locale']='zh'` with `add_init_script`;
③ this antd version has no `.ant-select-selection-item` class and `.ant-modal:visible` cannot be located either ⇒ **when stuck, probe the real DOM first** (`evaluate` dumping button text / innerText) instead of guessing selectors.

### 20.20 🔴 Revision 3.14 **Home-seed construction batch: a real caller for the home seed + register no longer accepts an account** (construction batch; driven by feedback from live testing)

**Trigger**: two pieces of feedback from live testing in a real environment (2026-09-01), two decisions taken on the spot:
① "Why does Execution Environments not show the current execution environment?" ② "Initialization must not ask the user for an account; execution environments may strictly only be added in different regions of the control-centre account."

| Deliverable | Description |
|---|---|
| `lib/exec-env/seed-home.ts` | `fetchHomeFacts` (live query; throws if any of the three core truths is missing) + `buildHomeDescriptor` (pure function; every name goes through the home exemption of the naming SSOT; **the gated components dms/flink are probed one by one and claimed only when found** — claiming an uninstalled one would be refused by the component ↔ resource pairing check of the [specification](./multi-region-execution-spec.md), section 3.1a, and rightly so) |
| `POST /api/admin/exec-envs/seed-home` | An explicit bgp-admin action (**≠ the "self-healing on the read path" forbidden by the code-review round**): assemble → **probe for real first; red ⇒ 409 and nothing persisted** → the existing `seedHomeTarget` |
| `lib/exec-env/aws-context.ts` | The single source of the control plane's account / region / partition (STS, cached per process) |
| register route | Client-field allow-list (receiving `accountId` / `partition` ⇒ 400) + 🆕 **cross-partition guardrail** (an account in the aws partition cannot create an aws-cn target; say it plainly instead of letting registerTarget refuse through a more obscure cross-check) |
| Wizard / client | Step 1 drops the account input; the account segment of the naming preview shows a `<this account>` placeholder; the client-side copy of `partitionForRegion` is deleted (against a second definition) |

**Deployment status (from this batch on, the real environment keeps up with the code)**: the platform stack rolled twice + `BgpExecEnvDeployStack` deployed in a test account
(broker Active / CodeBuild project present). 🔴 **Hit by the real deployment and fixed on the spot**: an IAM Role `description` **accepts Latin-1 only**;
a Chinese description ⇒ 400 — **synth does not validate it; only a real deployment exposes it** (lesson: IAM text fields are always English; Chinese goes into code comments).

**Tests**: +10; 🔴 tripped once more over a **TDZ** (a top-level client × a `vi.mock` factory: **green by luck when run alone, guaranteed to fail in the full run**),
root-fixed with `vi.hoisted` — this kind of "order-sensitive false green" is more dangerous than a red; a warning sign has been posted in the comments of both test files.

**Honest boundary**: seed-home's `emrSubnetIds` comes from `EMR_DEFAULT_SUBNET_IDS`, injected by CDK at deploy time —
this is not the "guessing from env" that the specification, section 1.1 forbids (that rule forbids reading an env variable that was **never injected** as truth, like the old `REDSHIFT_SECRET_ID`);
it is a real name injected at deploy time, from the same source as the live EMR jobs, and it throws when empty.

---

⇒ **Next: the second-target real-deployment batch** (actually deploying a second target in a test account). It is the only batch that takes multi-region from 0% to "something exists",
and its acceptance criterion is "the second region really runs end to end" — convergent, rather than an unbounded debate about "is this validator strict enough".
